# Release notes

What changed in Socio, newest first. Each release has a version, its date, a short summary, its highlights and then its full notes. The app shows them under **Release notes** in the account menu.

                    minor one while Socio is before 1.0
    ### New         makes a minor release
    ### Changed     only these three: a patch release

## Unreleased

### New

- **The groundwork for Socio in other languages.** A new `@socio/i18n` package holds the strings and the shared i18next setup; the server serves the dictionaries from its own build at `/i18n/manifest.json` and `/i18n/<locale>/<ns>.<hash>.json` (public, cached for good, never fetched from anywhere), and pages render in the person's locale: their saved preference, then a `socio_locale` cookie, then the browser's languages. `PATCH /api/preferences` takes `locale` (a supported locale, or null to match the device). Everything is still English: Settings › Language appears once a second language is translated. See docs/I18N.md.
- **The server side of Google and Apple sign-in for the Socio app.** The app can sign people in with Google (iOS and Android) and Sign in with Apple (iOS), for accounts that linked one first; nobody gets an account this way, and an email never finds one. It works when the server is connected to Socio's cloud services, which verify the sign-in: a self-hosted server needs no Google or Apple setup, and holds no client ids. Each sign-in carries a single-use nonce from this server, so it can't be replayed on another. Admins can turn either off under Settings → Access, and unlinking one ends the sessions it made. For deployments: there are no new settings, and `AUTH_OAUTH_PROVIDERS` can no longer name a provider `google-native` or `apple-native` (the server refuses to start with either). The app's buttons come in a later app release (CB-570).

### Fixed

- Search no longer finds a folder by words that are only in its hidden body, on the web, in MCP or in a socio's page tools: a folder matches by its title only, and turning it back into a page makes its text searchable again (CB-426).
- A folder on the web no longer offers **Ask about this page**: the page chat shows only on pages, and comes back when a folder is turned into a page (CB-426).

## v0.56.0 (2026-11-26)

A Space's pages can now live in folders, and the Socio app tells you plainly when a workspace needs a newer version. Admins can also share AI reply reports with Ingenio; it stays off until they turn it on.

- Folders in Spaces (CB-426)
- Update Socio when a workspace needs a newer app (CB-553)
- AI reply reports, off until an admin turns them on (CB-382)
- A simpler Add a device dialog on the web (CB-567)

### New

- **Organize a Space's pages in folders (CB-426).** **New folder**, next to **New page** in the library and inside every folder, asks for a name and makes a folder where you are. A folder shows its name and what it holds, never a body, and opens to its contents even when empty, from **All pages** too. Any page can become a folder with **Turn into folder** in its menu and go back with **Turn into page**: its text is kept, only hidden while it is a folder. Folders keep their place through moves, exports and imports, match search by their title, and socios can make and switch them with the page tools.
- **See the cloud connection on the Platform page (CB-559).** A new **Cloud connection** card shows admins whether this server is connected to Socio's cloud services, its deployment id and mode, what it may use, and when it enrolled and last synced. If the key was revoked or the stored identity can't be opened, the card says so and what to do. See Cloud connection in docs/SETUP.md.
- **AI reply reports (CB-382).** Once admins turn on sharing, a thumbs-down that the person confirms, with a reason (offensive, harmful, wrong or other) and an optional note, goes to Ingenio's reports collector. It carries the reply and the message it answered, redacted and cut to size, and never the person's name, email or ids. Reports wait in an outbox and are sent once a minute, with retries. Removing the thumbs-down before a report goes withdraws it, and turning sharing off drops everything not yet sent. On the web, admins turn sharing on with **Share AI reply reports with Ingenio** on the Platform page, after reading what leaves, and see who turned it on, when, and how many reports wait or couldn't be sent; the Cloud connection card shows the reports that couldn't be sent. While sharing is on, each thumbs-down on a socio's reply asks first: a reason, an optional note and what goes to Ingenio, including the line when the message it answered is someone else's; Cancel gives no thumbs-down. A sent report shows **Shared with Ingenio**, and removing its thumbs-down warns that it doesn't recall the report. The mobile app's confirmation comes next; there a thumbs-down stays plain until then. It needs a cloud connection that grants `reports:write`. See AI reply reports in docs/SETUP.md and SECURITY.md.
- **Socio Mobile tells you when it needs an update (CB-553).** Each server now says which app versions it works with at `GET /api/client/compat`, and answers an app below its minimum with one clear refusal (`426 client_update_required`) instead of whatever breaks first. The app then shows Update Socio for that workspace, with TestFlight on iOS, Check again and your other workspaces; nothing on the phone is signed out or erased, and what you wrote sends once you update. A newer recommended version shows a banner you can dismiss. The web, MCP and agents are never refused for a version. Today's minimum is 0.2.7; operators can override it with `APP_MINIMUM_VERSION` and `APP_RECOMMENDED_VERSION` (docs/SETUP.md).

### Fixed

- The Add a device page on link.socio.sh now says to open the link from your camera or a message, or to update Socio (or reinstall it) and scan the code again, instead of pointing to an in-app scanner that doesn't exist yet (CB-565).
- Once a phone claims the code, the web's **Add a device** dialog says which device is asking to sign in and shows the time left to confirm. Typing or pasting the 6-digit code (spaces and dashes are fine) sends it at once, with no **Sign in** button, and the dialog no longer shows the scan instructions or "No app yet?" (CB-567).

## v0.55.0 (2026-11-26)

A Space's library now opens at its top level, so you open a page to see the pages inside it, with All pages still one click away. Deployments can also enroll with Socio's cloud services; nothing uses the connection yet.

- Browse a Space's pages by level (CB-426)
- A deployment can enroll with Socio's cloud services (CB-559)
- Closing an issue report you have edited asks first (CB-495)
- Search in a Space's library says it's sorted by relevance (CB-497)

### New

- **Browse a Space's pages by level (CB-426).** The library opens at a Space's top level instead of listing every page at once. A page with pages inside shows how many, and opening it shows what's inside, with a trail back up and **Open page** for the page itself. **New page** creates the page inside the level you're in. **All pages**, in the new **Browse pages** menu, keeps the full list with each page's path, and search still covers the whole Space and now shows each result's path.
- **A deployment can enroll with Socio's cloud services (CB-559).** With a provisioning token in `SOCIO_CLOUD_ENROLLMENT_TOKEN_FILE`, the server makes its own Ed25519 key, registers the public half with the issuer and keeps the private half sealed under the data key. It then gets short-lived access tokens with signed requests, and syncs once a day: its version, origin and a fingerprint, nothing else. A deployment that sets none of the new settings runs exactly as before and sends nothing. Nothing uses the connection yet; the push relay and reply reports come next. See Cloud connection in docs/SETUP.md.

### Fixed

- **A long socio name stays on one line in the sidebar (CB-430).** A long name next to the Personal badge wrapped out of its row into the rows below; it now ends in an ellipsis instead.
- **The Space library says "Sorted by relevance" while you search (CB-497).** Search results come in order of relevance, but the sort menu still showed "Recently edited" or "Name A–Z". During a search the menu now gives way to "Sorted by relevance", and clearing the search brings back the sort you chose.
- **Closing an issue report you have edited asks first (CB-495).** Escape, the close button, Cancel or a click outside the report threw away what you had typed and attached. If you changed anything, Socio now asks whether to discard the draft; Keep editing returns you to the field you were in. A report you haven't touched still closes right away.

## v0.54.0 (2026-11-26)

Saving a page keeps everything you didn't edit exactly as it was, and socios no longer fail a tool call over a number or a yes or no sent as text. Socio Mobile testers need the latest build.

- Saving a page keeps the parts you didn't edit exactly as they were
- Socios no longer fail a tool call over a number or a yes or no sent as text
- Socio Mobile 0.2.6 and older need 0.2.7
- Dependencies move to patched versions

### Breaking

- **Socio Mobile 0.2.6 and older need 0.2.7 (build 3806): TestFlight on iOS, Socio Preview 3806 on Android.** The server no longer keeps answering in the shapes the app had before AG-UI 1.0: attachments come only as AG-UI 1.0 media parts, a transcript message's `author` and `at` only in its `metadata`, and the `X-Socio-Reads` header is gone. An installed build of 0.2.6 or older shows no attachments, loses some run details (a message's author and time), may send a delivered message again after a lost answer, and can't send an attachment (the server refuses the old reference and says to update). Testers update to 0.2.7: from TestFlight on iOS, and Socio Preview on Android. Conversations stored before AG-UI 1.0 still read in full, their old attachment references included.

### Changed

- **A tool input with a number or a boolean sent as text runs instead of failing.** When a socio's call to a server tool fails the tool's schema only because a number came as text (`"timeoutMs": "18000"`) or a boolean as `"true"` or `"false"`, Socio reads it as the number or boolean and runs the tool, so the model no longer spends a round on the retry; the `Model tool call … repaired input` log line names each field. Nothing else is repaired: `null`, an empty or other text, a missing field, and a field whose schema also takes text or an object stay as they were, and the model gets the schema error as before. A whole number field takes plain digits only (`"1e3"` and `"1.5"` stay text). The library's own repair stays off. Each schema failure, repaired or not, is a new `tool_input` entry in the turn's trace: the tool, each issue's path, code and expected and received types, and the repair, never a text value.
- **AG-UI 1.0 and CopilotKit 1.90.** Socio moves to `@ag-ui/client` and `@ag-ui/core` 1.0.5 and `@copilotkit/*` 1.90; Telegram stays on `@copilotkit/channels-*` 0.11.1, the last release that has it. Attachments are now written as AG-UI 1.0 media parts (`image`, `audio`, `video` or `document`, by the file's type) whose `source` is `{ type: 'file', value: <attachment id>, provider: 'socio' }`, with the file name in the part's `metadata`, from the web app, Socio Mobile, the steering queue and Telegram. The old `{ type: 'binary', id, mimeType, filename }` reference is still read in stored conversations, and every client gets it as the new part. A transcript message's `author` and `at` come in its `metadata`, which AG-UI 1.0 keeps. Runs stored before the upgrade replay as before: their reasoning events (`THINKING_*`, now `REASONING_*`) and their attachments come through. Changing dependencies now goes through `pnpm deps` (docs/TOOLING.md).
- **Saving a page keeps untouched blocks byte-identical.** Editing a page in the web editor or in Socio Mobile no longer rewrites the whole document on save: every block you didn't touch keeps its exact Markdown, spacing and markers included, and only the blocks you changed are written again. A page that can't be matched block by block saves as it did before.
- **Dependencies move to patched versions.** The security pass clears 60 of the 69 open dependency alerts (Hono, the MCP SDK, nodemailer, Zod, Vite and others, plus scoped overrides). Effect moves to 4.1, Vitest to 5.1, jsdom to 31, TanStack AI to 1.8, SimpleWebAuthn to 14, web-push to 4 and `@noble/curves` and `@noble/hashes` to 2.4. Passkeys, Web Push and DPoP proofs work as before; nothing changes in how Socio works.

### Upgrading

- **Images come from `ghcr.io/erden-sh`.** From this release, Socio's images are published as `ghcr.io/erden-sh/company-brain:vX.Y.Z@sha256:<index-digest>`, and the same for `company-brain-apps`, `company-brain-coding-git`, `socio-coding` and `company-brain-supervisor`. Point every image reference at the new owner and pin the new index digests. No migration or setting changes.

## v0.53.1 (2026-11-24)

A maintenance release: the docs and examples use generic names, and nothing changes in how Socio works.

- Docs and examples use generic names

### Changed

- The docs and the examples in them use generic names only, such as an `events` or `dashboards` plugin and hosts under `example.org`. Nothing changes in how Socio works, and no setting, migration or image change is needed.

## v0.53.0 (2026-11-23)

A plugin's Update now asks an admin to approve anything the new version adds, and the old `openAnyHttps` acknowledgment no longer updates a plugin. Admins can make data API tokens for scripts, socios can upload images and PDFs into a Space and set an item's status, plugins get release notes with a What's new dialog shown once per person, and nothing acts for a deactivated person.

- A plugin's Update asks an admin to approve what the new version adds (CB-531)
- Data API tokens for admins' scripts
- Plugins' release notes, and a What's new dialog shown once (CB-527)
- Socios upload images and PDFs into a Space (CB-524)
- Nothing acts for a deactivated person (CB-528)

### Breaking

- **A plugin's Update asks before it adds anything (CB-531).** Update used to adopt whatever the catalog's new manifest added, asking only about `bridge.openAnyHttps`: a new host the service may reach was applied at the next provision with no prompt, and new permissions, secrets, datasets, write access, webhooks and resource requests weren't shown. Now **Update** opens a review listing what the new version adds, in plain words ("Can now reach api.example.com", "Asks for a new optional secret RESEND_API_KEY"), and the settings whose stored values it deletes. The server adopts an update that adds anything only with an acknowledgment of exactly that diff, bound to the installed and the new manifest's revisions; without one, or with a stale one, `POST /api/plugins/:id/update` answers 409 with the review, and an update committed meanwhile on another runtime is refused the same way. Removals and a new image or version alone need no acknowledgment. The workspace audit's `update` line lists what was acknowledged. `GET /api/plugins` gives each installed plugin's `update` review; the shapes and the diff are in `@socio/shared/plugins/plugin-update`, for Socio Mobile. The old `{ "acknowledged": ["bridge.openAnyHttps"] }` still installs, but no longer updates. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Install, enable, disable, uninstall.

### New

- **Data API tokens for admins' scripts.** A workspace admin makes a data API token in **Settings → Data API**: a name, the scopes `data:read`, `data:write` and `data:admin`, an expiry (7 days unless chosen, 30 at most) and its own budget of data operations a minute (600 unless set, up to 100,000). It is shown once and kept as a hash; admins list and revoke tokens there (`/api/data/api-tokens`). A script, such as a Notion import, sends it as a bearer token to the data API: `data:admin` creates company datasets (owned by the workspace, counted against no person's quota, in backups like every dataset), declares tables, saves field settings and places datasets in a Space its maker edits; `data:write` writes rows; `data:read` reads. It reaches only the workspace's own datasets that no plugin is bound to, never a person's, a socio's or a plugin's, and never deletes, files, exports or grants. Its writes are recorded as `token:<id>`, attributed to its maker; the workspace audit has each token's making, a summary of its use and its revoke. A token stops at once when revoked, when it expires, or when its maker stops being an active admin. See docs/SETUP.md, Data API tokens.
- Socios can change an item's status on the Improvements board when the person asks, with `set_improvement_status`: one item at a time, only as far as the person's own role allows, recorded in the item's status history with the socio and the person. Setting the status it already has changes nothing. Connected agents' `update_issue` says the same (CB-520)
- **Plugins' release notes, and a What's new dialog (CB-527).** A plugin's catalog manifest may carry `releaseNotes`: up to 20 versions, newest first, each with a day, an optional title and Markdown notes (8 KB each, 64 KB in all). The workspace reads them at install and update and keeps the last 50 versions per plugin. What's new and Release notes show a card per plugin the person may open: the versions since they last looked, then the older ones folded away. The first time someone opens the app after Socio or one of those plugins moved to a newer version, a What's new dialog sums it up; closing it is saved for them on the server, so it shows once, on every device. Notes are rendered without raw HTML or images. For Socio Mobile, `GET /api/v1/release-notes/plugins`, `GET /api/v1/whats-new` and `POST /api/v1/whats-new/seen`, with their shapes in `@socio/shared/updates/whats-new`. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Release notes.
- **Socios upload import assets into a Space (CB-524).** `space_media_upload` copies images and PDFs a socio may read (its own folder, granted folders, files attached in the chat) into a Space's media and returns their real URLs and the Markdown to put in the page. Space readers open them, and only they: removing someone from the Space stops their access at once. The person must edit the Space and approve sharing new content (capability `media.space_upload`, under their usual policy). Content is checked by its bytes (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP up to 5 MB, PDFs up to 25 MB, 20 files a call), retries reuse the same asset, and each file reports its own success or failure. Uploads no page names are cleaned up after 7 days; deleting a page never removes one another page still names, and moving a page moves links to its PDFs too. Repairing an existing import's assets is a separate follow-up.

### Fixed

- Nothing acts any more for a deactivated person (CB-528). A socio's turn for them is refused before any tool runs, whatever started it (a chat, Telegram, a scheduled task, a goal, a work cycle or an approval's continuation), and a turn already running stops at its next tool call, saying the person is deactivated. Board writes (`file_improvement`, `vote_improvement`, `comment_on_improvement`, `edit_improvement`, `set_improvement_status`) refuse a deactivated person, an admin's too. Their pending approvals can't be decided or carried out, and their socios' computer and job data tokens open nothing from the next request, even when the deactivation came from the command line.

### Upgrading

- **Plugins' release notes (CB-527).** The plugins' migration 5 (expand only) adds the `plugin_release_notes`, `plugin_release_state` and `plugin_release_seen` tables. The release before runs on it; no setting to apply.
- **Data API tokens.** The data space's migration 17 (expand only) adds the `data_api_tokens` table and `byUserId` to `data_audit`. The release before runs on it; no setting to apply.
- **Space media uploads (CB-524).** The media migration 7 (expand only) adds five nullable columns and two indexes to `media_embeds`; it is instant, and the release before runs on it. No setting changes.

## v0.52.0 (2026-11-23)

Operators become admins and each workspace gets an owner; admins now decide early access for the whole workspace, personal opt-ins are removed, and features in development stay off unless the deployment is marked internal. Plugins with their own storage are now backed up with the workspace, and an idle HTTP/2 connection no longer restarts the server.

- One admin tier, and each workspace has an owner (CB-390)
- Admins decide early access for everyone; personal opt-ins are removed (CB-503)
- Features in development are off unless the deployment is internal (CB-503)
- Plugins with their own storage are backed up with the workspace (CB-458)
- An idle connection closing no longer restarts the server (CB-516)

### Breaking

- **One admin tier: the operator role is retired (CB-390).** Socio has two roles, admin and member. Every operator is an admin from the upgrade on, with one workspace audit line each, and nobody shows as "Operator" anywhere. Admins now remove people permanently, manage every account (other admins' included), change sign-in under Settings → Access (in person, with a step-up), edit branding, and see everyone's data space use and the workspace's schedule counts. The last-admin rule is the only one left: an admin may leave or be demoted as long as another active admin remains. `pnpm run auth create-user --role operator` is refused with a message naming the change, and `bundle import --as` must name an active admin. An older app or script that sends `operator` as a role gets 400 "Unknown role".
- **No more personal opt-in for early access; feature pages move to the workspace's settings (CB-503).** Admins now decide every switchable feature for the workspace, for everyone or for some roles and people, on two pages in the Workspace group: **Optional features** (released features that stay switchable) and **Early access** (experimental ones). Account → Early access is gone, and no route takes a person's own choice any more. People who had opted into an early-access feature lose it on upgrade, unless an admin's rule covers them: check Settings → Early access and add the people or roles who should keep it. The pages show what this server's configuration decides (`FEATURE_FLAGS`, a stored server rule, the plan) read-only, keep owner, removal date and tools under Details, search long lists, and offer no server-level controls. What's new links admins to Early access.
- **Features in development are for internal deployments only (CB-503).** A `dev` flag is never listed nor on in a customer deployment, whatever `FEATURE_FLAGS`, a stored rule or a plan says; its routes answer 404 and its socio tools stay off. On a customer deployment, `dev` flags switched on through `FEATURE_FLAGS` turn off on upgrade, unless the deployment is marked internal or the flag is promoted to early access; the start log names the entries it ignores. On an internal deployment admins switch them on Settings → In development.

### New

- **The workspace owner (CB-390).** Each workspace has one owner: an admin other admins can't demote, deactivate or remove, sign out or make a password link for, and whose way to sign in they can't take away under Settings → Access. People marks the owner; the owner hands ownership to another active admin with **Make owner** (their name typed and a step-up), stays an admin, and every admin is told. The owner can't demote or deactivate themselves, or leave, until then. Only the server's shell can deactivate the owner; ownership then passes to the earliest-created active admin.
- **Run a dataset query spec (CB-451).** `POST /api/data/specs/run` checks a query spec as validate does, then runs it as one parameterized SQLite query through the person's own read access, with the data space's read-only checks, time limit and row and byte caps, and answers typed columns and rows, totals the database computed, partial periods, the window and the rows it read. Running totals carry their opening balance into the first month shown, and comparison windows with a bucket give "this year vs last year, by month". A view computed on the server keeps the same rows in the same order as the offline evaluator does; that evaluator now reads numbers stored as text and stored dates and times strictly, as the server does.
- **Field settings for dataset columns (CB-451).** Each column of a dataset table can keep a kind (such as select, currency or person), a label and its settings: `GET /api/data/datasets/:id/tables/:table/fields` gives every field to anyone who can read the dataset, and `PUT …/fields/:field` saves one for people who may write it (its owner, or an admin for workspace and plugin datasets), refusing a kind the column's type doesn't take, a stale version (409 `field_conflict`) and, until confirmed, removing options that rows still use (409 `options_in_use`). Every save and refusal is audited. The query spec validator reads the stored settings, and a plugin manifest may declare a column's `field`, applied at install and update when the column has none and never over a person's choice. With `datasets-in-spaces` on, editors get a **Fields** panel on the dataset's page. The shared view contract gains the writes, pickers and exports the native apps need.
- **Row stamps and a changes read for offline copies (CB-451, CB-469).** Every dataset row with a primary key now carries the stamp of the write that last changed it, from a per-file sequence that never goes back (history prunes don't touch it), with a generation that each restore of a backup replaces, and deleted rows leave a tombstone kept for **Remember deleted rows for** in **Settings → General → Data space** (`DATA_STAMP_TOMBSTONE_DAYS`, 30 days). `GET /api/data/datasets/:id/tables/:table/changes?since=<token>` answers the rows changed and the keys deleted since a token, or `reconcile` after a restore. Datasets get stamps on their next write. See docs/SETUP.md, Row stamps and changes.
- **The app SDK backs up a plugin's volumes, with a conformance kit (CB-458).** For a service plugin's own image: `socio.backup.serve({ volumes, schemaVersion })` answers the workspace's snapshot protocol on `/__cb/backup/*`, for the workspace's host envelope only. By default it walks each volume, copies each SQLite database through SQLite's online backup API into a standalone copy (no `-wal`, `-shm` or `-journal` file goes with it, and its journal mode is set back on restore), takes other files as they are, and skips symlinks, an `exclude` list, and `*.key`, `*.pem` and `.env` unless listed; a plugin with its own format passes `begin`, `openItem` and `end` instead. While the workspace holds writes for a snapshot, writing requests answer 503 with `Retry-After` until the hold's 10-minute lease or its own end. In restore mode (`CB_RESTORE_SNAPSHOT`) it serves only health and the restore routes, checks every pushed chunk and item against the manifest, and on commit exits with status 0 so the task starts again on the restored data; after a restore, `socio.backup.restored.held` stays true until an admin releases the hold on outward work (`CB_RESTORE_HOLD`, `onRelease`). `socio.backup.request({ reason })` asks for a snapshot. `@socio/app-sdk/backup-check` checks any plugin, in any language, against the protocol in its own CI. The workspace's backups call it (below). See packages/app-sdk/README.md, Backups.
- **Plugins with their own storage are backed up with the workspace (CB-458).** Every scheduled backup now takes a snapshot of each enabled service plugin whose manifest declares `service.backup`, one at a time, after the database and the data spaces: only the chunks that changed are fetched, each is sealed on the backup volume under the data key and checked again after the snapshot, and the off-site copy (the app's and `pnpm run backup`'s) sends them under the run and once per chunk. A plugin that hangs, fails, lies about a hash, runs short of space or goes over its size cap fails alone, with a reason that names no path; the workspace's own backup, its off-site copy and workspace upgrades go on. Such a plugin stops showing **Not backed up** once it has a good snapshot. Its page gets a **Backups** card with its status, **Back up now** (admins, audited) and the snapshots kept; Service setup and the Plugins page show a plugin whose backup is failing or out of date, and admins get a notice on the first failure, when it goes out of date (at most daily while it lasts) and when it recovers. A plugin may ask for one itself with `socio.backup.request({ reason })`: at most one an hour and four a day. One plugin's snapshot holds at most 20 GiB by default (`PLUGIN_BACKUP_MAX_GB`), which an admin raises in **Settings → General → Plugin backups**. A plugin's newest good snapshot is kept past `BACKUP_KEEP` until a newer one succeeds. Plugin snapshots need `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Volumes and backups.

### Changed

- Datasets in Spaces is now in early access: workspace admins can turn it on in Settings → Early access (CB-451).

### Fixed

- **An idle HTTP/2 connection closing no longer stops the server (CB-516).** Node's `fetch` can report "socket idle timeout" with no handler when a pooled HTTP/2 connection it no longer uses times out, and the server exited with code 1 and was replaced. That notice is now logged as one `Recovered:` line and the server keeps serving; any other error that reaches the top still logs its `Fatal:` line and exits with code 1.

### Upgrading

- **Mark internal deployments (CB-503).** Set `SOCIO_DEPLOYMENT_KIND=internal` on each deployment the team that builds Socio uses to try features in development; leave it unset on customer deployments (the default). See docs/SETUP.md, Feature flags.
- **Flags migration 2 retires personal opt-in (CB-503).** It sets `optIn` to 0 on every stored flag rule and deletes every row of `flag_choices` (each choice stays in `workspace_audit`); triggers keep them that way while the previous release still runs during a start-first deploy, so no stored choice takes effect in either release. The column and table are dropped by a later contract migration.
- **The operator role's data migration (CB-390).** Auth migration 16 adds the `auth_workspace_owner` table and records the workspace owner: the earliest-created active admin or former operator that isn't a service account (one audit line), and writes one audit line per former operator. Stored `operator` values stay as they are for now: the previous release keeps running on the database during a start-first deploy and still guards those accounts, so this release reads each as `admin` and a later release's contract migration rewrites them. Each boot adds `admin` to feature-flag rules aimed at `operator`, so they keep reaching the former operators.
- **`AUTH_FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL` and `AUTH_FIRST_ADMIN_LINK` replace `AUTH_OPERATOR_EMAIL` and `AUTH_OPERATOR_LINK`.** The old names are read for two more releases, with a startup warning; the new ones win when both are set. The first admin is made only while the workspace has no active admin, and is its owner. Rename them in each deployment's environment.
- **Field settings (CB-451).** The data space's migration 16 (expand only) adds the `data_field_settings` table; the release before runs on it.

## v0.51.0 (2026-11-22)

Spaces can now hold datasets beside their pages, with a Private group for your own, socios ask for your consent on an approval card instead of in a reply, plugin backups get the sealed store they go into, and plugin authors can test against the real data API.

- Datasets in Spaces, and a Private group, behind the `datasets-in-spaces` flag (CB-451)
- Approvals for actions always show as a card in the chat (CB-492)
- A sealed, per-plugin snapshot store on the backup volume (CB-458)
- A data API test harness for plugins (CB-476)

### New

- **Datasets in Spaces, and a Private group (CB-451).** A dataset shared with a Space is now one of the Space's items, at its root or under one of its pages, beside the pages. `GET /api/spaces/:spaceId/items` lists a Space's pages and datasets in tree order (404 for a Space you can't read), and `GET /api/data/private-items` lists your own datasets that are in no Space, socio scratch datasets included. Each item has a `version` that changes whenever any field it lists changes (title, place, owner, tables and row counts, the socio and plugin badges); compare it for equality only. Placing a dataset in a Space, or moving it within or between Spaces, takes the Space's editor role (or a workspace admin) and managing the dataset; taking it out takes only managing it. When a page is deleted, its datasets move up to its parent. In the web app, behind the `datasets-in-spaces` flag: the sidebar shows datasets in their Spaces, a **Private** group sits at the top of Spaces, a Space's **Dataset** menu (**New dataset**, **Add existing dataset**) is shown to its editors, and a dataset's page can put it **Under page**. Item kinds from a newer release show as "Not supported in this version".
- **A data API test harness for plugins (CB-476).** A service plugin's tests can run against Socio's own data API (the query rules, write guard, limits, batch `expectChanges`, `RETURNING`, read batches and the plugin budget) over a temporary database: in process with `startDataHarness`, or as a process from the app's release image with `node dist/server/data-harness/main.js`, which serves only `/api/data/*` and the data token exchange. `harnessData(origin)` in `@socio/app-sdk/check` points `fakeSocio` at it. See docs/DATA-TEST-HARNESS.md.
- **The dataset view contract (CB-451).** `packages/shared/src/dataset-views/` holds the view definition, answer and sync types that the server, the web app and the phone app share, with fallbacks for parts from a newer release, a row evaluator and formatter, and reference fixtures. Nothing serves views yet.
- **A sealed, per-plugin snapshot store on the backup volume (CB-458).** `BACKUP_DIR/plugins/<id>/` keeps each plugin's snapshots as content-addressed chunks (SHA-256, 4 MiB each for databases and anything over 64 MiB), each stored once and sealed under the data key on the local disk too, with a sealed record per snapshot. The workspace checks a plugin's manifest before fetching anything (20 GiB per snapshot by default, which an admin will be able to raise per plugin; 200,000 items; a 16 MiB manifest; no `..`, absolute or control-character paths, unknown volumes, duplicates or SQLite `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` files beside a database), refuses a chunk whose size or hash doesn't match, and checks free space first. Retention keeps run snapshots with their run, three of each other kind and uninstall snapshots for `PURGE_BACKUP_DAYS`, then sweeps chunks nothing lists; a check re-reads every chunk and runs SQLite's integrity check on each database in a worker. Nothing takes plugin snapshots yet: the scheduled run does in a later step.

### Upgrading

- **Datasets in Spaces (CB-451).** The data space's migration 15 (expand only) adds `spaceParentId`, `spacePosition` and `spaceAddedAt` to `data_datasets`, with an index; datasets already shared with a Space stay at its root, in the order they were last changed. The release before runs on it.

### Fixed

- A large linked-page import asks on an approval card ("Import up to 20 linked pages from …") and runs once you approve it, or reports that nothing was done once you deny it, instead of asking you to type your approval. Socios are told never to ask for approval in a reply; one that does is logged, and the socio's next turn is told to use the card (CB-492)

## v0.50.0 (2026-11-21)

Plugins now say whether their own storage is backed up, get their own data budget, read batches and RETURNING rows, and can open any https link after you confirm it.

- Plugins say whether their own storage is backed up (CB-458)
- A plugin's data token has its own budget (CB-473)
- Read batches, and batch assertions with RETURNING (CB-475)
- Embedded plugins can open any https link, after you confirm it

### New

- **The workspace can call a plugin as itself, on one task (CB-458).** Socio's server signs a host envelope (`x-cb-actor` with `acc: "host"`) for its own calls to a service plugin on the reserved `/__cb/` prefix, and the app SDK takes it there and nowhere else: a person's or a socio's envelope on `/__cb/` gets 401, and a host envelope opens no other route. A host call can name one running task (`x-cb-runtime-task`); the runtime ingress sends it to that task only, or answers 502, so every request of one snapshot reaches the same replica. Nothing calls it yet: the backup steps that follow do. See packages/app-sdk/README.md.
- **Plugins say whether their own storage is backed up (CB-458).** A service plugin's manifest can now mark a volume `ephemeral: true` (a cache) and declare `service.backup: { protocol: 1, schema: <n> }`, which every other volume needs. The workspace doesn't take plugin snapshots yet: every installed plugin with such a volume shows **Not backed up** on the Plugins page and its own page, and Service setup lists it under **Plugin data**. One whose installed version declares no `backup` keeps running, in red, and admins get a notice when it is flagged and a weekly reminder until an **Update** adopts a catalog entry that declares it. The catalog warns before install, and at load, about an entry with a volume and no `backup` ("this release is the last that installs it": the next minor release refuses it) and about any entry that keeps data in private storage instead of workspace datasets. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Manifest.
- **A plugin's data token has its own budget (CB-473).** Plugins no longer share socios' 120 data operations a minute: each plugin's token gets 600 (`DATA_PLUGIN_STATEMENTS_PER_MINUTE`), which an admin changes for all plugins in **Settings → General → Plugin datasets** and for one plugin on its page, under **Datasets**. Socios' and people's limit doesn't change. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Datasets.
- **Read batches (CB-474).** `POST /api/data/datasets/:id/read-batch` runs up to 32 SELECTs read from one snapshot and counted as one operation, each with the query's checks, time limit and caps.
- **Batch assertions and RETURNING (CB-475).** A batch statement may say how many rows it must change (`expectChanges`): any other count rolls the whole batch back with a `409` naming the statement. The batch now answers each statement's changes, last row id and `RETURNING` rows, which is what a D1-style driver needs.
- **Check a dataset query before it runs (CB-451).** `POST /api/data/specs/validate` checks a dataset query spec (source, filters, sorts, groups, measures, time windows, segments and totals) against the dataset's live table, through the person's own read access, and answers every problem at once with its JSON path. Nothing runs queries from a spec yet: the dashboards that follow build on it.
- **Standard Webhooks in the app SDK (CB-479).** `@socio/app-sdk/webhooks` signs and verifies Standard Webhooks v1 (`verify`, `sign`, `generateSecret`, `parseSecrets`) and wraps a fetch-style `receiver` that refuses a bad signature and answers a repeated `webhook-id` once. It has no dependencies and no Node APIs, so it runs in plugins, workers and adapters. See packages/app-sdk/README.md, "Standard Webhooks".
- **Embedded plugins can open any https link, after you confirm it.** A plugin whose manifest declares `bridge.openAnyHttps: true` may ask the workspace to open a website, a stored file or a PDF in a new tab; the workspace asks first ("Open docs.example.com in a new tab?", **Open** / **Cancel**) in its own dialog, which the plugin can't draw or press. Links the plugin lists in `bridge.open` still open without asking; only `https:` links ever open. The declaration is a sensitive scope: the install page, the plugin's own page and an update that adds it show it with a "Sensitive scope" warning, and that update asks for a review first. Plugins without it work as before. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Manifest.

### Changed

- **A query's byte cap counts UTF-8 bytes (CB-474).** `DATA_QUERY_MAX_BYTES` and a plugin dataset's query byte cap used to count characters, so text outside ASCII could return up to three times the cap. Queries, read batches and `RETURNING` rows now count the bytes the answer carries, so such a query may be truncated sooner.

### Upgrading

- **Plugin data budgets (CB-473).** The data space's migration 14 (expand only) adds `data_plugin_budgets`, where an admin's budget for one plugin is kept. A plugin that made more than 120 data operations a minute before now gets 600.

### Fixed

- **The apps proxy no longer forwards a person's request on `/__cb/…` to a service plugin (CB-458).** Only `/__cb/auth` is the proxy's on a plugin's origin; any other path under `/__cb/`, and spellings a router could decode or fold to it (`/__CB/`, `%5F%5Fcb`), now answer 404 over HTTP and WebSockets, for people and socios alike, instead of reaching the plugin.
- **A RETURNING subquery in a batch reads only the dataset's own tables (CB-475).** SQLite names a table read inside a `RETURNING` subquery without its database, which the batch's guard took for a CTE: a statement's `RETURNING` could read the engine's own tables (row history, `_writes`), at most as an error that told one bit, since the rows weren't returned. Those reads are now checked as every other read is, and refused.

## v0.49.0 (2026-11-21)

Plugin datasets now belong to the workspace, so one plugin can read what another writes, and a plugin can no longer reach another plugin's volumes.

- Plugin datasets belong to the workspace (CB-451)
- A reinstalled plugin asks before it takes its old datasets back (CB-451)
- CSV exports can't carry spreadsheet formulas (CB-451)
- A plugin can no longer mount another plugin's volumes

### Breaking

- **A reinstalled plugin no longer takes its old datasets back by itself (CB-451).** The install screen offers them, preselected; an install through the API without `bindings` gets new, empty datasets. And a dataset a plugin writes is that plugin's alone: writes to it by people, socios, apps and admins (rows, tables, indexes, batches) are refused with "Written by <plugin>". Reading it through grants and Space sharing is unchanged.

### New

- **Plugin datasets belong to the workspace (CB-451).** A service plugin's manifest now declares the datasets it needs, each with `read` or `write` access and the columns it expects. At install, an admin binds each need to a new or an existing workspace dataset; a read need may stay unbound (**Not now**). Each dataset has one writer. Columns are checked when binding, and a writer's update that would break a plugin reading its dataset is refused, naming that plugin. Uninstalling unbinds and keeps the data, shown on the Data page as "Was written by <plugin>". A plugin's data token covers every dataset bound to it with its access, so a dashboards plugin can read what an events plugin writes on its own schedule, with no person present. The plugin's page has a **Datasets** section to see and change the bindings. A manifest entry without `access` works as before: a new dataset, bound at install with no question. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Datasets.

### Upgrading

- **Plugin datasets move to the workspace, with no gap during the deploy (CB-451).** The data space's migration 13 (expand only) adds `data_plugin_bindings`. At start, each live `plugin:<id>` dataset its plugin declares is bound to it under the same name, keeping its owner and file, so a runtime of the previous release still finds it during a start-first deploy, and datasets made meanwhile keep the plugin's owner too. Once no previous-release runtime runs (the `workspace-datasets` runtime contract), they move to the reserved owner `ws:workspace`, keeping their ids, grants, Space shares, retention, history and audit; their files move from the plugin's directory to the workspace's under `USER_DATA_DIR`. The hourly run repeats the step. Datasets of plugins no longer installed become unbound workspace datasets. Take the usual pre-upgrade backup.

### Fixed

- **A plugin can no longer mount another plugin's volumes.** The Docker socket proxy checked a plugin's volume names by prefix, so plugin `foo` could mount `socio-plugin-foo-bar-data`, plugin `foo-bar`'s `data` volume. It now takes only an exact `<prefix>-<id>-<volume>` name that no other service mounts and that no installed plugin whose id extends this one's (`foo-bar` for `foo`) has in its own namespace. It checks and applies service creates, updates and volume deletes one at a time, and it deletes a volume only when no service mounts it. A plugin network is deleted only when its labels, if it has any, name that plugin. Volume names do not change.
- **CSV exports can't carry spreadsheet formulas (CB-451).** A text cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return is now written with a leading `'`, so a spreadsheet shows it as text instead of running it. Numbers stay numbers. BLOBs are base64 and get the same apostrophe when their base64 starts with `+`; strip it before decoding. Every CSV export goes through one helper, `csvField` in `packages/shared/src/text/csv.ts`; today that is the Data page's table export.

## v0.48.0 (2026-11-19)

Board items can now say which plugins and apps they are about, and a plugin's own pages get addresses you can reload and share.

- Tag issues with the plugins and apps they're about (CB-442)
- Admins see every app's name and owner (CB-442)
- Plugin pages you can link to, and plugin downloads (CB-446)

### New

- **Tag issues with the plugins and apps they're about (CB-442).** An item on the Improvements board can name the plugins and apps it is about, or **All plugins** or **All apps**, under **Components**: chosen when reporting (a plugin's page, its settings and an app's **Report issue** start with it chosen), changed afterwards by admins only, shown as chips on cards and filtered with the board's **Component** filter (an exact match). Everyone sees only the plugins they may use and the apps they may open; a tag they can't see is left out everywhere, and an admin's edit keeps the tags it can't see. A tag keeps the name it had, so an uninstalled plugin or a deleted app still reads, and a new app at a deleted app's address is a different app. Automatic triage suggests components too: admins see every suggestion, others only those they may see, and its comment never names them. Connected agents get `components` on `file_issue`, `update_issue`, `read_issue` and `list_issues` (`component` filter), and `list_issue_components`; socios' `file_improvement` sets the plugin or app the conversation works in when the person can see it. API: `GET /api/issues/components`, `components` on `POST` and `PATCH /api/issues…`, `GET /api/issues?component=`, and a `components` label map on the list and the detail. Help: [The Improvements board](/docs/improvements).
- **Admins see every app's name and owner (CB-442).** The Apps page lists, for admins, the apps they can't open under **Other apps in the workspace**, by name and owner only (`others` on `GET /api/apps`). Opening an app still takes being its owner or reading the Space it is shared with.
- **Plugin deep links (CB-446).** An embedded plugin whose manifest sets `bridge.routes` gets workspace addresses for its own pages, `/plugins/<id>/<path>?<query>#<fragment>`, mirroring the app's route exactly, so a page survives a reload and can be shared. `@socio/plugin-ui`'s bridge reports the route by itself and follows the workspace when a link or Back moves it; the frame never reloads. A plugin's settings page moved to `/settings/plugins/<id>`, and a tab plugin opens from `/plugins/<id>`; the old `/manage` and `/open` addresses still lead there. The sidebar keeps the plugin highlighted on its deep links. Embedded plugins may also start downloads (the frame's sandbox adds `allow-downloads`), such as the Events plugin's CSV export.

### Upgrading

- One expand-only migration (`issues` 16): a new `issue_components` table, and a `components` column on `issue_triage`. The release before runs on it.

## v0.47.0 (2026-11-19)

A plugin can now reach a host an admin types into its settings, such as the Cloudflare Worker behind the Forms plugin.

- Plugin egress from a URL setting (CB-441)

### New

- A plugin manifest's `egress` may list `{ "fromConfig": "<FIELD>" }`, where FIELD is a declared `url` setting: at every provision Socio adds that setting's https host (its installed value, else its default) to the plugin's signed egress allow list, adds nothing for an empty or unusable value, and shows the allowed hosts, and why a setting added none, under **Outbound network** on the plugin's page; a settings change provisions the service again so the host follows it (CB-441)

## v0.46.4 (2026-11-18)

A room's history now says whom each person's message mentions, so the phone can show the same "@you" badge as the web.

- A room's history marks the messages that mention you (CB-416)

### Changed

- A room's history, on a cold load and on pages before or after a cursor, gives each person's message that mentions people its `mentions` (each one's name and whether it is you), as the room's transcript gives the web, so the phone shows "@you" without reading the transcript; a message that mentions nobody, and any message outside a room, has none (CB-416)

## v0.46.3 (2026-11-18)

A chat deleted in one tab no longer flashes an error in another tab that had it open, and the phone says why a failed turn wasn't answered from the chat's history alone.

- A tab showing a chat that another tab deletes goes straight to "Conversation not found." without an error toast
- A chat's history names each failed run's reason, so the phone needs no room transcript to word it

### Changed

- A chat's or room's history, cold pages and pulls after a cursor alike, says why each failed run failed (`failure`, the code the web reads from the transcript), so the phone words "Sebas's message wasn't answered: …" without reading the room's whole transcript (CB-406)

### Fixed

- A tab that had a chat open when another tab deleted it no longer asks for that chat's screen capture once more and shows a "Conversation not found." error toast on top of the page: its capture poll waits from the moment the other tab's note arrives, and a capture that comes back 404 says nothing

## v0.46.2 (2026-11-18)

A plugin page shows its name once: the second bar is gone and Open in a new tab moves into the top bar.

- Plugin pages drop their second header bar; Open in a new tab sits in the top bar, or its … menu on phones

### Changed

- A plugin page that runs in Socio's frame no longer draws a second bar under the header with its name: the header alone shows the name, Open in a new tab is an icon button beside it (in the ⋯ menu on phones), and the plugin gets that room

## v0.46.1 (2026-11-18)

Service plugins follow their image when the operator moves it to another registry.

- An installed plugin follows the catalog to its image's new registry when the digest is the same, without an admin's Update

### Fixed

- An installed service plugin follows the catalog when its image moves to another registry at the same digest, so a runtime that allows or pins only the new location no longer refuses every provision. A plugin whose service has no revision label also gets a new or rotated `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_AUTH` login, once per login, and a failed check against an install now logs the reason (the runtime's refusal, say)

## v0.46.0 (2026-11-17)

Service plugins keep working after an upgrade, and plugin images can come from a private registry.

- Socio joins the apps ingress, the app and the egress proxy to every plugin's network again within a minute of an upgrade dropping them
- Plugin images pull from a private registry with a login an admin saves as the `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_AUTH` secret

### New

- **Plugin images from a private registry.** An admin saves the registry login as the workspace secret `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_AUTH` on **Settings → Secrets** (JSON: `{"registry": "ghcr.io", "username": "…", "password": "…"}`; for GHCR a classic token with only `read:packages`), over the deployment's `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_AUTH(_FILE)`. Socio sends it with every plugin service create and update, only for images on that registry, so Swarm keeps it with the service and every task start pulls with it, on any node. The service carries the login's fingerprint as `socio.registry` (keyed, never the password), and the plugins check provisions again any service whose fingerprint isn't the login in force, so a new or rotated login reaches every plugin within ten minutes. Socio's first read of each version is in the workspace audit as `system:plugins`. A login that isn't that JSON is logged once (never quoted) and plugins pull without it.

### Fixed

- Service plugins no longer go unreachable after a stack update. An upgrade rewrites the apps ingress', the app's and the egress proxy's networks from the stack file, which doesn't know the plugins, so the ingress answered 502 and plugins couldn't reach Socio or the egress proxy until their next provision. Socio now joins those services to every enabled service plugin's network again at start and every minute (`POST /v1/services/:id/networks` on the apps runtime); a service already attached isn't touched. The full plugins check still runs every ten minutes.

### Upgrading

- Optional: set `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_AUTH_FILE` on `app` (the IaC side's render, from the provider) for a deployment whose plugin images are private, or let an admin save the `PLUGIN_REGISTRY_AUTH` secret. The `app` and `apps` images must be the same release: an older apps runtime keeps no `socio.registry` label, so a newer app would provision its plugins on every check.

## v0.45.0 (2026-11-17)

Each installed plugin gets its own admin page, with typed settings that save as you go, undo and redo, and Uninstall in a separate danger zone. Admins can also set how long analytical tables keep their data.

- Each installed plugin has its own admin page, and Uninstall sits in a danger zone that asks for the plugin's name (CB-393)
- Plugin settings use switches, number fields and choices, and are checked as you type (CB-395)
- Plugin settings save as you change them, with undo and redo, and the plugin restarts once when you're done (CB-398)
- Admins set how long each analytical table keeps data, with a preview of what a change would drop (CB-387)

### New

- **Admins control analytical tables' retention (CB-387).** On the Data page an admin overrides a table's retention (days, or **Keep everything**), and the override wins over plugin updates that declare another value until the admin clears it. Before saving a value that drops partitions, the page previews them: how many, their dates, rows and bytes. Each table shows its retention and where it comes from: an admin's override, the plugin, or the workspace default. That default, `DATA_ANALYTICS_RETAIN_DAYS` (0 keeps everything, the default), applies to tables whose plugin sets none, and admins change it in **Settings → General → Analytical tables**. The nightly run uses the effective value. Overrides and default changes are recorded in the data audit (`table_retention_override`, `analytics_retention_default`). New routes: `PUT` and `DELETE /api/data/datasets/:id/tables/:table/retention`, and `GET …/retention/preview?days=N`.
- Plugin settings can be typed: a manifest's `config` field may set `type` (`string`, `boolean`, `number`, `integer`, `enum`, `cron`, `url`), `options`, `required`, `min`, `max` and `pattern`. The plugin's page shows a switch, a number field or a list of choices for them and checks each value as you type, and the server refuses a save that doesn't fit. Values are still stored as strings, and a field without a type is free text as before (ING CB-395)
- A plugin's settings save as you change them, with **Saving…**, **Saved** and the reason when a save is refused, and only values that pass their check are sent. **Undo** and **Redo** (Ctrl+Z or ⌘Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z, ⌘⇧Z or Ctrl+Y) step through the saves made on the page (ING CB-398)
- Saving a running service plugin's settings no longer restarts it on every pause in typing: each save is stored at once, and the plugin restarts once, about 10 seconds after the last save, or at once with **Apply now** or when you leave its page. The page says when the change will apply and when it has. A Socio that stops before then applies the stored settings when it starts (ING CB-398)

### Changed

- Each installed plugin has its own page, `/plugins/<id>/manage`, and **Plugins → Installed** is an overview that links to them. The page lays out the plugin's status, who can use it and its settings as Settings cards, and puts **Uninstall** apart in a danger zone that asks for the plugin's name. The replica count is no longer an admin control: the **Scale** control and `POST /api/plugins/<id>/scale` are gone, and the operator sets `service.replicas` in the manifest (ING CB-393, CB-394, CB-396, CB-397)

## v0.44.0 (2026-11-17)

Workspace bundles now import into a running server, with no downtime, and renumber around issues already filed on the target. The Schedules list becomes a searchable, sortable grid like People, and cancelling a schedule asks you to type its name first.

- Schedules is a grid like People, and each row's actions target that row; cancel confirms by name (CB-392)
- Workspace bundles import into a running server from the command line, with no stop
- Bundle imports renumber an issue whose number is already taken on the target

### New

- **Workspace bundles import from the command line without stopping the server.** `bundle-cli.js import <dir> --as <operator id> --live [--apply]`, run in the target's app container, hands the import to the running server over a Unix socket private to the app's user (no network port) and prints the same report. The server checks that `--as` is an operator and runs the web import's own code: the same backup check, one import at a time, the same log lines, with `import_runs.via` set to `cli`.
- **Workspace bundles renumber around issues filed on the target.** A new source issue whose number a live issue filed on the target holds is now imported under the target's next free number. The dry run lists it as `renumber CB-388 → CB-393 (CB-388 here is another issue)`. Every mention and link of it in imported text uses the new number, the issue notes its source number, and later imports update the same issue. `--keep-numbers-strict` (`"keepNumbersStrict": true`) keeps the old conflict.

### Changed

- **The Schedules list is a data grid, like People in Settings (CB-392).** Both now use one shared grid: search by title, socio or job, filter by status and socio, and sort by title, status, next run or last run, with a Goal column and a card per schedule on a phone. Each row's run, pause, resume and cancel sit in its own ⋯ menu named for that schedule, and cancel, which can't be undone, asks you to type the schedule's title first, on the list and on the schedule's page.
- `bundle-cli.js` takes BACKUP_DIR as the server does: unset, it is `data/backups` (it was `backups` next to the database), so the offline import checks the same backup status the server writes.

### Upgrading

- The `bundle` schema's migration 2 adds `import_runs.via` (expand only).

## v0.43.0 (2026-11-17)

Telegram topics answer queued messages once, under each sender's name. Signed-out phones stop getting notifications sooner, and the audit says why each device was signed out. A job approved during a storage outage no longer disappears; its socio can inspect and retry it. Removal backups expire after 30 days.

- Messages that waited behind a running turn in a Telegram chat are answered once, not again by an empty extra turn (CB-303)
- Socios can inspect a job's saved state and retry a failed job; a retry stores only the code the user approved (CB-336)
- Phones whose app session ran out stop getting push notifications within the hour (CB-379)
- Each device sign-out in the audit says why: a password reset, an admin, deactivation, signing out everywhere (CB-379)
- Admins set how long the Socio app keeps a phone's copy without contact, the app lock and notification text (CB-379)

### New

- **Inspect and retry jobs (CB-336).** A job approved while file storage was down used to vanish: the approval failed as it wrote the code, nothing was recorded, and every job tool answered "Job not found." Now the approved version is recorded anyway, its code shows as missing under Schedules & jobs (with **Restore code and run**), and runs say why they can't start. Socios get `job_inspect` (the job's version, whether its code is stored and matches what was approved, its last run and error, and a save that was approved but never recorded) and `job_retry` (stores the approved code again when it is missing or changed, saves an approved job whose save failed, then runs it). A retry only ever writes the code the user approved, checked against the version's digest; new code still goes through `job_save` and approval. A refusal (the data space full, a rate limit) still records nothing.
- A service plugin can declare analytical tables in its manifest, with their kind, date partitioning and how many days to keep: install, enable and **Update to <version>** make them or add new columns, and refuse a change of kind, partitioning or a column's type. Partitions past a table's retention are retired at the nightly run and their files deleted two days later; the Data page shows the retention (CB-387)
- Analytical queries take DuckDB's `query_table('t')` and `query('SELECT …')` with constant strings, reading the tables they name; the SQL inside `query()` is held to one read-only SELECT (CB-387)
- **Admins set the Socio app's policy (CB-379).** Settings → General → **The Socio app on phones** sets what the workspace manifest (`/.well-known/socio/workspace`) tells the app: the days without reaching the workspace before a phone's copy locks (1 to 365) and the days after that before it is erased (7 to 365), whether the app lock is required, and whether notifications may show message text. Each is saved over a new variable, `AUTH_APP_LEASE_DAYS`, `AUTH_APP_WIPE_DAYS`, `AUTH_APP_LOCK` and `AUTH_APP_NOTIFICATION_TEXT`, whose defaults are the app's own (30, 30, off, on), so nothing changes until someone sets one. `PATCH /api/settings` takes `appPolicy` (the ones left out stay as they are), and `GET /api/state` gives the policy in force. The app reads it when it signs in; today's app follows the lock and erase periods, and the app lock and notification text once a version supports them.

### Changed

- **Device sign-outs in the workspace audit say why (CB-379).** Each row now reads `<device>; <signed_out|revoked|revoked_and_erase>; <why>`: from the person's Devices list, by an admin from the person page, an admin signing out all their sessions or ending the device's last session, deactivation, leaving the workspace, signing out on the device, signing out everywhere or of the other sessions, a password change or reset, or the device's last session ending another way. A person's Activity tab shows it.
- **`GET /api/auth/session` gives the Socio app its device codes (CB-379).** For a request with the app's install id (`X-Socio-Install`) whose device is refused, it answers 401 with `code` (`session_expired`, `session_ended`, `device_revoked` or `deactivated`) and `error`, as the session check does, with the sign-in view (`provider`, `branding`, `user: null`) beside them. The web's answer, a signed-in answer, and one for an install id the server doesn't know are unchanged (200). `GET /api/auth/devices` answers the same codes as before, so the app can confirm a code on either.
- README, LICENSE, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES and the socio.sh footer now state that Socio, Socio.sh, Erden, and Erden.sh are trademarks of Ingenio Ventures Inc.; the license terms are unchanged

### Fixed

- In a Telegram chat, two or more messages sent while a socio was answering got a second, empty turn after the one that took them all, and the socio answered again with nothing new; that turn no longer runs. Tests now cover two linked people in one forum topic, and in a room's topic, where a message queued mid-turn is stored with its own sender, not the turn's starter (CB-303)
- **Expired device sessions lose their push subscriptions (CB-379).** Every hour each server removes the push subscription of any app device left with no live session, so a phone whose 30-day session ran out gets no more notifications without waiting for that person's next sign-out.
- **One person's device sign-outs and push changes take turns across servers (CB-379).** They already ran one at a time within a server; during a start-first upgrade, when two servers share the database, each now also holds a lease row in the auth database for its turn (renewed while it works, and running out after 15 seconds if its server stops). A turn that can't get the lease within a minute goes ahead and reports it.
- **A sign-in that finishes while its person is being deactivated ends at once (CB-379).** Once a new session is saved, the ban is read again; a session made after deactivation's last pass is deleted, and its device erases its copy, audited as `system`.
- **A sign-out on the device stays best effort for the audit (CB-379).** Better Auth ends the session first; a failed audit is reported and never keeps the device signed in. Tests now hold this.
- **Permanent removals' backups now expire (CB-391).** Each permanent removal (CB-360) copies what it deletes into `BACKUP_DIR/purge-<time>/`, and nothing ever removed those folders, so deleted data stayed in the backups for good. Now the first scheduled backup after 30 days removes them (`PURGE_BACKUP_DAYS`, 1 to 365, or an admin's choice in **Settings → General → Backup schedule**). Younger ones, one whose cleanup still waits for `purge --resume` (or whose `cleanup.json` can't be read, which is logged), and every other backup are untouched; `pnpm run backup` by hand leaves them for the next scheduled run. They were never sent off-site, so there is nothing to remove there.
- **A workspace bundle import no longer stops at a number held by a deleted issue (CB-380).** When the only issue on the target under a bundle issue's number was filed there and then deleted, the import used to report a conflict and skip the bundle's issue, and there was no way to free the number. Now the dry run lists it as `replace deleted CB-12 here (deleted <date>)` and counts the bundle's issue as a create; applying removes the deleted issue for good, with its comments, history, attachments, votes, links, triage and other rows, as the import starts, before it writes anything else. A live issue, or a deleted one an import brought, is still a conflict.

### Upgrading

- The auth database gets migration 15 (`device turns across processes`, expand only): `auth_device_turn`, one lease row per person whose device turn is under way. The previous release ignores it and takes no lease, so turns are ordered across servers from the first upgrade where both run this release.
- New optional settings: `AUTH_APP_LEASE_DAYS`, `AUTH_APP_WIPE_DAYS`, `AUTH_APP_LOCK` and `AUTH_APP_NOTIFICATION_TEXT` (docs/SETUP.md). The server refuses to start with a value that isn't one.
- Optional: `PURGE_BACKUP_DAYS` (default 30) sets how long permanent removals' backups stay, until an admin changes it in Settings (CB-391).

## v0.42.0 (2026-11-17)

People can now leave a workspace on their own, and Socio is now published by Ingenio Ventures Inc. under a Sustainable Use License instead of MIT.

- Leave workspace, under Settings → Account → Profile, ends your membership and signs you out everywhere (CB-383)
- Socio's license is now the Sustainable Use License, modeled on n8n's, with Ingenio Ventures Inc. as licensor (CB-385)
- Removing a person for good keeps its backup copy in the same folder as the other backups (CB-360)

### New

- **Members can leave a workspace on their own (CB-383).** Settings → Account → Profile → **Leave workspace** ends the person's membership: they can't sign in again unless an admin reactivates them, and every session and device of theirs ends, including the Socio app, which erases its copy. What they made stays with the workspace, where admins can transfer it under Settings → People. The last operator and the last admin can't leave (operators don't count as admins here, since on a hosted workspace the operator is the host). The app's API is `GET`/`POST /api/account/leave`, described in `@socio/shared/leave-workspace`.

### Changed

- **New license and publisher (CB-385).** Socio's own code moves from the MIT license to the Sustainable Use License, modeled on n8n's: anyone may run and modify Socio for their own internal business, non-commercial or personal use, but not sell it or offer it to others as a commercial product or service. Ingenio Ventures Inc. replaces cbnsndwch LLC as the copyright holder and publisher in `LICENSE`, `README.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`, every package's `license` field, the `author` field where a package has one and the images' OCI labels (`org.opencontainers.image.vendor` and `org.opencontainers.image.licenses`). Code that came from OpenDots stays under its MIT license, whose notice is kept in `LICENSE`, and the other third-party components keep theirs.

### Fixed

- **A person's permanent removal keeps its backup with the others.** Removing a person for good (CB-360) copies the database first. Without `BACKUP_DIR`, that copy went to a `backups` folder beside the database, while the backups and the bundle import use `data/backups`; a `BACKUP_DIR` with spaces around it was also taken as written. It now reads `BACKUP_DIR` the way the backups do, so every copy lands in the same folder.

## v0.41.3 (2026-11-16)

Workspace bundle import no longer turns `#n` in issue text into links to the source board.

- `#524` and other `#n` shorthand survive a bundle import as written (CB-380)

### Fixed

- **Bundle import leaves `#n` alone (CB-380).** Importing a workspace bundle rewrote `#n` in issue bodies, resolutions and comments into `[#n (<source>)](…/issues/CB-n)` when issue n wasn't copied. In real data those are mostly GitHub pull request numbers, so the rewrite corrupted them. `#n` is now kept as written; `CB-n` mentions and links are still rewritten as before.

## v0.41.2 (2026-11-16)

This release carries everything in v0.41.0 and v0.41.1, and starts on every site that sends its backups off-site, which v0.41.1 did not. Deployments go from v0.40.0 straight to this one.

- Socio starts again where backups are sent off-site (CB-380)

### Fixed

- **Socio starts where backups are sent off-site (CB-380).** v0.41.0 and v0.41.1 stopped at startup with "BACKUP_S3_ENDPOINT needs DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY" on any site with `BACKUP_S3_ENDPOINT` set: the workspace bundle import read its backup folder through checks meant for the off-site copy. It now reads only `BACKUP_DIR` (default `data/backups`), the folder the backups write to, so an import's "backup in the last 15 minutes" check reads the backup that just ran, off-site copy or not.

### Upgrading

- Skip v0.41.0 and v0.41.1: v0.41.0's images were never all published, and v0.41.1 doesn't start on sites with off-site backups. Upgrade from v0.40.0 straight to this release, and follow v0.41.0's Upgrading notes, which all apply here. A site that already ran v0.41.1's migrations before it rolled back needs nothing more: they are expand-only, and this release finds them done.

## v0.41.1 (2026-11-16)

This release carries everything in v0.41.0, whose images could not all be built, so deployments go from v0.40.0 straight to this one.

- Every change of v0.41.0, with all of its images published

### Fixed

- **The coding image builds again.** v0.41.0's coding sandbox image (`socio-coding`) failed to build twice: three build stages shared corepack's cache at once, and one stage's permission pass walked into another's temporary files as they were moved away. The stages now take turns with that cache. Nothing else changes.

### Upgrading

- Skip v0.41.0: its tag exists, but its coding sandbox image was never published, so it was never deployed. Upgrade from v0.40.0 straight to this release, and follow v0.41.0's Upgrading notes, which all apply here.

## v0.41.0 (2026-11-16)

Dataset tables can keep less row history, service plugins can keep append-only history in analytical tables queried with DuckDB, and backups no longer grow in memory with the data.

- Dataset tables can keep no row history, history can be kept for a number of days, and a dataset's file can be compacted (CB-369)
- Service plugins can keep append-only history in analytical tables, stored as Parquet and queried with DuckDB alongside their SQLite tables (CB-369)
- Backups keep their memory flat however large the data grows, so they no longer risk being killed for memory (CB-378)
- Operators can copy chosen issues, Spaces and pages from one Socio to another with workspace bundles (CB-380)

### New

- **Workspace bundles (CB-380).** Copy a chosen set of issues, Spaces and pages from one Socio to another. `bundle-cli.js export` reads a snapshot of the source, never its live database. It takes explicit lists of issue numbers, Space ids and page ids, and it requires a deny-list of Spaces it must never read: if the selection reaches one of them, it refuses. On the target, an operator imports in the running server (`POST /api/operator/bundles/import`). The import is a dry run by default, and its report lists counts, authors, rewritten links, conflicts and sensitive details to review. Applying needs a backup from the last 15 minutes, and copies the database first. Ids, issue numbers and timestamps are kept. People are matched by email, and anyone else gets an "Originally by" byline. Mentions of issues that weren't copied link back to the source, and code is left as it is. Page images are sealed again with the target's key. Nobody is notified and no model is called. Running the import again only adds or updates what changed at the source, and edits made on the target always win. See Operator help, Workspace bundles.
- **Row history you can bound (CB-369).** A table made with `"history": false` (the data API, or a plugin's manifest, which may now declare its tables) keeps nothing its writes replace, for marts rebuilt from other tables; its owner, an admin for a plugin's dataset, or the plugin itself switches an existing table on or off. A dataset's **Row history** setting on the Data page keeps history for 7 to 365 days (or `historyDays` through the API); the default keeps everything, as before. Each night, between 02:00 and 06:00 server time, the leading runtime deletes what is past that, and what tables without history still keep, audited as `history_prune`. **Compact file** gives the freed space back (SQLite `VACUUM`) at the next nightly run. See SETUP.md, Row history.
- **Analytical tables for service plugins (CB-369).** A plugin's own dataset can hold append-only tables stored as compressed Parquet beside it and queried with DuckDB in a separate process: declare one partitioned by a date column, append up to 200,000 rows per call (NDJSON, idempotent with `x-cb-append-id`), copy an existing table across with `append-from`, and join both kinds of table in one read-only `POST …/analytics/query`. Their bytes have their own budget per owner (1 GB), apart from a person's data. They are in every backup, copied once and hard-linked into each run, and sent off-site once, sealed. Admins see each table's partitions on the Data page and query them there, and tune the limits in **Settings → General → Analytical tables**. `DATA_ANALYTICS_MODE=off` turns them off. See SETUP.md, Analytical tables.
- **Cancel on the new device ends an Add a device claim (CB-352).** `POST /api/auth/device/pair/cancel { claimId }`, with the claim id only the claiming device holds, ends the claim and its QR code at once; the web page shows it refused. It always answers the same, so it tells nothing about other claims.
- **Sign-in links and codes by email (CB-352).** Where email is set up (`SMTP_URL`), **Sign-in links by email** in Settings → Access can now be turned on. Then "Send me a sign-in link" on the web, the admin's **Send sign-in link by email**, and the Socio app's sign-in codes all go to the account's own address, ahead of Telegram. The message holds the link or code and nothing else about the person. A link opened from email signs in as `link:email`; turning email links off ends those sessions and stops emailed links that haven't been used. People whose only way in was email are listed before the change is saved.
- **The workspace manifest for the Socio app (CB-352).** `GET /.well-known/socio/workspace` is public, reads no cookie, and is cached a minute. It says which workspace this is (a random id that never changes), its name and logo, the server version and protocol, and the ways in the app can offer now: password, passkey, the sign-in sheet, sign-in codes (by email, by Telegram while the bot runs, or none) and Add a device. It also gives the app's lease and notification policy, for now the app's defaults: 30 days to the lock, 30 more to the erase, no app lock, message text in notifications.
- **The app learns why it was signed out (CB-352).** For a request carrying the app's install id, the session check's 401 now says `session_expired`, `session_ended`, `device_revoked` (sign out and erase) or `deactivated`. The web's 401 (`sign_in`) is unchanged.
- **Your devices, and signing one out (CB-352).** The Socio app's devices are listed for the person (`GET /api/auth/devices`, the one asking marked) and for admins beside their sessions (`GET /api/accounts/:id/devices`). **Sign out this device** and **Sign out and erase** (`DELETE …/devices/:id`, `?erase=1`) end its sessions; the device then says it was signed out, or erases its copy. Sign out everywhere, signing out on the device, a password change that signs the other sessions out, a password reset, an admin's sign-out of a person's sessions, and deactivation (which erases) end the person's devices too. Each is in the workspace audit: before anything ends for a sign-out from the Devices list, an admin's, or deactivation; right after Better Auth ends the device's last session for the rest (a failed audit there never keeps a device signed in), and the device's push subscription is removed with it, so a signed-out phone gets no more notifications. The app says which push subscription is its own (`PUT /api/auth/device/push`).
- **Invites that open the Socio app (CB-352).** With **Invites open the Socio app** on (Settings → General, over `AUTH_APP_INVITE_LINKS`, off by default), an invite or password link is a link-domain link, `https://link.socio.sh/app/i/<workspace>#t=…`, with the token only in the fragment. The link page shows the workspace's address and opens the app, or this workspace's password page. A production invite never carries a port: a workspace off https or its default port keeps the web's own link, and `AUTH_APP_PREVIEW_LINKS` makes them preview-build links instead. Signed in on the web, a person can also have the workspace's app link sent to them (`POST /api/auth/device/send-link`), by email or Telegram, as limited as sign-in links. It fills in the workspace and signs nobody in.
