- **Add a device (CB-352).** Where the Socio app can reach the workspace, the account menu offers **Add a device**: a QR code for the app, blurred until **Show**, with its countdown and **New code**. When a phone scans it, the code goes and the page asks **Sign in on a new device?** with the device's type and time: type the 6 digits the phone shows (three wrong tries end it), or choose **Not my device**. Leaving the page ends the code. **Send me a link for the app** sends the workspace's app link to your own email or Telegram.
- **Your devices with the Socio app (CB-352).** Settings → Account lists your phones with the Socio app (platform, how it signed in, last seen) with **Sign out this device** and **Sign out and erase**, each after a confirm that says what it does; erasing removes the workspace's copy from the device, unsent work included, the next time it connects. Admins see a person's devices, with the same actions, on the person's Sign-in & devices tab.
- **Get the Socio app (CB-352).** Where the workspace sends links for the Socio app (Invites open the Socio app), the account menu has **Get the app** for everyone: the workspace's QR code to show a colleague. A phone's camera opens the app with the workspace filled in, and they sign in as themselves; the code carries nothing that signs anyone in. On a phone's browser, a dismissible banner offers the app. `GET /api/auth/device/app-link` gives the signed-in web that link.
- **Signing in to the Socio app from its sign-in sheet (CB-352).** The app's sheet lands on the web's **Sign in to the Socio app on iPhone?** page (`/device/continue`). Signed out, it shows the workspace's sign-in first, without "Create an account", and a provider's sign-in comes back to it. Signed in, it names the account and does nothing until **Continue**, which approves the app's request from this browser and hands the app its one-time code. **Cancel** approves nothing.

### Changed

- **Development builds of the Socio app get their links on their own host (CB-352).** The link domain serves development links only on `link-staging.socio.sh`. Sign-in sheet callbacks, Add a device QR codes and sign-in code links for a development build now use `AUTH_APP_LINK_HOST_DEV` (default `link-staging.socio.sh`); production and preview keep `AUTH_APP_LINK_HOST` (default `link.socio.sh`). One server serves both kinds of build. The server refuses to start if the development host is the production one. `AUTH_APP_DEV_LINKS=true` (off by default, refused with `NODE_ENV=production`) lets Add a device make QR codes for a development build, so the app can be tested against a local server; `POST /api/auth/device/pair` takes an optional `variant`.
- **A QR code encoder for the web (CB-352).** The web now ships `uqr` 0.1.3 (MIT, no dependencies), declared in `packages/client` and at the root as the client README asks, for the coming Add a device and "Get the app" QR codes. Nothing uses it yet.

### Fixed

- **The app's sheet sign-in resists leaked start links (CB-352).** The start URL's challenge and state can reach access logs, and so can the Continue page's request id. Each challenge and each state now starts one request only (kept 30 days); a copy gets the same answer as any bad link. The approval belongs to the sheet browser that started the request, through a 10-minute httpOnly cookie, so a request id from a log can't be approved in another browser. None of these values, nor a code, appears in Socio's logs.
- **Release notes' issue links open Socio's own board (CB-381).** The issue keys in the release notes, What's New and the early-access features in Settings name issues on Socio's product board. They linked to the board of the instance showing them, so anywhere else a link to `CB-157` opened that board's own, unrelated issue 157. They now open on `https://brain.ingenioventures.com` in a new tab, and stay local links on that instance itself. Every other issue link is unchanged.
- **Backups no longer grow with your data (CB-378).** The in-app backup (scheduled, by hand and before upgrades) streamed only its SQLite copies; the object mirror and the off-site copy read each file and object whole, and sealed the database in memory, so its memory grew to several times the largest file and a 2 GB container could kill it. They now stream, a megabyte at a time: on a test with 400 MB of database and 1 GB sent off-site, the backup's peak memory went from about 1.8 GB to under 100 MB, about what it is with a quarter of the data. Backups, the off-site layout and the sealed format are unchanged, and `pnpm run backup open` reads old and new files, now in flat memory as well. The off-site copy seals each file into `BACKUP_DIR/offsite.sealed.tmp` before sending it, so `BACKUP_DIR` needs room for one more copy of the largest database file while it runs.

### Upgrading

- The data space gets migration 11 (`dataset history retention`, expand only): two nullable columns on `data_datasets`, `historyDays` and `compactRequestedAt`. It runs at boot under a start-first deploy; the previous release reads and writes datasets without them. No dataset file changes until someone sets a history limit, switches a table's history off or asks for a compaction. Going back a release keeps the settings unused: nothing is pruned, and a column added to a table without history by the previous release turns that table's history back on.
- The data space gets migration 12 (`dataset analytical bytes`, expand only): `data_datasets.analyticalBytes`, 0 for every dataset. No dataset file changes until a plugin makes an analytical table, which waits until the previous release has stopped. Wherever a plugin uses analytical tables, give the app's container about 1.3 GB more memory (`DATA_ANALYTICS_QUERY_CONCURRENCY` × `DATA_ANALYTICS_QUERY_MEMORY_MB` plus 256 MB), and keep `USER_DATA_DIR` on local disk. The backup volume gets a `parts/` mirror. Going back a release leaves the parts on disk and in backups but unread, and lists the hidden `_olap_tables` and `_olap_parts` as tables of those datasets.
- The auth database gets migration 12 (`sheet requests: unique and bound to their browser`, expand only): unique indexes on `auth_device_request.challenge` and `state` (a later request with the same challenge or state, such as a retried start URL, is deleted first, even if still live; that sign-in must be started again in the app) and a `browserHash` column. Start rows are now kept 30 days (the challenge and state stay taken) instead of about 20 minutes. The browser binding holds once no previous-release instance serves `/api/auth/device/approve`; during a start-first rollout, an old instance can still approve without it. A request started on the previous release can't be approved by the new one and must be started again.
- The auth database gets migration 14 (`push endpoints to clean up`, expand only): `auth_device_push_leftover`, the push endpoints a device no longer holds whose subscriptions are still to be removed. The previous release ignores the table.
- The auth database gets migration 13 (`sign-in link channels`, expand only): `auth_link_via`, which channel each sign-in link went by (a hash of its token, kept while the link lives). A link sent before the upgrade counts as Telegram's, as it was. An emailed link carries its token sealed with the server's secret, which the previous release can't open, so it can never redeem one, as sent or altered: an emailed link opened on a previous-release instance (during a start-first rollout, or after a rollback) fails and must be asked for again. Turn email links on once no previous-release instance serves sign-in.
- The database gets a new migration set, `bundle` 1 (`import runs and map`, expand only): two new tables, `import_runs` and `import_map`, which record what a workspace bundle import brought in. It runs at boot under a start-first deploy; the previous release ignores both tables, and nothing is written to them until an operator applies an import (CB-380).

## v0.40.0 (2026-11-16)

Spaces can be renamed, from their Settings or by a socio, and keep every page and link. Operators take note: the calendar is no longer offered by default, so add `bundled:calendar` to `PLUGIN_CATALOGS` to keep offering it.

- **Operators:** no plugin is offered by default any more; add `bundled:calendar` to `PLUGIN_CATALOGS` to keep offering the calendar (CB-371)
- Rename a Space from its Settings, or ask a socio to, and keep every page and link (CB-366)
- A socio's computer is told how to download data space files and upload them back, and a company socio's `shared/` path no longer leads its scripts to a 404 (CB-361)
- Deleting a Space checks the person's rights again as it runs, and a socio's "Space already exists" refusal in a shared chat no longer names someone's private Space
- Service plugins can take a SaaS's webhooks on their own origin, and admins update a plugin to its catalog's new version in place, keeping its data (CB-370)

### Breaking

- **Operators: the calendar is no longer offered by default (CB-371).** An instance now offers exactly what `PLUGIN_CATALOGS` lists. No plugin is offered just because Socio ships it: the calendar, until now offered on every instance, is offered only where `PLUGIN_CATALOGS` lists `bundled:calendar`, like every other catalog entry. Unset, empty or `off`, **Plugins** offers nothing. An installed calendar is not touched: it stays installed and keeps working as before, with its audience, settings, events, tools, feeds and connected-agent scopes (`plugins:calendar:read` and `plugins:calendar:write` stay listed on every instance, so agents can still be connected to an installed calendar that is no longer offered). Without the offer, **Plugins → Installed** marks it "no longer in the catalog", and once uninstalled it cannot be installed again until it is offered. `-bundled:<id>` is still accepted, and still refuses to boot for an unknown id or one also listed, but it now takes nothing away. `bundled:calendar` is a catalog the operator sets, so `PLUGIN_CATALOG_HOSTS` and `PLUGIN_CATALOG_DENY_HOSTS` apply to it. No migration: the previous release runs on the same database. Help: [Plugins](/docs/admin/plugins).

### New

- **Public webhooks for service plugins (CB-370).** A service plugin's manifest can declare `webhooks: [{ path, method?, maxBodyBytes?, perMinute? }]`: exact paths on its own origin that the apps proxy serves with no session, through the existing public edge, so a SaaS such as a CRM or Stripe can call the plugin without a tunnel of its own. Each path takes one method (`POST`, or `GET` for a single OAuth callback), is rate-limited (default 120 a minute, at most 600) and size-limited (default 256 KiB, at most 1 MiB), and gets the body byte for byte. Cookies, forwarding headers, every client `x-cb-*` header and a socio's credential are stripped, no identity header is added, and the ingress attaches the plugin's proof. The plugin verifies the sender itself, from a signature over the raw body or its OAuth `state`. A path the manifest doesn't declare still needs a session. See docs/PLUGINS.md, "Public webhooks". No migration. A plugin installed before this gets its webhooks, with no reinstall, when the catalog lists a version that declares them and an admin updates it (below).
- **Update a service plugin in place (CB-370).** When the catalog lists a different manifest for an installed service plugin (a new version, image, permissions, datasets or webhooks), its card under **Plugins** shows **Update to** with the new version (`POST /api/plugins/<id>/update`, admins). Update adopts that manifest without an uninstall: the plugin's data, datasets, settings it still declares, audience, replicas and sessions stay, an enabled plugin's service moves to the new image with a new identity version, and when the runtime refuses nothing else changes (new datasets the version declares stay, as on a failed install). Every runtime of a deploy serves the updated webhook paths at once. Its sockets close, its data tokens end and socios' grants are checked again. Every update is in the workspace audit with both versions and the webhook paths before and after. A restart or a new catalog file alone never changes what an installed plugin serves. A webhook request reaches only a service running the installed manifest's revision, and only its tasks of the current container (image, settings and secrets), so neither an update's rollout nor a change racing it on another runtime lets other code answer a public path (it answers 502 until they agree; a racing change is provisioned again from the database). This needs the apps runtime and socket proxy of this release: service and task listings carry a `SocioRevision` digest in place of the removed environment, and plugin services a `socio.plugin.revision` label.

- **Rename a Space (CB-366).** A Space's **Settings** tab has a **Name** field for its editors (workspace admins edit every Space): rename it there, or ask a socio, which now has `rename_space` for a Space it was given, on behalf of one of that Space's editors. Only the name changes. Its pages, chats, memory, people and who sees it stay as they are, and every link keeps working, because links name the Space's id, not its name. The name follows the rule for a new Space (1 to 60 characters, trimmed), and a socio won't take the name of another Space it may use in that chat (it never names a Space the chat can't see). Members and readers are refused (403), and someone who can't see the Space gets the 404 of one that isn't there. Each rename is recorded in the workspace audit (`update` on the Space). API: `PATCH /api/spaces/:id` with `{ "name": "…" }`. No migration.
- **Devices and 30-day sessions for the Socio app (CB-352).** The app sends each workspace a random install id of its own (`X-Socio-Install`), and the server keeps only its SHA-256, never the id. A sign-in from the app, by password too, belongs to that device and lasts 30 days, renewed to 30 at most once a day, with the cookie saying so; web sessions keep seven days and the same cookies. A device belongs to one person: another person signing in with the same install id gets a device of their own, and nobody else's session ends because of it. A session made without an install id (any session from before this release) stays as it is, with or without one later. A device that is signed out takes its sessions with it, including on Better Auth's own endpoints, and a sign-in without "remember me" is never renewed into a lasting cookie. A request from the app that is refused now records why (the session expired or ended, the device was signed out, the person was deactivated), for the coming 401 codes. Each workspace now has a random id of its own, made once, for the coming workspace manifest. Workspace apps and plugins never receive the install id.
- **Sign in to the Socio app through the system sign-in sheet (CB-352).** The app opens the workspace's own sign-in in the phone's sign-in sheet (`/api/auth/device/start`, with a PKCE challenge). A person already signed in there approves it with one click on the coming Continue page (`/api/auth/device/approve`), and the browser is sent to `link.socio.sh` with a one-time code in the link's fragment, never its query. The app redeems the code with its verifier (`/api/auth/device/redeem`) for a 30-day device session. That session carries the approving session's sign-in method, so turning that method off in the access policy ends it too. A code works once, for 5 minutes, only for the app build that asked; a wrong verifier uses it up. It stops working when the approving browser signs out or the person is deactivated. Codes are stored only as hashes and never logged. Each such sign-in is recorded in the workspace audit and sends the person a "New device signed in" notice. `AUTH_APP_LINK_HOST` (default `link.socio.sh`) points the links at a test bench's own host.
- **Add a device by QR code, confirmed with 6 digits (CB-352).** A person signed in on the web makes a QR code (`/api/auth/device/pair`). It is a `link.socio.sh` link with a 12-symbol pairing code in its fragment, good for 5 minutes, and a new one voids the last. The new device claims it (`/api/auth/device/pair/claim`). The server then makes 6 random digits that only that device is shown, with the account it would sign in as. The web shows the device's type and time (never the digits), and the person types the digits there. Only the web session that made the QR code can answer it. Three wrong tries, "Not my device", 2 minutes, or the page closing ends the claim and its QR code, and there are at most 10 tries per 15 minutes per session. So a QR code seen on a shared screen and claimed by someone else first gets them nowhere: the person, whose own device was told "already used", has no digits to type. The new device then gets a 30-day session carrying the web session's sign-in method, recorded in the audit with a notice to the person. Links are for the production app, which needs the workspace on https at its default port. `AUTH_APP_PREVIEW_LINKS=true` makes them for the preview build instead.
- **Sign-in codes for the Socio app, by Telegram or email (CB-352).** In the app, a person enters their email and gets an 8-symbol code (`/api/auth/device/code`). It goes to their linked Telegram, or to their email while an admin has **Sign-in links by email** on, with a link that fills it in on the device that asked. The code works once, for 15 minutes, only in that app (PKCE), with 5 tries per request, counted in the database so a restart doesn't reset them. An email with no account gets the same answer, tries and refusals, as fast. Requests share the web's limits (5 per 15 minutes per address, 3 codes per account). A code works only while its channel still reaches the same recipient: unlinking that Telegram, or linking another, kills it. The device's session is a sign-in link session (`link`, or `link:email` by email), so the access policy ends it with its method. The web's "Send me a sign-in link" is unchanged.
- **Sign-in links by email, an opt-in access policy key (CB-352).** Settings → Access has a new **Sign-in links by email** toggle, off by default. It can be turned on (with the usual step-up) only once an email channel delivers sign-in links (the coming email channel, which needs `SMTP_URL`), and turns off by itself if that goes away. Its sessions get their own sign-in method, `link:email`, so turning it off ends only those. While it's on it counts as a way in for every account, so turning it off warns about, and signs out, people whose only way in was email. Nothing sends email links yet; the coming email channel uses this switch.

### Changed

- **The device sign-in contract (CB-352).** `@socio/shared/device-sign-in` holds what the coming device sign-in endpoints (`/api/auth/device/*`), the workspace manifest (`/.well-known/socio/workspace`), the link domain (`link.socio.sh`) and the native app agree on: request and answer schemas, the code formats (one alphabet without look-alike characters, shown in groups of four, and Add a device's 6 digits), PKCE (S256 only), install ids, the refusal codes the app words, and the links' shape, with codes and tokens in the fragment only. Nothing uses it yet, so nothing changes for anyone.
- **Sign-in codes have their message (CB-352).** The channels that carry sign-in links (Telegram, and email next) can now carry a sign-in code the Socio app asked for instead: "Your Socio sign-in code: ABCD-EFGH. Only type this into the Socio app on your own device. It works once, for 15 minutes." with a link that fills it in on that device. Links keep their message, word for word. Nothing sends codes yet.

### Fixed

- **Settings → Secrets is easier to read.** In Settings → Secrets, each plugin's and app's secrets are one aligned list, required ones first, each showing whether it is set (and when) with its action in a fixed column; it stacks on a phone, and "Rotate identity" moved to the plugin's footer. Values stay write-only (CB-377).
- **Deleting a Space checks who may again as it runs.** A delete waiting behind another change (an editor removed, a grant rolled back) is now refused when that change left the person without the right to delete it, as renaming already was. Before, the check ran only when it was asked, so such a delete still went through.
- **A computer's scripts can find the data space's file routes (CB-361).** The routes already existed, but no socio was told about them, so a socio that wanted to build a PDF from files on its computer guessed URLs and got 404s. `computer_exec` now describes them: `GET $CB_DATA_URL/files?prefix=` lists files, `GET $CB_DATA_URL/files/raw?path=` returns a file's bytes, and `PUT $CB_DATA_URL/files` with `{ path, content, encoding: "base64", mediaType }` saves one, all with `Authorization: Bearer $CB_DATA_TOKEN`. A company socio's `files_copy` names its shared folder `shared/<name>`, while its computer's token names the same file `<name>`. The tool descriptions now say so, and a read of `shared/<name>` from the computer that finds no file says which path to use. Access is unchanged. No migration.
- **A session with no recorded sign-in method no longer outlives the access policy.** Sessions from before the access policy (CB-124) recorded no sign-in method and were let through, so turning a method off didn't end one that a person with another way in kept using (sessions slide, so they could last indefinitely). Such a session is now refused, as is any new session from a way in Socio doesn't recognize. People with one sign in again, once.
- **`create_space` no longer names a private Space in a shared chat.** A socio refuses a new Space whose name another Space already has only when everyone reading the chat can see that Space, as `rename_space` does. In a room, the person's private Spaces are never named in the refusal (the create dialog allows a repeated name too). In their own chat, every Space they see still counts.
- **Iggy's avatar no longer has palm trees (CB-298).** The two palm trees left beside Iggy after CB-295 are gone from the web art and the mobile app's avatar pictures, so only the iguana shows on the theme's background.
- **Tests that failed only on a busy machine pass there too.** The logo upload test waits for the file to be read instead of 20 ms; the telemetry opt-out scan parses only modules that name CopilotKit; the scheduled backup test waits for the backup's report instead of polling for a second; the backup and row history tests keep their files in memory where there is a tmpfs, so they don't wait on a shared disk; the mini app tests lease their port so two runs at once never pick the same one. No change to the app.

### Upgrading

- **To keep offering the calendar, add `bundled:calendar` to `PLUGIN_CATALOGS`** before or with this upgrade: `PLUGIN_CATALOGS=bundled:calendar` where it was unset, or append it to the existing list (`bundled:calendar,file:/run/catalog.json`). If the instance pins `PLUGIN_CATALOG_HOSTS` or sets `PLUGIN_CATALOG_DENY_HOSTS`, its own host must be allowed, or it refuses to boot. Where the calendar should not be offered, do nothing, and remove `-bundled:calendar` when convenient (it no longer does anything). An installed calendar keeps running either way. Rolling back to v0.39.0 is safe: it offers the calendar by default again and reads `bundled:calendar` as before.
- Migration auth 7 deletes the sessions that have no sign-in method (only ones from before CB-124, or a password change of one). Those people are signed out and sign in again. The previous release runs on the result, so a start-first upgrade needs nothing else.
- The auth database gets migration 8 (`devices`, expand only): the `auth_device` table, a nullable `deviceId` on sessions, and `auth_workspace` with the workspace's random id. It runs at boot, with the usual backup first; the previous release runs on the result.
- The auth database gets migration 9 (`device sign-in requests`, expand only): the `auth_device_request` table. It runs at boot, with the usual backup first; the previous release runs on the result.
- The auth database gets migration 10 (`add a device`, expand only): the `auth_device_pairing` table. It runs at boot; the previous release runs on the result.
- The auth database gets migration 11 (`sign-in codes`, expand only): the `auth_device_code` table. It never stores the email asked for. It runs at boot; the previous release runs on the result.

## v0.39.0 (2026-11-13)

Service plugins install again, admins give code access with groups instead of an environment list, signed-in pages load faster in workspaces with many Spaces, operators can stop offering a bundled plugin without editing the catalog, and a page moved to another Space brings its link cards' images with it.

- Installing a service plugin works again: the socket proxy changes only the network list of the apps service, and a refused change says why
- Admins and groups they make may run code, managed under Settings → People → Groups (CB-376)
- Signed-in pages check Space access in a few queries, however many Spaces and socios there are (CB-374)
- `PLUGIN_CATALOGS` takes `-bundled:<id>` to stop offering a default plugin
- A moved page's link-card images, pictures and cover show for readers of the new Space, whoever moved it (CB-362)

### Breaking

- **Who may run code is a permission now, given by groups (CB-376).** Admins and operators may run code (coding sessions, and the coding gateways for models and Git) without being listed anywhere. Anyone else needs a group that gives **Run code**. `CODE_USERS` no longer decides it, and Settings → Access no longer has a **Code access** list: a save that sets `codeUsers` is refused with a pointer to People → Groups. With mock auth (local development) there are no groups: admins may run code and nobody else.

### New

- **Stop offering a default plugin.** `PLUGIN_CATALOGS` takes `-bundled:<id>` to stop offering a bundled plugin every instance offers by default, and keeps the rest of the catalog: `PLUGIN_CATALOGS=-bundled:calendar,file:/run/catalog.json` offers that file's plugins without the calendar. An unknown id refuses to boot, as `bundled:<id>` does, and so does an id both listed and excluded. An exclusion only takes offers away, so `PLUGIN_CATALOG_HOSTS` and `PLUGIN_CATALOG_DENY_HOSTS` do not apply to a value of only exclusions. It never uninstalls anything or deletes data: a plugin already installed stays installed and working, **Plugins → Installed** marks it "no longer in the catalog", and an admin can still disable or uninstall it there (uninstall keeps its data). Help: [Plugins](/docs/admin/plugins).
- **Groups under Settings → People (CB-376).** A new **Groups** tab lets admins create, rename and delete groups, choose what each gives (**Run code** is the only permission for now), and add and remove people. A person's page lists their groups and can take them out of one. Changes apply at once: removing someone from a group, deleting the group or taking **Run code** off it stops them at their next check. Groups are Better Auth's organization plugin with dynamic access control, in one hidden workspace organization; its own `/api/auth/organization/*` endpoints are not served, and groups change only through the admin routes under `/api/groups`.

### Fixed

- **Installing a service plugin works again.** v0.38.0 refused every service plugin install: the socket proxy hides the apps service's environment, secrets and configs from the runtime, and then refused the runtime's network attach because the spec it sent back lacked them. The proxy now compares an infra service update with the spec the runtime sees, refuses any update that carries environment, secrets or configs, requires the current `version`, and forwards the engine's full spec with only the task's network attachments changed, without the caller's registry credentials. The service-level network list may not change, so an attachment cannot be moved where Swarm ignores it. Had the old update gone through, it would have dropped the service's environment and secrets.
- **A refused plugin change says why.** A plugin install, enable or update that the apps runtime or the socket proxy refuses is now answered as a refusal (HTTP 409) with its reason, and an unavailable runtime as a 503 that says so. Both were a 502 before, which the browser could show as "Server returned an unreadable response." Other failures show a short message and leave the detail in the server log.
- **The socket proxy has a health check that works.** It inherited the apps runtime image's check of port 4330, which it does not serve. The plugin overlay now checks the proxy's allowed `/_ping` route.
- **Signed-in pages no longer stall on Space access (CB-374).** Each page asked two or three questions of the database for every Space, once for the sidebar and again for every socio, so a workspace with many Spaces and socios held the server for hundreds of milliseconds per page, and longer right after an update, when every open tab reloads. A page now reads the person's Space memberships and the private Spaces once and decides the rest in memory. Who can see and edit what is unchanged.
- **A moved page brings its link cards' images (CB-362).** Moving a page to another Space now copies the images on its link cards into that Space, as it already copied its pictures and cover, in the same step as the move. Readers who can see the new Space but not the old one see the cards' previews instead of broken ones, and the cards no longer point at the old Space's images. A socio's `move_space_page` now does the same as **Move to…**: it copies the page's pictures, link-card images and cover too.

### Upgrading

- To stop offering the calendar on an instance, add `-bundled:calendar` to its `PLUGIN_CATALOGS`. If the calendar is already installed there, an admin uninstalls it from **Plugins → Installed** when it should go; the setting alone leaves it running.
- If your stack gives `docker-socket-proxy` its own health check, point it at `http://127.0.0.1:2375/_ping` (as `deployment/portainer/plugins.overlay.yml` now does). Any other path, such as `/`, is refused and logged as a denial on every check.
- The auth database gets migration 6 (`groups`, expand only): the organization plugin's tables, the workspace organization, and a member row for every account. It runs at boot, with the usual backup first.
- `CODE_USERS` is deprecated and read for this release only. On the first boot, if it (as narrowed under Settings → Access) lists anyone, its non-admins are put in a group called **Code**, once; the log says how many. While it is set, the server logs a deprecation warning. Check the **Code** group under People → Groups, then remove `CODE_USERS` (and `DISPATCH_CODE_USERS`, if still there) from the stack env.

## v0.38.0 (2026-11-11)

Approval requests name the chat they were asked in, service plugins can be pinned to a node on a multi-node Swarm, and a starting app no longer stalls itself while its health check waits.

- Approval requests say which chat asked for them, so the mobile app marks exactly that chat
- Operators can pin service plugins to nodes, so a plugin's volume stays where its data is
- A booting app is no longer paused by its own health check

### New

- **Approval requests name their chat.** `ApprovalView` gets `threadId`: the chat the request was asked in, or null when no chat asked (an app, a schedule). It comes with every list, get, decide and undo, and with `GET /api/approvals`. The mobile app uses it to mark the right chat as needing your approval, even when one socio runs in two chats at once.
- **Pin service plugins to nodes.** New operator settings `APPS_RUNTIME_PLUGIN_CONSTRAINTS` (apps runtime) and `SOCKET_PROXY_PLUGIN_CONSTRAINTS` (socket proxy) take a comma-separated list of Swarm placement constraints, such as `node.hostname == node-1`. The runtime puts them on every plugin service it creates or updates. The proxy enforces its own copy: a plugin service must carry exactly that placement, or none when the list is empty. Unset keeps today's behavior.

### Fixed

- **The health check no longer stalls a booting app (CB-318).** The container health check captured the main thread's stack on any failed `/healthz`, including while a new task was still loading and refused the connection. Attaching the debugger to a booting process stalled it for 2.6 s and cost one public probe during the v0.37.0 upgrade. It now captures only when `/healthz` times out on a server that is listening.

### Upgrading

- On a multi-node Swarm, set `APPS_RUNTIME_PLUGIN_CONSTRAINTS` and `SOCKET_PROXY_PLUGIN_CONSTRAINTS` to the same list before installing a service plugin that has a volume. If only one of the two is set, plugin installs and updates are refused. Existing plugin services take the constraints on their next install or update.

## v0.37.0 (2026-11-10)

Datasets get fresh query statistics on demand and show their indexes on the Data page; transfers say when an app restart was skipped; release and CI builds survive a lost spot runner.

- Run ANALYZE on a dataset, and see each table's indexes on the Data page (CB-355)
- Transfers note when a moved app's restart was skipped (CB-360)
- Builds rerun automatically when a spot runner disappears

### New

- **Fresh query statistics and visible indexes on datasets (CB-355).** A dataset's owner, its apps and socios can run SQLite `ANALYZE` on the whole dataset or one table, so the query planner picks the right index once rows have grown. It is a declared operation, never SQL: new socio tool `data_analyze`, new data API routes `POST /api/data/datasets/:id/analyze` and `POST …/tables/:table/analyze` (operation `analyze`, tier `write`). A socio's or app's call counts toward the per-minute operation budget (the owner's own doesn't, like their other changes), and it needs no approval on a dataset shared with a Space, since it changes no rows. The Data page lists each table's indexes (name, columns, unique), read-only.

### Changed

- **A transfer says when it couldn't restart a moved app (CB-360).** When the apps runtime can't be reached while a person's work is handed over, the transfer result now notes it under Apps for each promoted app it moves ("restart skipped; the app picks up the new owner's token on its next start") instead of skipping the restart silently. The app's old data token still ends.

## v0.36.0 (2026-11-10)

Service plugins can own a workspace dataset and write to it on their own schedule, with atomic batches, and servers now accept phone pushes through Socio's push relay.

- Service plugins own workspace datasets, with short-lived data tokens
- Atomic batches of up to 32 statements in the data API
- Admin-set limits for plugin datasets
- Phone pushes through push.socio.sh are allowed by default

### New

- **Service plugins own workspace datasets.** A service plugin's manifest may declare `datasets` (at most 16, each a `name` and an optional `description`). Install and enable create them, or adopt the ones it already owns by name; disable and uninstall keep them, and only an admin deletes one. They are owned by `plugin:<id>`, the company's like a company socio's workspace datasets (`socio:<id>`), so a person's transfer or purge never touches them and no person's quota counts them. The plugin exchanges its identity token and proof at `POST /api/workload/data-token` for a 15-minute data token (purpose `plugin`) that reaches only its own declared datasets through the data API, writes as the plugin with no `x-cb-obo`, and ends when the plugin is disabled or uninstalled or its identity version retires. Admins see the datasets on the Data page with a **Plugin** badge and manage them as an owner does (browse, export, share with a Space, grant a socio, delete); members reach one through a Space share, socios through the usual grants. See docs/PLUGINS.md, Datasets.
- **Atomic batches in the data API.** `POST /api/data/datasets/:id/batch` runs up to 32 statements in one transaction, all or nothing: SQL that changes rows (`INSERT … SELECT` with `json_extract` included, `UPDATE`, `REPLACE`, and `DELETE` in a plugin's own datasets) or rows to insert or upsert. Every statement goes through the same write guard as a single write (no DDL, `PRAGMA`, `ATTACH`, transactions, recursive CTEs or the engine's own tables), keeps its own limits and counts against `DATA_STATEMENTS_PER_MINUTE`. A batch runs in the query runner's process and is stopped and rolled back if it passes `DATA_BATCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s); its `504` says so, or that it committed as it was stopped (or that this isn't known), so the caller checks before retrying. Socios', jobs', apps' and plugins' tokens may send one where they may write (operation `batch`, tier `write`); a dataset shared with a Space is refused unless the token is a promoted app's or a plugin's own.
- **Limits for plugin datasets.** **Settings → General → Plugin datasets** sets the size of one plugin dataset (`DATA_PLUGIN_DATASET_MAX_MB`, 100) and the rows one query of it returns (`DATA_PLUGIN_QUERY_MAX_ROWS`, 500), apart from people's limits.

### Changed

- **Phone pushes through Socio's relay are allowed by default.** `DEFAULT_PUSH_HOSTS` now includes `push.socio.sh` and `push-staging.socio.sh` (exact names, no wildcard). A deployment that sets `PUSH_ENDPOINT_HOSTS` replaces the defaults, so it must list these hosts too for the mobile app's notifications to reach phones.

## v0.35.0 (2026-11-09)

Datasets can carry indexes, so apps and socios can make slow queries fast; a company socio's shared data can be promoted into an app's own dataset; handing over a person's work now restarts the apps it moves.

- Indexes on datasets, declared by owners, apps and socios (CB-355)
- Promote an app built on a company socio's shared data into its own dataset (CB-354)
- A busy chat's refusal has a stable code the apps understand (CB-357)
- Transfers restart moved apps with the new owner's access (CB-360)

### New

- **Indexes on datasets (CB-355).** Socios, apps and a dataset's owner can declare secondary indexes on a dataset's tables, so a query that looks rows up by another column than the key stops scanning the whole table and timing out. An index is a name, the table's columns in order and an optional `unique`, never SQL; the engine checks it against the schema and creates it, at most 10 per table, counted toward the dataset's quota. New socio tools `data_create_index` and `data_drop_index`; new data API routes `POST /api/data/datasets/:id/tables/:table/indexes` and `DELETE …/indexes/:name` (operations `createIndex` and `dropIndex`, tier `write`); `data_describe` and `GET /api/data/datasets/:id` list each table's `indexes`. Indexes live in the dataset file, so backups, the off-site copy, app promotion and transfers keep them.

### Changed

- **A busy chat's refusal has a stable code (CB-357).** A run sent to a conversation whose turn is still running, here or on another runtime, is refused before its run or message is stored, with `409 {"error": "Thread already running", "code": "thread_running"}` (the text is unchanged). The web app says the socio is still answering and shows the message as not sent; the native app's busy-thread rule reads the code. Clients should match `code`, never the text.

### Fixed

- **Promoting an app built on a company socio takes its data from the socio's shared dataset into a workspace dataset (CB-354).** A company socio's apps and chats use its shared dataset as scratch (CB-337), but the promotion wizard showed the promoting person's own scratch, usually empty, and a move acted on that. The wizard now shows the shared dataset and its tables, says everyone who uses the socio sees it, and says where the data goes. Move or share takes the app's tables (with their indexes) out of the shared dataset into a new workspace dataset: it lives in the company socio's area, not in the promoter's data space, so a transfer leaves it in place (CB-360) and the shared area's retention never deletes it. The shared area's audit records what left it and where. The socio then asks, on the promoter's approval card (always a person's decision, never bypassed or auto-approved), to keep writing to it; once approved it can, from chats and its computer. Deleting the app with the data it brought deletes the workspace dataset too, and the socio's access with it. Keep leaves the promoted app on the shared dataset, through a grant that survives a transfer.
- **People: handing over a person's work leaves no loose ends (CB-360).** A transfer now restarts the promoted apps it moves that are running, so they act for their new owner straight away instead of holding the old owner's data token until their next start; the transfer's result says which restarted. The permanent removal's dry run lists a person's scheduled tasks, socios, apps, jobs and conversations once, as what they still own and a transfer moves, not again as separate blockers. Settings → People reloads the list after a transfer started from its Deactivate dialog.

### Upgrading

- No new tables: indexes are stored in each dataset's own SQLite file.

## v0.34.0 (2026-11-08)

Mini apps now repair themselves, admins get a full People page for onboarding, offboarding and handing over a person's work, and admins hear about it when the continuous backup copy falls behind.

- Mini apps heal themselves from refused tokens, stopped processes and crash loops (CB-291)
- A People page per person, with transfer of everything they own and permanent removal for operators (CB-360)
- Admins are alerted when the continuous backup copy lags or its WAL grows (CB-315)
- Purging a socio also removes its computer

### New

- **A People page for admins (CB-360).** Settings → People lists everyone with role, state and last sign-in, with a Service accounts tab. Each person has a page at `/settings/people/:id` with their access, sign-in devices (sign out one), activity and what they own. Deactivating shows what stays theirs and can hand all of it, or chosen kinds, to someone else first; operators can remove a deactivated person for good after a dry run and typing their name. Revoked service keys can now be deleted.
- **Alerts when the continuous copy falls behind (CB-315).** Every minute Socio compares the Litestream replica's newest file and the database's WAL size against thresholds (5/30 minutes, 256 MB/1 GB by default; `LITESTREAM_*` variables or Settings → General → Continuous copy alerts). Admins get one notice per change of level, an hourly reminder while critical, and a notice on recovery; Operations → Health shows the level, and `/healthz` reports only `litestream: off|ok|warning|critical`.
- **Mini apps heal themselves (CB-291).** A data token the data API refuses (expired, revoked, or from before a restart) names its app: Socio renews it in the app's token file at once, so an app that reads it again on a 401 carries on without a restart. An app that keeps sending the dead token has its process replaced: the platform stops its tracked process group (or whatever of its identity still holds its port), waits for the port, and starts it with a live token, so a replacement no longer fails with EADDRINUSE. An app in a socio's computer that stops answering, and a workspace app the runtime gave up on, are restarted the same way. Repairs are bounded: six, 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 minutes apart; then admins get one notice and the app waits for its owner's Start or a new version. Its data is never touched. The app's card shows its state (recovering, degraded, failed), its attempts and last recovery to its owner and admins, and Operations → Health lists the apps that aren't healthy and whether the once-a-minute check of workspace apps works.
- **Hand everything a person owns to someone else, and remove people for good from the web (server, CB-360).** Admins see what a person owns, by kind (`GET /api/accounts/:id/assets`), and move all of it or chosen kinds to another active person (`POST /api/accounts/:id/transfer`): apps, schedules, jobs and goals, personal socios, Space places, their data space (datasets, private scratch areas, files and media), conversations and memories (which arrive as proposals). Their sign-in, subscriptions, MCP logins, Telegram, preferences and notices stay theirs. Each transfer is one transaction, audited for both people. The deactivation preview lists everything that stays theirs. Operators remove a deactivated person who owns nothing any more, in person (`POST /api/accounts/:id/purge`): the purge's dry run, then their typed name and a step-up, with the purge's backup, checks and audit.
- **The purge removes a purged socio's computer too.** The computer supervisor gets `POST /computers/:id/destroy`, which removes a computer's container and its three volumes, and only its own. The purge's dry run lists the purged socios' computers, and after the commit the purge has the supervisor remove them (`purge --resume` finishes one that failed). Update the supervisor image with this release before purging socios that have computers: with an older one, a purge of socios refuses before deleting anything, unless `--keep-computers` leaves their computers.

### Upgrading

- One new table, `app_health`, created at boot (expand only).

## v0.33.2 (2026-11-08)

A maintenance release: a person renamed offline keeps the identity mini apps, plugins and coding workspaces know them by.

- Mini apps, plugins and coding workspaces keep seeing a renamed person as the same person (CB-338)

### Fixed

- **A renamed person stays the same person to apps and plugins (CB-338).** `backup rename-user` keeps the id that the opaque person ids, actor envelopes and coding volume keys are derived from (a new `person_seeds` table), so per-person state in mini apps and plugins stays theirs. An id a rename retired is never passed on to someone who is given it later.

### Upgrading

- One new table, `person_seeds`, created at boot (expand only).

## v0.33.1 (2026-11-08)

A maintenance release: operators can remove test records for good and rename a user id offline, a missing dataset file is reported instead of looking empty, and computers started under an older key recover by themselves.

- A missing dataset file is reported as missing, never shown as an empty dataset (CB-340)
- Computers started under an older computer key restart with the current one on their own (CB-331)
- A company socio sees its shared dataset as its scratch in chat too (CB-337)
- Operators can purge test records and rename a user id from the command line (CB-338)

### New

- **Rename a user id offline (CB-338).** `backup rename-user <old> <new>` (in the container: `node dist/server/server/backup/backup-cli.js rename-user …`) renames a person's id in every table, key and JSON value that holds it, and moves their data space folder. It runs only while the app is stopped, is a dry run unless given `--apply`, and `--leftovers <id>` lists anything that still names the old id.

- **Operators can purge test records for good.** `purge` (in the container: `node dist/server/server/purge/purge-cli.js`) removes listed people, socios, Spaces, pages, datasets, apps, jobs, rooms and conversations with everything that belongs to them, including sign-in accounts, sessions, memberships, grants, messages, dataset files and stored objects. A dry run is the default and prints the rows, files and bytes that would go, and the history that stays. It refuses protected ids, active operators and admins, schedules, and anything under a listed record that the list doesn't name or cascade to. `--apply` backs up first. See SETUP.md, "Purge test records".

### Changed

- **The scripts no longer sign in as `alice` by default.** On a local mock-auth server, `exit-test`, `ui-smoke`, `wait-idle`, `learning-live` and `performance-smoke` sign in as the first configured admin and member, or as `--admin-user` and `--member-user`.

### Fixed

- **A missing dataset file is an error, not an empty dataset (CB-340).** Reading, writing, querying or copying a registered dataset whose file is gone now fails with "dataset file missing" instead of silently creating an empty file, and `/healthz` and the Operations page report how many are missing.

- **A company socio sees its shared dataset as its scratch in chat too (CB-337).** In chat, a company socio's scratch now resolves to its shared dataset, as it already did in jobs and on its computer, so what its jobs wrote shows up in conversation. Page dataset views accept it for the same people who can see it.

- **Computers started under an older computer key recover on their own (CB-331).** A computer that refuses the app's credential is restarted once with the current key, keeping its volumes, and the call is retried. Attempts are limited and audited.

- **No more "Migration … changed after it was applied" at start.** v0.30.0's formatting pass reformatted ten applied migrations (core 1, workspace 1 and 19, pages 1, 5 and 7, computers 1, runs 1 and 3, chat 2) without changing what they do, so every server that ran them before v0.30.0 logged a warning per migration at each start. Each now lists the checksum those servers recorded. The build now fails when a released migration changes (`src/server/modules/released-migrations.json`), and `pnpm release` records each release's migrations.

## v0.33.0 (2026-11-07)

Spaces get a knowledge graph, chats suggest their own names, a socio can edit its own SOUL, and admins decide who can create Spaces. Under the hood the server now runs on Effect 4, and the first pieces of a native mobile app have landed.

- Knowledge graph for Space pages: tags, typed links across Spaces and where each link came from (CB-328)
- Chats suggest a name that fits what they are about, to accept, reject or change inline (CB-292)
- A socio can edit its own SOUL (CB-330)
- Admins choose whether members can create Spaces, and the Releases page loads as you scroll
- On a phone, the header shows the title, one status indicator and one menu for everything else

### New

- **Append to a page from a connected agent.** `append_page` adds Markdown to the end of a page for a person who granted `pages:write`. Create and update already went through the pages service; writes now use the same Space access check as the page routes and the same “a person may edit” check as moving a page, and Markdown is stored the way the editor saves it. A connection that only has `pages:read` cannot write, and approving the connection again is how it gains `pages:write`.

- **Knowledge graph for Space pages (CB-328).** Pages now carry tags and typed links that can point across Spaces, with source attribution recorded for each link. A socio can unfurl a linked graph recursively, and delete a Space page through a socio tool. New `page_tags` and `page_links` tables (additive migration); the page library and snapshots account for the new columns.

- **Chat name suggestions (CB-292).** An unnamed chat, or one whose content has drifted from its name, gets a content-based suggestion. A reviewer accepts, rejects or customizes it inline; a reject cools down re-suggestions for K messages so a settled name isn't re-litigated. One pending suggestion per chat at a time. New `chat_name_suggestions` table (additive migration).

- **A socio can edit its own SOUL (CB-330).** A socio is allowed to edit its own SOUL, where before only an admin could.

- **Homepage release cards capped at five (CB-333).** The What's-new cards on the homepage cap at five and show a see-all link, so a long release history no longer pushes the page.

- **Browser tab title follows the page (CB-305).** The tab title is now the page title followed by the app title, instead of a static app title.

- **The mobile sidebar toggle responds promptly (CB-332).** The toggle no longer waits or double-fires on a double-tap.

- **Admins decide who can create Spaces.** A new **Spaces** card in Settings, General has the switch "Members can create Spaces". It is on by default, so nothing changes until an admin turns it off. With it off, only admins and operators can create a Space, from the sidebar or through a socio's `create_space`, and the sidebar shows the control only to people the server allows.

- **The phone header fits the title.** Below the tablet width, every page's header shows the title, one status indicator and one menu ("More options") that holds search, Ask a socio, Report issue, pause and resume, the computer, the chat or room menu, and the Help items. The desktop header is unchanged.

- **The native mobile app has started (apps/mobile).** A new workspace member holds the approved plan's first building blocks: the app shell and root gate, the theme and primitives generated from the web colors, the local store, the network layer, workspace address checks, staged sign-in, the shell snapshot sync engine and a minimum-version gate. It is not shipped to anyone yet, and the server and web images do not include it.

### Changed

- **Learning's Analyze now is hidden behind a flag, off by default.** It has never worked, so the button no longer shows and `POST /api/learning/containers/:id/analyze` answers 404 while the new flag `learning-analyze-now` is off. This hides it; it doesn't fix it. The daily analysis and the 15-conversation threshold run as before, and a running analysis can still be canceled. An admin can turn it back on under **Settings → Features** (or `FEATURE_FLAGS=learning-analyze-now=on`), which brings the old behavior back unchanged.

- **DomainsService is built from ModuleContext, not the Platform constructor (CB-314).** The server's domain wiring no longer hangs off a single Platform constructor; each domain builds from its ModuleContext. Internal refactor; no behavior change for users.

- **Client split into modules (CB-317).** Coding components move into a `coding/` module, and the Page and ReviewedPage types move to a shared page-library. Internal refactor; no behavior change.

- **The server runs on Effect 4.** The server's wiring moved from one large `Platform` object onto Effect 4 (pinned to 4.0.5): each feature provides its services as layers, the composition root builds them into one graph, and a single `ManagedRuntime` in `main.ts` runs it. The `Platform` class and its compatibility shims are gone, and a guard fails the build on new cross-feature cycles, on `Effect.run*` outside the composition root, and on any second `ManagedRuntime`. Internal change; no behavior change is intended for users.

- **The code is split into packages.** Shared code is `@socio/shared`, the React client is `@socio/client`, and the lowest server layer is `@socio/foundation` and `@socio/data`. Builds and tests run through a cached Turborepo task graph, in CI too.

- **Database migrations run in one boot step.** Migrations no longer run from constructors; the server migrates once at startup before anything opens the database. No behavior change for a running server.

- **Image builds move to GitHub Actions on tag push (release-images.yml).** Socio images are now built off-host on tag push and published to `ghcr.io/cbnsndwch/<generic-name>` as multi-platform manifest lists (amd64 now), retiring the local-build, push-nothing model. The runtime composes pin the index digest. See `.github/workflows/release-images.yml`.

### Fixed

- **The Releases page loads as you scroll.** It loads ten releases at a time instead of all of them at once, with a Load more button for the keyboard and Try again when a page fails. A link to one release still opens it.

- **The socio-coding image builds again.** Its build context now includes `packages/`, and the install stages copy `pnpm-workspace.yaml`.

- **The Grok Build stage assertions are scoped (CB-326).** The coding-sandbox test's Grok Build assertions are scoped to the grok-build and final stages, which CB-326's session-host image stages had broken.

- **The model-free UI smoke runs in CI (CB-316).** A built server with mock auth and a scripted fake model runs the no-model smoke scenarios in CI, failing the run on any smoke failure.

### Upgrading

- **Connected agents that should edit pages need `pages:write`.** Existing grants keep the scopes they were approved with, and refreshing a token does not add any. The client has to request `pages:write` and the person has to approve that connection again. No migration.

- **Two additive migrations.** `chat_name_suggestions` (CB-292) and `page_tags`/`page_links` (CB-328) are new tables; both are expand-only and safe to apply on startup. Existing rows are unchanged.

- **Images come from GHCR.** From this release, deployments pull `ghcr.io/cbnsndwch/company-brain:vX.Y.Z@sha256:<index-digest>` (and `-apps`, `-coding-git`, `socio-coding`, `-supervisor`) instead of locally-built images. The first deployment to switch over repoints its compose to the published index digests.

## v0.32.0 (2026-11-02)

A coding session can run its agent through a session host that streams its work back live, and the coding image now carries that host. It stays dark behind `CODING_HOST_MODE` (off by default).

- The coding session-host loop, with live transcript and steer/rollback/fork (CB-326, dark)
- The session-host entry baked into the `socio-coding` image (CB-326)
- Faster, CPU-limited image builds with BuildKit cache mounts (CB-330, CB-331)

### New

- **The coding session-host loop (CB-326, dark).** A coding session can run its agent through a session host — a process in the sandbox that drives the agent (Codex, today) over `codex app-server`, streams its thread events back to Socio, and lets the person watch it work live, approve its requests, steer, interrupt, roll back to a turn, or fork from one. The session card shows a live transcript of the agent's steps with Interrupt, Steer, Roll back and Fork controls; approvals stay as Socio's approval cards. The person and admins can view the session's logged gateway exchanges (every model request and response, metadata and content hashes) and export them as JSON Lines. A host-backed session keeps its sandbox for up to 4 hours while idle (the host stops at 30 min idle and resumes on the next prompt). Dark behind `CODING_HOST_MODE` (off by default); the one-shot CLI exec path stays the live one until the host is on at release.

### Upgrading

- **The session-host entry needs the coding image (CB-326).** `CODING_HOST_MODE` is off by default, so nothing changes until the operator turns it on. This release bakes the session-host entry into the `socio-coding` image and swaps the supervisor's `HOST_PLACEHOLDER` for the real argv (`node --import /usr/local/lib/socio/node_modules/tsx/dist/loader.mjs /usr/local/lib/socio/session-host.js <sessionId> <kind> [model]`), so an operator can turn the host on by setting `CODING_HOST_MODE=1` and rebuilding the sandbox image. The host covers Codex only today; the other coding agents (Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Grok Build) keep the one-shot path. No database migration to run (the `coding_thread_events` and `gateway_exchanges` tables are expand-only and dark).

## v0.31.0 (2026-11-02)

Connected agents can now create, edit and delete pages in Socio, the same way they already manage issues.

- Create, edit and delete pages through the agent MCP server

### New

- **Create, edit and delete pages through the agent MCP server.** Connected agents (Claude Code, Cursor, …) granted the `pages:write` scope can now create, edit and delete pages in any Space the person they act for can read — the same way they already manage issues. Edits are revision-guarded (`expectedRevision`) so two agents don't clobber each other; deletes follow the same rule as the web (the creator or an admin). Everything is recorded as the person's, as with all agent tools. A self-hosted-model research agent can now keep its notes in Socio pages.

## v0.30.0 (2026-11-01)

Long scheduled conversations no longer stall the server, pages can have covers, and admins can check their recovery keys. Unused parts (dispatch and the standalone browser) are gone, and a deployment can share a swarm with another.

- Scheduled turns on long threads no longer restart the server (CB-318)
- Page covers, from your uploads or the Space's images (CB-308)
- Recovery keys you can verify, and a re-seal of old data (CB-322)
- Dispatch and the standalone browser are removed (CB-325, CB-319)
- Deployment-prefixed names for a shared swarm (CB-327)

### Breaking

- **The standalone browser service is retired (CB-319).** `WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER=browser` now stops startup with a message: use `parallel`, `searxng` or `disabled`; socios read rendered pages with their computer's browser. `BROWSER_URL` and `BROWSER_SECRET` are no longer read.
- **Dispatch is removed (CB-325).** Sending issues to coding agents (Claude, Codex, the socio executor) from the board, its runs and its settings are gone, with the `dispatch` worker, its `egress` proxy and the dispatch gateway overlay. Coding sessions are the one coding-agent feature. The GitHub App connection stays, now under Settings → General → GitHub App.

### New

- **Page covers (CB-308).** Editors add a cover to a page from their uploads or from images already in the Space, change it or remove it (with Undo). The library shows covers on its cards once a Space has any.
- **Secrets for workspace apps (Socio Secrets, phase 1).** Admins set API keys and other secrets for promoted apps in Settings → Secrets: write-only, versioned with an overlap window when replaced, granted per app (or owned by one app), sealed with the data key and audited on every change and read. Apps declare `secrets` in their manifest and read them with their data token (`GET /api/workload/secrets/:name`, or the SDK's `secrets.get`, `require` and `onRotate`); an app missing a required one shows "Needs setup". Socios and development apps get none yet (plugins: phase 2, below).
- **Secrets for plugins, and an identity per plugin (Socio Secrets, phase 2).** Each plugin service has its own identity token, minted by Socio and delivered by the apps runtime (on Swarm, a secret it creates through the socket proxy and replaces on every provision), so a call or a secret read is attributed to exactly one plugin. Plugin manifests declare `secrets` like apps'; Settings → Secrets lists plugins with the same needs-setup, grant and set-its-own actions; plugins read with `socio.secrets` unchanged. Admins can rotate a plugin's identity (`POST /api/plugins/:id/identity`).
- **A secret's history, read flags and a rotate button (Socio Secrets, phase 4).** On Settings → Secrets, **History** on a secret shows its versions, each app's or plugin's reads (first, last, how many, refused) and its events page by page, with an app's or plugin's first read as an event of its own; names only, never a value. Three conservative rules flag unusual reads for admins (a read rate far above the workload's own baseline, repeated reads after a revoke, a plugin using a stale identity), listed under **Unusual reads** with why each fired and sent once to every admin as a notice; dismissing keeps the record. Each enabled plugin has a **Rotate identity** button.
- **Recovery keys you can check (CB-322).** Service setup shows the data keys' ids (fingerprints, never the keys) and how many stored values are still sealed under the key from `SESSION_SECRET`, per kind, with **Re-seal under the data key** to move them now; `/healthz` adds `dataKeyId`, `dataKeyPreviousId` and `legacySealed`. `key-id-cli` prints a key's id offline from stdin, to check an escrowed copy. **Verify a recovery key** (and `backup-cli verify-key`) restores the latest point and opens a sample of every kind of sealed data with a key supplied from escrow, reporting each kind; the key is never stored, logged or echoed.
- **A warning when the server falls behind (CB-323).** The Updates card warns, and operators get one notice per newer release, when the newest release came out more than `UPDATES_STALE_DAYS` (90, changeable on the card; `0` turns it off) after the running one, and right away when a newer release's summary is marked `(security)`. It is only a warning: nothing updates on its own.
- **Operations sees the whole deployment (CB-321).** Health adds **Deployment services**: CPU and memory of every running container of the deployment's stacks, plugin services and socios' computers, as totals against the sum of memory limits and the node, and per service against its limit, with restarts and a "no limit" flag, kept with the resource samples' retention. It's read through the read-only disk usage proxy (a new numbers-only `GET /deployment/stats`: label-filtered container lists and per-container stats, never inspect, logs or exec). The host-wide panel is now labelled as such. Without `COMPUTER_DISK_USAGE_URL`, or when the read fails, the dashboard says the deployment metrics are unavailable and shows what it has.
- **Codex and Claude Code for coding sessions (CB-326).** A socio can start a coding session with Codex or Claude Code instead of OpenCode (`coding_start`'s `harness`; OpenCode stays the default). All three reach models only through Socio's model gateway with the session's own credential, never a vendor sign-in, so listed models, budgets and limits apply; the sandbox's network is unchanged. The card names the agent and says it runs on Socio's gateway. Claude Code can't run on a person's own plan. Settings → Coding → Coding agents lists each agent with its route, its switch (`CODING_HARNESSES_DISABLED`) and its terms record. The gateway now serves the Messages API to coding sessions (`/v1/messages`), for Claude Code.
- **Gemini CLI and Grok Build for coding sessions (CB-326).** Two more coding agents, `gemini-cli` (Gemini CLI) and `grok` (Grok Build, xAI's official coding agent), on Socio's model gateway like the others: no Google or xAI sign-in, the session's own credential, listed models, budgets and limits, and no change to the sandbox's network. For Gemini CLI the gateway now serves the Gemini API's `generateContent` and `streamGenerateContent` to coding sessions (function declarations only; Google Search, code execution, URL context and stored files are refused); Grok Build uses the Responses API, as it does against xAI. Neither can run on a person's own plan. Both are in Settings → Coding → Coding agents with their terms records (not checked yet) and can be turned off with `CODING_HARNESSES_DISABLED`.

### Changed

- **Coding agents' pins follow the owner's decisions (CB-326).** Claude Code follows npm's `stable` channel (2.1.309) instead of `latest`. Grok Build is compiled from its Apache-2.0 source at a pinned commit in the coding sandbox image instead of downloaded as xAI's unchecked binary, and runs on the gateway's Responses API, the one it uses with xAI's own models.
- **The updater deploys through a platform port (CB-323).** Deploy a tag, wait until healthy, roll back: Portainer is its adapter, chosen by `UPDATER_PLATFORM=portainer` (the default and only value so far). Existing updater settings and its Portainer key work unchanged. The updater supports Docker Swarm only for now: Portainer today, other platforms later, no Kubernetes.
- **Analytics runs as its own service (CB-320).** `deployment/portainer/analytics.overlay.yml` is the stack's analytics service and the default for every stack, so DuckDB can't take CPU or memory from the app. `OPS_ANALYTICS_MODE=child` stays for development.
- **Names for a shared swarm (CB-327).** `PLUGIN_NAME_PREFIX` (apps runtime and socket proxy, `socio-plugin` by default) prefixes every plugin service, network and volume; `PLUGIN_SERVICE_TOKEN_SECRET` and `APP_BROWSER_SECRET_SWARM_NAME` name those Swarm secrets. Defaults are unchanged.
- **The code style changed (formatting stage 1):** 4 spaces, 120 columns, sorted import groups. Rebase open branches once, then run `pnpm run format`.

### Fixed

- **Scheduled turns on long threads no longer stall the server (CB-318).** Rebuilding a thread's messages replayed its whole history on the main thread, copying the conversation after every event; on one deployment that blocked the server for over 100 s twice an hour, and the healthcheck restarted it. Replays now run in a worker thread, and each thread's last result is kept, so the next turn replays only what was added. `SOCIO_REPLAY=inline` keeps the old in-process replay (the tests use it).
- **Operations health agrees across daylight saving changes (CB-324).** The memory reader and the analytics store bucketed the repeated or skipped hour differently.
- **A release commit keeps `package.json` formatted** (4 spaces), so `pnpm run check` passes after `release`.

### Upgrading

- **To use Codex or Claude Code (CB-326),** rebuild the coding sandbox image (`node scripts/build-images.mjs --only socio-coding`, which now also pins Codex 0.166.0 and Claude Code 2.1.309, npm's `stable` channel, from `deployment/coding/codex.pin` and `claude-code.pin`), set `CODING_SANDBOX_IMAGE` to the new image ID, and rebuild the supervisor image (it builds their commands). Run the live checks in deployment/coding/README.md first. Until then, sessions with them fail at their first prompt; OpenCode sessions are unaffected. Each agent needs a listed model whose backend speaks its API: Responses for Codex, Messages for Claude Code.
- **To use Gemini CLI or Grok Build (CB-326),** rebuild the coding sandbox image, which now also pins Gemini CLI 0.66.0 (`deployment/coding/gemini-cli.pin`, from npm) and builds Grok Build 1.0.45 from its Apache-2.0 source at the commit `grok.pin` names (a multi-stage Rust build that takes a while and several GB of memory on the build node; nothing comes from `x.ai`), set `CODING_SANDBOX_IMAGE` to the new image ID, and rebuild the supervisor image. Run their live checks in deployment/coding/README.md first. Gemini CLI needs a listed model whose backend speaks the Gemini API (its base URL ending in `/v1beta` for Google's); Grok Build one whose backend speaks the Responses API (xAI's does).
- **Rename the settings dispatch left behind (CB-325).** `DISPATCH_CODE_USERS` is now `CODE_USERS`, and `DISPATCH_GITHUB_APP_ID`, `DISPATCH_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` (and `_FILE`), `DISPATCH_GITHUB_INSTALLATION_ID` and `DISPATCH_GITHUB_REPO` are now `GITHUB_*`. For this release the old names are still read when the new ones are unset, with a one-line deprecation warning at start; the next release drops them. `DISPATCH_GITHUB_BASE` and the other `DISPATCH_*`, `CLAUDE_*` and `CODEX_*` settings are no longer read.
- **Deployment metrics (CB-321).** Rebuild the supervisor image (`--only supervisor`, which carries `disk-usage-proxy.mjs`), then update `company-brain-computers`: `disk-usage` gains `SOCIO_STACKS` (default `company-brain,company-brain-computers,company-brain-updater`; a deployment with other stack names must set it), `PLUGIN_NAME_PREFIX` (match the plugins overlay) and `DISK_USAGE_NODE`. The app's service now has a limit, `APP_CPUS` (2) and `APP_MEMORY` (3G). Plugin tasks carry their labels on the container once the apps runtime writes their services again. No migration: the samples use the existing table.
- **Plugin identities (Socio Secrets, phase 2).** Rebuild and update `app` and `apps` together (the image that runs `docker-socket-proxy` too). Nothing to create: at start, Socio gives every enabled plugin service its own identity and updates its service. `PLUGIN_SERVICE_TOKEN` is deprecated (read this release with a warning, never for secret reads), `PLUGIN_SERVICE_TOKEN_SECRET` is no longer read, and a manifest's `service.secrets` still mounts with a warning; `SOCKET_PROXY_SECRET_NAMES`/`APPS_RUNTIME_SECRET_NAMES` default to empty. Keep rendering `plugin_service_token` on `app` this release; the next one stops reading it, and then the IaC side drops it (handoff `2026-11-01-socio-to-iac-plugin-identities.md`). A `file:` catalog plugin moves its `service.secrets` to declared `secrets` and reads `SOCIO_IDENTITY_FILE` (docs/SETUP.md, Secrets for workspace apps and plugins).
- **Secret history and read flags (Socio Secrets, phase 4).** Two expand-only migrations run on their own at start: workspace 20 indexes the audit by kind and time, and workspace-secrets 2 adds `workspace_secret_flags`. Nothing to configure.
- **Remove what the stack no longer names (CB-325)**, once this release is deployed: the `company-brain-dispatch` volume and images, and the `company_brain_dispatch_token`, `company_brain_anthropic_api_key`, `company_brain_claude_oauth_token` and `company_brain_codex_api_key` secrets (deployment/portainer/README.md). No migration: the dispatch tables stay, unread, until a later release drops them; the GitHub App's saved connection is kept.

## v0.29.0 (2026-10-31)

A calmer, more readable interface across Home, the board, Spaces, Runs and Settings; settings that save as you change them; and a fix for the stalls that made the server restart every half hour.

- A refreshed interface: one Help menu, a graphite dark theme, Home with your socios' state, and quieter board cards with colored priority (CB-308)
- Settings save as you change them, with Undo (CB-308)
- Long scheduled turns no longer freeze the server; stalls are now logged with what blocked them (CB-318)
- Socios' computers reach only the data API, and only the proxy may set the client address (CB-309)

### New

- **Settings save as you change them (CB-308).** Workspace, Coding, Branding, Transcription, Calls and dispatch settings no longer have Save buttons. Each card shows Saving…, then Saved, and a toast offers Undo; number and text fields save when you leave them. Settings with side effects say what happened in the toast. Keys save on Enter or when you leave the field, and are never shown again or offered for Undo. Adding a repository or a model, turning on push, and clearing or replacing a key stay explicit actions.
- **Main-thread stalls are logged, with what blocked them (CB-318).** Any stall over a second writes a log line with its length and the work running. The shutdown log says which signal stopped the app, after how long, and the last stall. When the healthcheck fails, it captures the blocked thread's JavaScript stack to `/data/reports` (at most one every ten minutes, the newest five kept), so the next freeze names its function.

### Changed

- **A refreshed interface (CB-308).** From the CB-307 design review:
    - The shell has one Help menu (Help and guides, What's new, Keyboard shortcuts, Theme), the version and a connection dot in the account row, a one-row passkey nudge, and Pause as a text button. The getting-started guide opens from Help.
    - Home leads with a Home title and real counts, socio cards with each socio's state, and the apps and At a glance as single cards with their colored module tiles.
    - The board has a quieter filter row (won't fix moved into the Status menu), status rings and counts on columns, cards with a colored priority pill for every level, and a violet dispatch bar. An issue opens with its ID, status and votes on one line.
    - Space libraries drop the generic page icon and no longer repeat a page's title in its excerpt.
    - Runs show one date format, status as a dot and a word, the trace as a row that expands, and one token format.
    - A socio's home with nothing going on is one line instead of empty panels.
    - The dark theme moves to a graphite ramp; section labels are in sentence case and titles are lighter.
- **Agent turns no longer copy the whole conversation on every streamed event (CB-318).** A long thread's scheduled turn spent over a minute of the server's time on these copies, long enough to fail the healthcheck and get the app replaced every 30 minutes. Server-side turns now pass events straight to the runner.
- **Faster tooling (CB-311).** Linting and formatting moved from ESLint and Prettier to oxlint and oxfmt, and type checking to TypeScript 7.1 (native). `pnpm run check` runs all three in parallel in about 7 seconds, down from over two minutes. See `docs/TOOLING.md`.
- **Licensing and attribution are cbnsndwch LLC's (CB-312),** with OpenDots credited in `LICENSE` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`, both of which now ship in every image.

### Fixed

- **Socios' computers reach only the data API, and only the proxy may set the client address (CB-309).** With `COMPUTERS_SUBNET` set, requests from the computers network reach only the data API, health and actor routes. With `TRUSTED_PROXY_SUBNETS` set, `X-Forwarded-For` counts only from the proxy's network, so the address that sign-in rate limits count can't be spoofed from elsewhere. Both are optional: unset, the app behaves as before and logs a warning.

### Upgrading

- Set `COMPUTERS_SUBNET` and `TRUSTED_PROXY_SUBNETS` per deployment (`docker network inspect`; see `deployment/portainer/company-brain.env.example`).
- The app's healthcheck is now `node dist/server/server/healthcheck.js` (Dockerfile and `company-brain.yml`), and the image sets `NODE_OPTIONS=--inspect-port=127.0.0.1:9339`. The inspector listens only while a failed check captures a stack, on the container's loopback.

## v0.28.1 (2026-10-30)

Capability requests now link to the issue they file, so an inline card renders in chat and rooms.

- request_capability relays an issue link, so the inline card renders in chat and rooms (CB-306)

### Fixed

- **Capability requests now link to the issue they file, so an inline card renders in chat and rooms (CB-306).** `request_capability` relayed the filed item as plain `CB-N` text, but the inline issue card only renders for issue links — so a user filing a request in a chat or room saw no card, just literal text. The tool result now emits a Markdown link (`[CB-N](/issues/CB-N)`) in its filed, merged, and already-requested messages, so the socio relays a link and the card appears in any chat or room (the same path direct chats already used for explicit links).

## v0.28.0 (2026-10-30)

Voice can start a new chat, a Space finding can become a follow-up, and each Space gets a change radar.

- Voice or a voice call as the very first message of a new chat (CB-302)
- Turn a Space page into a board issue, schedule, or goal with a citation back to the source (CB-281)
- A Space Radar tab showing recent changes, open decisions, and stale pages (CB-282)

### New

- **Voice as the first message of a new chat (CB-302).** A new chat's composer now offers the same voice-call button a running chat has, gated on the same setup flags. Tapping it creates the conversation and starts the call on it, so your spoken turn is the first message — no placeholder text needed first. Dictation into the prompt was already available as a first message (CB-266); this adds a voice call. Rooms (CB-205) keep their own composer path and are a follow-up.
- **Create a follow-up from a Space finding (CB-281).** A useful page in a Space can now be turned into a board issue, a recurring schedule, or a goal from the page's ⋯ menu. "Create follow-up" opens a review dialog prefilled with the page's title and an excerpt, plus a citation link back to the page (`From [Title](/spaces/…/pages/…)`), so the destination carries its source. Edit the fields, pick a destination and confirm — nothing is created until you do. Issues file into triage through the existing report flow; schedules bind to the page's conversation through the existing task flow; goals ask the page's specialist to set one, reusing the existing goal-set approval. Destinations are gated by what the page can reach: a specialist in the Space for schedules and goals, inference set up for schedules, and any signed-in person for issues.
