- **Space change radar (CB-282).** A new Radar tab on each Space shows a project change radar over its existing pages: recent changes (newest first, with a kept-revision count), open decisions/experiments flagged by simple title/content markers (todo, decision, experiment, tbd, unresolved…), and potentially stale pages older than the Space's activity threshold. Every entry links to its page with a clear timestamp. The view is opt-in (`/spaces/:id/radar`), read-gated the same way pages are, and supports a subtree scope (`?root=<pageId>`). Dismissals ("not stale" / "resolved") are client-only for the session — nothing is written.

## v0.27.0 (2026-10-30)

A faster, lighter iOS PWA, a new way for socios to edit board items, and the workspace brand now reaches the favicon in the initial page.

- iOS PWA startup is leaner, with a built-in performance recorder in Settings (CB-299)
- Socios can edit the title and description of Improvements board items (CB-286)
- The workspace brand favicon is now in the server-rendered HTML, not only after hydration (CB-300)

### New

- **Socios can edit Improvements board items (CB-286).** A socio acting for a person may edit the title and/or description of an item: members edit their own items while they are in triage, and admins edit any item. The edit is attributed to the person through the same dot-action records as filing, commenting and voting.
- **A built-in browser performance recorder (CB-299).** Settings → Account → Performance offers an opt-in, tab-local recording across SPA navigation: request and resource timing with compressed/decoded sizes, server processing time, navigation duration, slow interactions, and a frame-gap fallback for Safari. It runs for up to ten minutes, keeps the latest 500 entries, exports a diagnostics JSON without credentials, query strings or content bodies, and clears on sign-out. Reload resumes the remaining consent window and starts a fresh sample.
- **Leaner iOS PWA startup (CB-299).** The chat framework is no longer loaded globally before sign-in: CopilotKit moves into the lazy chat runtime, so its SDK doesn't load on pages that never open a chat. Only the selected mascot's artwork loads, animations cap at 30 updates/sec, and the four static characters skip animation loops entirely. Chat Markdown is memoized so unrelated renders don't repeat parsing. Initial bundle size and main-thread work on a cold, signed-out page are materially reduced.

### Fixed

- **The workspace brand favicon now ships in the server-rendered HTML (CB-300).** v0.26.1 set the tab title and favicon only after hydration, so the built-in `/favicon.svg` still served on first paint and reload. The favicon is now emitted by route `meta` — the shell renders the workspace brand logo, and the built-in is the fallback for the docs frame and error pages — so the correct icon appears in the initial HTML regardless of logo type (SVG, PNG, JPEG, WebP path or uploaded data URL).

## v0.26.2 (2026-10-30)

A quick fix for the intermittent multi-second "Sending to X" delay.

- Chat turns start faster by running pre-turn extension hooks in parallel (CB-301)

### Fixed

- **Chat turns no longer block serially on extension hooks before the model starts (CB-301).** `pageContext`, `news`, and `turnNote` now run concurrently, so one slow hook cannot keep the client waiting in the "Sending to X" state. If the stall persists, deeper timings around inference resolution will come next.

## v0.26.1 (2026-10-30)

A small polish release: the workspace brand now shows in the browser tab, and an audit test no longer flakes on a random UUID.

- Workspace logo and name now set the tab title and favicon (CB-300)
- Web-reads audit test ignores the random UUID id when checking for leaked secrets (CB-296)

### Fixed

- **Changing instance branding now updates the browser tab title and favicon (CB-300).** The workspace logo and name already updated the sidebar and sign-in screen; they now also set `document.title` and the favicon link so the tab reflects the current brand after a reload or a settings change.
- **Web-reads audit test no longer flakes when a UUID contains `abc` (CB-296).** The leak check now ignores the random `id` field and guards only deterministic/redacted fields, so UUID entropy cannot trip the assertion.

## v0.26.0 (2026-10-30)

Rooms now show the work a socio does in a turn — its thinking and tool calls — the same way a private chat does, while keeping every other member's view private. Inviting and mentioning people in a room got faster too: an `/invite` slash command and socio-aware @mention autocomplete join the composer.

- Room transcripts show per-round thinking traces and tool calls, with the actor's own view private to them (CB-283)
- `/invite` in the room composer invites people without leaving the conversation (CB-285)
- @mention autocomplete in rooms now includes active socios alongside people, routing a mention to the named socio (CB-284)

### New

- Rooms show each socio turn's thinking traces and tool calls in the transcript, with the same privacy the live stream keeps: the acting person sees their own reasoning and results, other members get reasoning emptied and tool results as a withheld note (CB-283)
- A `/invite` slash command in the room composer invites people or socios as admins do in Members, with the room's existing authorization (CB-285)
- The room composer's @mention autocomplete now includes active socios alongside people, each labeled by kind; picking a socio inserts @Name and routes the message to it (CB-284)

## v0.25.6 (2026-10-29)

A small polish release for Rooms avatars and the room header.

- Room headers now show every active socio and make the global Pause scope clear (CB-293)
- Flake and Iggy avatars no longer carry conflicting baked-in backgrounds (CB-295)

### Changed

- Recovered the stranded `cb110-w3-apps` branch: the CB-110 apps bounded-context repositories (apps, sessions, grants, versions and attempts) it carried were already on main via the sibling `cb110-w3-pages-data-apps` merge, and have since been evolved by CB-232 (socio-actor access). The merge records the recovered branch; no migration, no visible change. (CB-110)
- Recovered the stranded `feat/cb250-custom-mcp` branch: its seven commits (personal external MCP connections, OAuth consent, tool review and per-call approval, disconnection and CLI deactivation revocation) were already on main via an earlier cherry-picked path with different SHAs, and shipped in v0.20.0. The merge connects the branch history; conflicts resolved with main's version. No migration, no visible change. (CB-250)

### Fixed

- **Room headers now show all participating socios and make the global Pause control clearer (CB-293).** The room header no longer shows only the lead socio's avatar; it stacks the active socios that answer there and lists their names in the status line. The app-wide Pause/Resume button now reads "Pause all socios" / "Resume all socios", and the room status line notes when all socios are paused. Breadcrumb intermediate items no longer grow unpredictably, removing the unexplained gap some routes showed.
- **Flake and Iggy avatars no longer carry conflicting baked-in backgrounds (CB-295).** Both Miami-native Locals now have transparent backgrounds, so the app's location- and theme-matched surfaces show behind them instead of a duplicate sunset or foliage backdrop.

## v0.25.5 (2026-10-29)

A quick fix for an unexpected side effect of adding new character options.

- Existing socios keep their character assignment when the roster grows (CB-294)

### Fixed

- **Existing socios keep their character assignment when the roster grows (CB-294).** Migration 19 backfills `dots.character` using the pre-expansion four-Local roster for socios that never had a character chosen, so adding new Locals no longer shifts the hash-derived look of existing socios. New socios now store their resolved character on creation, making future roster expansions stable for them too.

## v0.25.4 (2026-10-29)

Telegram ingress hardened with signed replay proofs and schema-gated reprime.

- Telegram ingress replay protection and schema-gated reprime (CB-240)

### Fixed

- **Telegram ingress now rejects replayed messages across schema upgrades and after the bounded dedup window (CB-240).** Each inbound Telegram message gets a signed proof that binds it to the exact stored message. Reprimes use schema-gated validation so an older provenance key cannot be replayed against a newer schema version; the dedup map is bounded and TTL'd.

## v0.25.3 (2026-10-29)

Eight Miami-native character options are now in the picker, and creating a room lets you bring in several socios and people up front.

- Four new Locals: Lino, Flake, Iggy, Rosy (CB-272)
- Pick several socios and people, and add a description, when creating a room (CB-278)

### New

- **Four new Miami-native character options: Lino the rooster, Flake the dolphin, Iggy the iguana, and Rosy the roseate spoonbill (CB-272).** They ship as static avatars for now; animation parity with the original four Locals comes later. The character picker offers all eight, and a socio's chosen Local (or the one its id hashes to) shows in the chat header and notifications.

### Changed

- **Creating a room now lets you pick several company socios and invite people up front, and add a description (CB-278).** The New room dialog adds an optional "More socios" multi-select (each starts as a request a workspace admin approves), an optional "Invite people" multi-select from the workspace, and an optional description / purpose textarea. The lead socio is still picked first; the creator's own membership is implicit (admin). The description textarea scrolls into view when focused, fixing the mobile PWA issue where the virtual keyboard covered it.

## v0.25.2 (2026-10-29)

Node 26 plugin data route fix

- Fixes every bundled plugin's data routes (the calendar's /state, /events, etc.) which returned 500 on Node 26: undici reads the duplex getter on the raw Hono request passed as new Request's init, and the brand check throws Illegal invocation. The data route now builds a plain RequestInit from the raw request's parts, with duplex half when there is a body

### Fixed

- **Bundled plugin data routes (calendar, social-strategy) no longer return 500 on Node 26 (CB-203).** Node 26's undici reads every property of the `RequestInit` passed as the second argument to `new Request()`. The plugin data route forwarded the raw Hono request (`c.req.raw`, a `@hono/node-server` `Request$1`) as the init, and reading its `duplex` getter fails a brand check ("Illegal invocation") on Node 26 — breaking every bundled plugin's data routes with a 500. The route now builds a plain `RequestInit` from the raw request's method, headers and body, with `duplex: 'half'` when there is a body. Rebuild the app image from this tag.

## v0.25.1 (2026-10-29)

The sidebar is now organized into collapsible sections: workspace apps have their own Apps section, bottom links are grouped by function, and every section can be expanded or collapsed. Socio also moves to Node 26.13.0, the latest LTS.

- Sidebar organization with collapsible sections (CB-276)
- Move to Node 26.13.0 LTS (CB-203)

### Upgrading

- **Socio moves to Node 26.13.0, the latest LTS (CB-203).** Every image built from this repository (app, browser, dispatch, apps runtime, coding sandbox) and CI's image now use `node:26.13.0-bookworm-slim`, pinned by digest, instead of 24.22.1; rebuild them all from the release's tag. The Dockerfiles install `corepack` with `npm install -g corepack` first, because Node 26 dropped corepack from the base image. In a checkout, use Node 26.13.0 (`nvm use` reads `.nvmrc`): package.json's engines now ask for `>=26.13.0 <27`, and an older Node fails the install. Node 26 ("Lithium") became LTS on 2026-10-28.

### Changed

- **Sidebar sections: workspace apps have their own Apps section, bottom links are grouped by function, and every section can be collapsed.** Workspace apps and installed plugins live under an **Apps** section; Telegram sits under **Integrations**, Settings & setup under **Settings**, Operations and Platform under **Platform**, and Help under **Help**. Collapse state is saved per person in this browser's `localStorage` (CB-276).

## v0.25.0 (2026-10-29)

You can now delete a Space you no longer need and restore it later; Rooms shows clearer notices and fixes, and buttons, pending messages and menus read better in light and dark. Coding-session and coding-gateway work is included but stays off by default.

- Delete a Space, and restore it (CB-277)
- Rooms follow-ups: removed-member notice, immediate mute, touch-friendly buttons, composer placeholder
- Readable buttons, pending messages and menus, in light and dark (CB-219 follow-up)
- Coding sessions in the Computer panel and coding gateway, off by default (CB-239, CB-267, CB-232)

### New

- **Delete a Space, and restore it (CB-277).** An editor of a private Space, or a workspace admin for any Space, can delete it from the Space's **Settings** tab, after a dialog that names what goes with it (its pages, page and Ask chats, shared memory, app, dataset and link-card shares, and socios' access) and asks for the Space's name. It is gone for everyone at once: the sidebar, lists, search, the ⌘K palette, socios, connected agents, apps and datasets shared with it, embedded views and link cards all answer as if it never existed, and apps shared with it drop their open connections. Nothing is erased: admins see it under **Settings → Deleted Spaces**, with who deleted it and when, and **Restore** brings everything back; it is kept until an admin restores it. Both are in the workspace audit. A Space a socio saves new pages to by default, and the workspace's last Space, can't be deleted; during a deploy, deleting waits until the previous release has stopped (a 409 says to try again in a few minutes). Socios have no tool to delete a Space.

- **Coding sessions: your sandboxes in the Computer panel, private results in rooms, extractions delivered to apps, and plugin WebSockets (dark, CB-239, CB-267, CB-232).** A socio's Computer panel gets a **Coding** tab with your coding sessions on that socio (status, repository, branch, age and the workspace's disk) and **Stop**, which ends one as `coding_stop` does; admins see everyone's and can stop any, recorded with their name. In a room, a coding session's results stay with the person it works for: everyone else sees one neutral line, and a long prompt's report there says only that it ended. An approved repository extraction now reaches the app that asked for it: apps that declare `"accepts": ["repo-extract"]` read it once (retries for 10 minutes) through `GET /api/apps/actor/extractions/:id`, with `extraction()` in `@socio/app-sdk`. Service plugins can take WebSockets, with the same checks as their requests and closed when access ends. All of it stays off behind `coding-sessions`, `repo-extraction` and `socio-actor-access`.

- **Coding gateway: the Responses API, personal plans and dispatch through the gateway (dark, CB-239).** The model gateway now also speaks the OpenAI Responses API to coding sandboxes, with the same checks as Chat Completions: the session's own model, only plain and custom function tools (never a provider's web search, file search, code execution or MCP), no stored state upstream, nothing that names the person, metering against the same budgets. An admin can let people run their own coding sessions on their own ChatGPT plan (Settings → Coding → Personal plans, off by default): only for the person's own session in their private chat, never in a room or for anyone else; the card says it runs on their plan, and Socio holds the plan's sign-in while the sandbox gets only its session credential. And the dispatch worker's Codex and Claude can call their models through the gateway, with an Anthropic Messages route for Claude, a credential per run and dispatch's own token budgets, so the worker needs no vendor key; models are marked **Dispatch may use it** under Settings → Coding → Models. Off until an operator deploys the switch (Upgrading).

### Fixed

- **Rooms follow-ups.** A member removed from a room no longer sees "Conversation not found." when they had it open: the room keeps its own "you are no longer in this room" notice. Toggling a room to Muted shows the crossed bell in the sidebar at once, without a reload. The Pinned and Members buttons in a room's header are a comfortable 44 px wide on a touch screen, not just tall. A long lead socio name no longer wraps the composer's placeholder to a second line; it is cut to fit on one line.

- **Readable buttons, pending messages and menus, in light and dark (CB-219 follow-up).** Primary buttons are a darker shade of the Socio teal, so their words are at least 4.5:1 at rest, hovered, pressed and disabled (a disabled one is grayed rather than faded); the teal stays for rings and bars. A message that is sending, queued or being read is no longer faded: it has a dashed outline on a light tint with its words at full contrast, yours and others' alike, beside the line that says where it is. Every sidebar row is 44 px tall on a touch screen, as the Rooms rows were, and the sidebar's quieter words (a chat's socio, Show all) and grayed text elsewhere are darker. The item a menu, a select or a picker has the keyboard on gets a ring in the brand color. Room members always see how many times a message's work may pass between the room's socios, even when its use and budget are hidden from them (`GET /api/rooms/:id/limits/handoffs`). Two people in a room with the same name are numbered by when they joined ("Ana Lima · 2") in Members, the people picker, the messages, presence and the leave dialog.

### Upgrading

- **Dispatch through the model gateway is a switch, off by default (CB-239).** Nothing changes on deploy: the worker keeps `DISPATCH_MODEL_ROUTE=vendor` and its keys. To move it, list the vendors' backends and mark models **Dispatch may use it** under Settings → Coding → Models, create the `company-brain-dispatch-gateway` network, set `DISPATCH_GATEWAY_SUBNET` and deploy with `deployment/portainer/dispatch-gateway.overlay.yml` (the app gets `GATEWAY_DISPATCH_SUBNET`, the worker `DISPATCH_MODEL_ROUTE=gateway` and `DISPATCH_GATEWAY_URL`), then set the worker's vendor secrets to `none` and drop the vendors from `EGRESS_ALLOW`; deployment/actors/README.md §10 has the steps and the way back. New app settings over env defaults: `CODING_PERSONAL_PLANS` (off), `DISPATCH_GATEWAY_RUN_TOKENS` (50 M) and `DISPATCH_GATEWAY_DAILY_TOKENS` (500 M). One expand-only migration, `gateway` 5: a `dispatch` mark on coding models, `gateway_options`, `gateway_dispatch_credentials` and a `plan` column on coding sessions. The coding sandbox's managed OpenCode config gains a `socio-responses` provider: rebuild `socio-coding` when coding sessions are deployed.

## v0.24.0 (2026-10-28)

Spaces can now be private to the people you choose, each Space keeps a shared memory its editors approve, and socios remember what came out of your earlier chats. Socios can also make a Space for you once you approve it, and issues show the issues they mention as chips and can be linked as related.

- Make a private Space for yourself and the colleagues you choose: people outside it don't see it at all (CB-181)
- Keep a shared memory in each Space: anyone can propose what the team knows, its editors approve it, and socios use it in their answers (CB-181)
- Socios remember the decisions and results of your earlier chats, which you can see, search and delete under Memories (CB-180)
- Ask a socio to make a new Space for you; nothing is made until you approve it (CB-271)
- See the issues an issue mentions as chips, and link issues as related on the board (CB-274)

### New

- **Socio access for app authors: the contract test kit, the guides and the build (dark, CB-232, CB-239).** App authors get `@socio/app-sdk/check`, a test kit their CI runs to prove the app refuses forged, stale and replayed envelopes, decides each action by the tier its manifest declares rather than by HTTP method, keeps socios away from admin actions, and runs a consequential action only once, after the person approved those exact parameters. The SDK has a developer README, and admins get a **Socio access for apps** help page (adopting the contract, tiers, **always ask**, background datasets and Space write access). A socio's deletes through the data API now need the person's approval for that exact call, consumed once by Socio (no app can delete through the data API yet, so nothing changes today). Operators get `deployment/actors/README.md` for turning these features on, and opt-in image targets `app-browser`, `coding-egress`, `coding-git` and `socio-coding` (OpenCode 2.2.0, pinned by checksum, since 2.1.4 was never published; the sandbox runs from the image's local ID), and `deployment/portainer/coding.overlay.yml` for the app's side of coding sessions. Service plugins can now declare consequential actions too, each one approved by the person and run once, with continuations for their queued work, and the SDK and its kit support plugins (`CB_APP_ID=plugin:<id>`). Everything stays behind flags that are off.

- **Socios can make a Space for you (CB-271).** Ask a socio for a new Space ("make a Space called Design, then unfurl this article into it") and it proposes one with the new `create_space` tool. Nothing is made until you approve it on the approval card, which shows the Space's name, its description and who will see it; this approval can't be bypassed or left to automatic review. A member's socio makes a private Space with them as its editor, as the create dialog does; an admin's can also make one for the whole workspace. The socio is given the new Space and can add pages to it right away in your own chat with it; it uses a private Space only where everyone in the chat can see it, so not in a room with others or a Telegram group. It won't make a second Space with the name of one you already see. Connected agents can't make Spaces.

- **Socios using plugins for you (dark, CB-232).** Plugins take part in socio access the same way apps do, with one set of rules: who may use a plugin is still its install's audience (**Admins only** or **Everyone**), and a socio acting for someone needs that person's grant for the plugin, whose read or write access matches the plugin's own read and write scopes. With **Socios use apps for you** on for a person, a socio uses the calendar's tools only after asking with `request_app_access` (`app: "Calendar"`): a read grant lists events and can't add, change or delete them. Narrowing a plugin to admins, disabling or uninstalling it ends socios' grants for it at once, and a revoked grant's open plugin streams close. Service plugins get the signed `x-cb-actor` envelope (`aud: plugin:<id>`) and, when a plugin passes its `x-cb-obo` back, Socio's plugin data routes act as that person or socio instead of an admin; a plugin can declare a `delegation` contract in its manifest so socios may open it. The runtime now passes a plugin only identity headers the proxy signed, `x-cb-person` included, and gives each plugin task its envelope key (`CB_ACTOR_KEY`). The legacy `plugins:read`/`plugins:write` connection scopes still cover the social planner only. During a deploy's overlap, a plugin reached through the older app gets no identity headers until the app is updated. While the flag is off, which it is, socios use plugin tools as before; nothing to do.

- **Socios using apps for you: the apps proxy and the app SDK (dark, CB-232).** The apps proxy can now serve a socio acting for a person: it takes the app browser's short-lived credential with a per-request proof of its context, checks the grant on every request, and sends the app a signed, one-minute `x-cb-actor` envelope instead of the person headers, so an app can tell a socio from the person and never mistakes it for the owner. The app browser reaches apps through a separate internal listener (port 4312, socio sessions only, from its own network). Open responses and WebSockets of a grant close when it is revoked; consequential actions go through the person's approval card. A new SDK for app authors (`packages/app-sdk`, for Hono, Express, plain Node and fetch handlers) verifies the envelope, authorizes each action by the tier its manifest declares, asks the person to approve consequential actions and calls the data API on behalf of the request. Manifests gain an optional `delegation` block (`ownerColumn` is refused until row ownership ships); promoted apps receive their envelope key at start; the app runtime passes identity headers on only when the proxy signed them. People's sessions get the envelope too only while **Socios use apps for you** is on for them; it is in development and off, so nothing changes yet. A new table records each app's key epoch (apps 7, expand only); nothing to do.

- **Rooms: socios in Telegram groups, through topics linked to a room (dark, CB-214).** A room admin who is also an admin of the Telegram group links a forum topic to the room: **Telegram topic** in the room's ⋯ menu gives a one-time code, after confirming who reads the topic, which they send in the topic as `/bind@yourbot <code>` from their linked account. There, a room member who mentions or replies to the bot talks to the room's socio as themselves, and its reply is posted in the topic. Anyone else, linked or not, is told once and nothing of theirs reaches the room or another person's turn; room admins see who wasn't answered, and people seen in the group, to invite. Existing team topics are unchanged, their readers too, and writing in a linked topic makes no one a reader of the group's team topics; someone who reads one can make it a new room the same way, with nobody added, its old transcript staying with its current readers. Archiving the room, or **Unlink topic**, unlinks it. Groups now get Telegram's command menu: the chat skills, and `/bind` for their admins. Behind **Settings → General → Rooms**, off by default. Telegram migration 3 adds two tables (expand only).

- **Rooms: pinned facts and shared notes (CB-217).** A room has **Pinned** beside **Members**: facts its socio reads with every message, word for word. Room admins pin, edit and unpin; members propose a fact or a change, which waits for an admin; whoever pinned a fact can change or unpin it. Every change is a version with who and when, shown in each fact's history and the room's audit (never the words). A room keeps at most 30 pinned facts and 6,000 characters, so they always fit. The notes the socio keeps on a long room's earlier messages are shown there too, with each version, the turns it covered and whose turn wrote it, and their facts can be pinned. Two turns compacting a room at once no longer mix their notes: the first one's stands. Pins never hold a socio's private results or anyone's memories, and rooms still make no episodes. Chat migration 9 adds two tables (expand-only).

- **Private Spaces (CB-181).** A Space is for the whole workspace or only its people, and private now covers everything in it: its pages and their history, page chats, search, apps, datasets and link-card images shared with it, and its memory. People outside a private Space don't see it at all: not in the sidebar, search, the palette, links (a 404 like a Space that isn't there), socios' answers or connected agents' tools. Anyone can make a private Space for themselves and the colleagues they choose (the **+** beside **Spaces** is everyone's now); only workspace admins make or open a Space for the whole workspace, and they see every Space. Choose when making it or under the Space's new **Settings** tab, which also holds its people; private Spaces have a lock. A socio uses a private Space only if it was given the Space and everyone in the chat can see it (in a room, every member and everyone invited). Someone removed loses it at once, their own page chats about it included, and gets it back if added again.

- **Rooms with several socios: ask one by name, and socios hand work to each other (CB-206).** In a room with more than one socio, a message goes to the lead unless it names socios with `@` (type it, or pick one with **@** next to the message box, which says who the message goes to before you send it); several named socios answer one after another, in the order named. A socio can hand a message's work to another of the room's socios with a reason, which the room reads as a line between the replies, and each reply shows which socio wrote it. Every socio acts as the person who sent the message, with their access and approvals, within the same limits: one message's work may pass between socios at most **Handoffs per message** times (4 by default, Settings → General → Work per message; a room admin may lower it for their room under Members), and never twice from the same socio to the same socio, so socios can't bounce work back and forth. Stop ends the reply and whatever was to follow. Rooms stay behind **Rooms** (off by default). A new table is added at start; nothing to do.

- **Socios using apps for you: the data API checks whose request it is (dark, CB-232).** When an app calls Socio's data API for someone, it now passes who that is, and the app gets only what that person, or the socio acting for them, may do. A socio with read access can't write through the app, even if the app asks on its behalf. An app that adopts socio access can no longer write with its own token alone, except to the datasets it lists for its background jobs. Deleting data on a socio's behalf is refused until per-action approvals land. Apps that haven't adopted socio access work as before. Nothing changes for anyone yet: it sits behind the **Socios use apps for you** flag, which is off, and no app can adopt socio access until the apps phase lands. Each server now records access contract 2, so during the first deploy with this change no socio access is granted until the older server has stopped.

- **Coding sessions: the model gateway (dark, CB-239).** The part of coding sessions that lets a socio's coding agent call a model without ever holding a provider key: an OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions endpoint on a separate internal listener, reachable only from the coding network and only through its egress proxy's stamp, with a fresh credential for each prompt that ends with it. A session can use only the model on its approval card, and only models on the operator's own servers unless an admin allows an outside provider for that repository. Requests are rebuilt from known fields: nothing that names the person, no client header, no provider-side tools and no linked images reach the backend. Each request reserves its tokens against the session's budget and the person's and the socio's last 24 hours at once (there is no server-wide budget), and only counts are kept, never prompts or answers. Admins get **Settings → Coding** for the models, the outside-provider repositories, the limits over `CODING_*` defaults and the last day's usage. It stays off behind **Coding sessions** until the sandbox and the sessions themselves land.

- **Coding sessions (dark, CB-239).** A socio can now run a coding agent (OpenCode) on a listed repository for the person it acts for: `coding_start` asks on a card that shows the repository, the base branch, the session's own branch, how long, the model and where it runs (and says plainly when code would go to an outside provider), the token budget, what it can and can't do, and who sees the answers in a room; every session, read-only or not, waits for the person's click. Approving creates the session in its own sandbox, with a fresh credential for every prompt and the socio's own Git identity, never the person's. `coding_prompt` sends work and waits while the turn allows; longer work keeps running, survives a deploy, and its result comes back in the conversation. `coding_status`, `coding_diff`, `coding_open_pr` (drafts only) and `coding_stop` complete the set, and the chat shows a session card with the repository, branch, model, state, tokens used and the pull request, with the agent's progress as it works. Only the person who started a session can use it, and only from that conversation; Telegram groups and scheduled runs are refused. Sessions end when stopped, after 30 minutes without a prompt, at their time limit or budget, or when access ends; their sandbox goes with them, unused workspaces go after 14 days, and the first refused request per host is in the access audit (`CODING_EGRESS_DIAGNOSTICS_URL`). Admins set the package registries sandboxes may read and how long unused workspaces are kept under Settings → Coding → Sandboxes, over `CODING_EGRESS_ALLOW` and `CODING_WORKSPACE_RETENTION_DAYS`. Everything stays behind the **Coding sessions** flag (in development, off). New tables are added at start (expand only); nothing to do.

- **Coding sandboxes and their egress, built dark (CB-239).** The groundwork for a socio to run OpenCode on a workspace repository for one person: the `socio-coding` image (OpenCode pinned by version and checksum, Git, Node 24 with pnpm, Python 3, a managed configuration that sends models and Git to Socio's gateway), the computer supervisor's sandbox API (one container and one internal network per coding session, created only from a spec fixed in its code, inspected after every create and removed if anything differs, commands only from a fixed list with the prompt on stdin), its start-up self-test that refuses to create sandboxes if anything outside resolves or answers, and the coding egress proxy, a sandbox's only way out: the gateway with a signed sandbox stamp, and npm and PyPI download-only, without credentials and without tunnels. Nothing runs yet: the supervisor creates no sandbox until an operator sets the image's digest, the proxy's stack service has no replicas, and coding sessions stay behind the **Coding sessions** flag until their last phase lands (deployment/coding/README.md).

- **Socios using apps for you: the app browser (dark, CB-232).** A new `app-browser` service, its image stage and an optional stack overlay (not deployed), and the socio tools `app_open`, `app_snapshot`, `app_click`, `app_type`, `app_screenshot` and `app_close`. Each approved access gets its own worker process, under its own user, running one Chromium with its sandbox on, locked to that one app: every request is fetched by the worker from Socio's internal apps listener with the access's credential added there, so no credential is ever in a page, a URL or the cookie jar, and nothing else is reachable, by request, name lookup or WebRTC. The service that routes the commands can't read the credentials it relays, and refuses to start if Chromium's sandbox isn't available. Only the person an access was approved by, through the same socio and in the same conversation, can use it; Telegram groups refuse, a room keeps the results private to that person, and a company socio can open only promoted apps and plugins. Nothing changes for anyone yet: it sits behind **Socios use apps for you** (in development, off), and apps can't be opened this way until the proxy phase lands.

- **Rooms: access audit and a security regression suite (dark).** A workspace admin still reads a room only in Operations, and every such view is now on the access log with the room: its transcript, what grounded it, its audit, and a room turn's trace, which also records who started the turn and which socio ran it, so the log traces a room's turn to them. A room in Operations is headed with its people and socios instead of an owner, and its new **Room audit** card lists who made it, joined, left, was invited, removed or refused, by name and never with content (`GET /api/operations/chats/:id/room-events`); a workspace admin's read of `GET /api/rooms/:id/events` for a room they don't administer is logged too. Runs lists a room's turns by role: a room admin sees every turn of the room, a member the turns their own messages started, each opening only their own traces, and each turn shows the person who started it. The socios admin page counts a room's members as its people and names a room's turns by who started them, and Operations' analytics no longer credit an unrecorded room turn to the room's placeholder owner. A new end-to-end suite covers cross-room leaks, removed members, invite abuse, forged speakers, history and stream access and every rooms route asked about a room the caller can't see; inviting now checks the caller's access to the room before the person invited, so an invite reveals neither. (CB-213)

- **Socios remember your earlier chats.** When a chat of yours gets long enough to be compacted (or you send `/compact`), the decisions, results, goals and open questions it kept, and what it was about, become your **episodes**: a socio brings the ones that match your message into a reply in your other chats, a small share of the recalled context, and the new `search_episodes` tool finds more when you mention an earlier conversation, each with its day and chat. Episodes are yours alone and only ever used in your own chats, never in a team topic, group or room. See, search and delete them (for real) under **Memories → From your chats**; admins set how long they are kept in Settings → General → Memory (180 days by default, `EPISODE_RETENTION_DAYS`), and only the leader deletes old ones (CB-180).

- **Rooms: each turn runs as the person who sent it (dark).** In a room the socio works with the sender's own access, files, connections, data, approvals and subscription, never the room's creator's, a room admin's or a socio's owner's; when the sender may not work there it stops and says so (ask a room admin for access) rather than use anyone else's. Personal socios never run in a room. What a tool returned in one person's turn, and a screenshot it took, stays theirs: other members, and the socio in their turns, see only that a tool ran, or that a request is waiting for its person's approval. Only the person whose access a request uses can see and decide it, never a room admin; it says which room it came from and that the socio's reply there is read by everyone, and it is decided and carried out only while they may still send in the room: leaving or being removed ends what they were asked there, and an archive or the room's socio being withdrawn ends everyone's. After a decision the socio carries on in the same work as before, on what is left of its time and tokens, in private chats too. A job's report in a room comes as the person who started it, while they are still a member. (CB-209)

- **Coding sessions' Git proxy and repository extraction for apps (dark, CB-239, CB-267).** The Git side of coding sessions: socios' coding agents fetch and push through Socio, which uses the GitHub App already connected for dispatch to mint a token for one repository and one request (read-only for fetches), never handing it to the agent, and revokes it when the request ends. Pushes go only to the session's own branch under `socio/<socio>/<session>`, and a new `coding-git` service checks each one first against a mirror of the repository: no changes to `.github/` or CODEOWNERS (in any case or lookalike spelling), no submodules, nothing shaped like a Socio token, every commit authored as the socio, and no co-author, email, issue-closing or CI-skip text in commit messages. Pull requests are drafts only, opened by Socio itself. Git requests are rate limited per session, and GitHub tokens per hour for the whole instance, so a busy agent can't use up the limit dispatch shares. Socios can also ask to give a workspace app (such as a Repomix app) a read-only copy of some paths of a private repository at one commit: the person approves every extraction, or gives standing access for one repository, path scope and app for up to 7 days on a separate card; the copy is sealed in the person's data space and only that app can read it, once, during the person's own request. Admins list the repositories, who may use them, push (after a short checklist) and extraction under Settings → Coding → Repositories. Everything stays behind the **Coding sessions** and **Repository extraction for apps** flags (in development, off), and nothing reaches it until the gateway listener and the coding sessions land. New tables are added at start (expand only); `coding-git` is a new image stage and an optional compose service (`coding` profile) that runs only when configured with `CODING_GIT_URL` and `CODING_GIT_SECRET`.

- **Rooms: members follow a room live, and lose it the moment they leave (dark, CB-212).** A room's members read it as it happens over one stream that stays open across turns (`GET /api/conversations/:id/stream`, and the runtime's connect), each seeing what the room may: other people's tool results, screenshots and approvals' notes come withheld, as in the history, and now in the transcript too. A member's streams close within a second when they are removed or leave, the room is archived or Rooms are turned off, also on the other server of a deploy. Only the person who started a turn or a room admin may stop it. Turning Rooms off stops the turns running in rooms and leaves them read-only for their members; turning it back on lets queued messages go on. A room's socios and limits now refresh each member's view of it.

- **Rooms: mentions, notifications and who's here (preview, CB-216).** Type **@** in a room to mention one of its members: the list shows only the room's people, and the one you pick is mentioned, even when two people share a name; a name typed or pasted without picking mentions nobody. Each member hears, through their own notification channels and choices, of mentions, of replies to their own messages and reports of their long jobs while they're away, and of invitations; never while they're looking at the room, never after they leave, never a tool's result. Each room's **⋯** menu has **Notify me about**: everything, mentions and my turns (the default), or muted, which shows the room's news quietly in the sidebar. The room's header shows the other members looking at it now, to members only; **Settings → Account → Notifications → Rooms** lets you hide yours. Help: [Rooms](/docs/rooms).

- **Rooms: your own queue, your own draft, and unread counts (preview, CB-215).** In a room each member sees, edits and cancels only their own queued messages; nobody else sees them before the socio reads them. A room admin also sees everyone's waiting messages and can cancel one (never edit it), which the room's audit records; a message whose writer left the room says it won't be sent. Once the socio has read a message it can no longer be edited or canceled. Drafts stay per person and room. A room with new replies from others shows bold in the sidebar with a count, which clears when you open the room, on all your devices (`POST /api/rooms/:id/read`); your own messages never count, though the report of a long job you started does.

- **Rooms in Operations, and who sees a room's use (CB-218).** Operations → Cost now shows **Who paid** (a person's own model subscription, the workspace, or not recorded for turns from before work cycles), **By room** (each room's cost, turns, tokens, people, socios, work cycles, handoffs and cycles stopped at a limit) and **Work cycles** (cycles, steps, the limits that stopped them, spend the provider didn't report, and time working beside start to end). A room's turn is credited to the person who sent the message, whose access it used, who paid, the room and the socio that ran it, so two people and two socios in one room each keep their share and a cycle with a handoff counts its spend once; turns from before work cycles stay unrecorded rather than guessed. A room's admins see its limits, its current message's time, tokens and spend, and the day's total once at least three people have written, in the room's **Members** panel; members see them too when an admin turns on **Members see their room's use and limits** in Settings → General → Rooms (off by default, over `ROOMS_BUDGET_VISIBLE`). Spend on someone's own subscription is never shown to anyone else in the room.

- **Rooms follow-ups: live replies, private reasoning, who wrote what (CB-212).** A room now updates over its live stream instead of polling: other people's messages show as their turn starts and the socio's reply as it writes it, with polling kept for when the stream can't be had. The model's reasoning in a turn that acted for someone else is withheld from every other member, as their tool results are, in the history, the transcript, the live stream, the runtime's connect and what later turns, recall and the shared notes read: others see only that the socio is thinking. Each reply records the socio that wrote it, and the room names that socio instead of the lead. A chat shared as a room shows its earlier messages as its former owner's, to members and in the model's labels, instead of "Team member". A turn that was refused or failed now says so under its message, for everyone in the room. Two people with the same name get different avatar colors. A queued message a room admin cancels during a turn disappears from its author's screen at once instead of showing as being read until the turn ends. The history no longer sends the server's note of whose turn each result was.

- **Issue references as chips, and related issues (CB-274).** Typing `#123` or `CB-123` in an issue's description or a comment shows a chip with that issue's key and title, which opens it; chips are read in one batch with the reader's own access, and a reference the reader can't see, or that doesn't exist, stays plain text. Code, links, `#1` in a heading, URL fragments and colors like `#fff` are left alone. Two issues can now be linked as **Related**, with no direction, besides one blocking the other: in an issue's side panel, anyone links an issue as **Related to** and removes related links, and admins also make one block the other (**Blocks**, **Blocked by**), change a link's type in place and remove any link (a pair has one link; `PUT` and `DELETE /api/issues/:number/links/:other`). Making, changing, removing or replacing a blocking link stays admin-only. `read_issue` and socios' `read_improvement` list `related` beside `blockedBy` and `blocks`, and `file_issue` and `file_improvement` take `related` for everyone (`blockedBy` and `blocks` for admins). One expand-only migration: a new `issue_relations` table (`issues` 15), so the release before runs on it. Help: [The Improvements board](/docs/improvements).

### Changed

- **Private socios are labeled everywhere (CB-269).** A "Personal" badge now marks a private socio in the chat sidebar, the socio crumb, chat headers and its home page, so you can tell your own socios from the workspace's at a glance.

### Fixed

- **Socio access and coding sessions, hardened before they turn on (dark, CB-232, CB-239).** An as-built check of every design-review item led to these fixes: the app browser's internal listener refuses every connection until its network's subnet is set, and the public apps host no longer takes socio credentials (development switch `APPS_SOCIO_ON_PUBLIC_HOST`); unauthenticated requests can't fill the access audit; a Git push revoked while it is being checked never reaches GitHub; the composer bar and Telegram buttons can't approve what must be decided on its card; extraction cards expire after 15 minutes; app logs drop Socio's tokens; coding sessions fail closed (each session enforced on its own, diff and status re-check access, one live session per workspace, report turns without tools, the outside model's host pinned at approval, the sandbox's proxy password redacted from agent output, an existing remote branch never pushed over); and a run of smaller fixes to the gateways, the coding sandbox and its egress proxy, the cards' text and the audit. Nothing is on yet, so nothing changes for anyone.

- **A room's note on an unanswered message speaks to the room, not the sender (CB-205).** When someone's turn was refused or failed, everyone else saw Socio's words to that person quoted under the message, such as "You can't start work in this room now". Others now read the reason about the sender, in the third person ("Nina's message wasn't answered: Nina can't start work in this room right now."), and the sender still sees what Socio told them. A provider's error text is still never shown; a failure of no known kind reads "The socio couldn't finish this turn."

- **A room no longer says Rooms are off when `ROOMS_ENABLED` turns them on (CB-212).** With `ROOMS_ENABLED=true` and nothing saved in Settings, the first page the server rendered had Rooms off (it left the environment's switch out), so a room said "Rooms are turned off" and hid Rooms while the server allowed them. The first page now has the same switch as the server and `/api/state`, the admin's setting over the environment's; the mini app log retention it showed is the one in force too.

- **A turn that waited only for approvals that all expired now ends at once (CB-212).** Its work cycle used to wait a week before it was tidied up; it now ends as soon as the last request expires or is withdrawn (its person left the room, say).

- **Rooms in the app (preview).** With Rooms on (Settings → General → Rooms, off by default), everyone gets **Rooms** in the sidebar: create a room with a company socio, or share one of your own chats as a room after confirming that everyone you invite reads its whole history. Invitations show on Home and in the sidebar, with what joining shares, and wait for **I understand** before **Join room**. A room shows who wrote each message (initials and name, **You**, the socio's name with a **socio** tag), sends each message to the room's queue so the socio answers it as its sender, and lets the sender or a room admin **Stop** a reply. Its **Members** panel lists people, invitations, roles and socios: room admins invite, change roles, remove, rename, archive and ask for socios, and the last admin hands the role on as they leave. Workspace admins let requested socios in from Settings → Rooms. With Rooms off nothing about rooms shows, and a room's link opens it read-only. Replies appear within seconds instead of streaming, for now. A room keeps the name it was made with when its first message would have titled it. A new `GET /api/rooms/:id/invitable` lists whom a room admin may invite. Help: [Rooms](/docs/rooms). (CB-212)

- **Shared memory for each Space.** A Space now has a **Memory** tab beside its pages: what the team knows together, one entry at a time, with who proposed each, in which chat, who approved it and every earlier version. Anyone who can read the Space proposes entries or changes, and a socio can propose one with the new `propose_space_memory` tool; nothing is used until one of the Space's editors approves it, and a socio's proposal always waits, whoever it acts for. Editors approve (in their own words if they like), reject, edit and retire entries, and manage the Space's people: editors and members (workspace admins are editors of every Space); a private Space's memory, like everything else in it, is its people's only (below). Socios that have the Space bring matching entries into a reply, in a small share of the recalled context and labeled as the Space's shared knowledge. Access is checked every turn: a person removed from a Space stops getting its entries at once, and a room gets them only when every member and everyone invited can read the Space (Telegram groups and team topics get none). Every change is in the workspace audit by id, never its words; what is out of use is deleted after Settings → General → Memory → **Keep retired Space memory for** (365 days, `SPACE_MEMORY_RETENTION_DAYS`), by the leader only (CB-181).

- **Rooms on a phone, with a keyboard and with a screen reader (CB-219).** The Getting started card no longer covers a chat's or a room's composer and its "thinking…" line (it waits for another page), and a room's composer gets the room a phone's keyboard leaves, as a chat's does. On a touch screen the room's controls are at least 44 px: the room list's rows, Pinned and Members, both mention pickers, the queued messages' Edit and Cancel, the panels' and dialogs' buttons, menus and close buttons. Focus moves into a pin being edited and a queued message's editor and comes back to Edit, goes to the message box once a queued message is canceled, and returns to whatever opened a room dialog; Escape in a pin's editor cancels the edit instead of closing Pinned. What the socio is doing (thinking, writing) is said politely, once per change, never the reply as it streams; the people picker says how many people fit; the Invitations count is read with its name; presence is read with the header rather than announced; the notification choices are labeled. Text in the room list, the empty room and the "@ you" mark now meets 4.5:1.

### Upgrading

- **Deploy order for plugins with socio access (CB-232).** Update the app before the apps runtime: the new runtime passes plugins only identity headers the app signed. Plugin services created before this release get their envelope key when they are next enabled, have their settings changed or are reinstalled. Turning on the app browser or the coding gateways needs their networks' subnets set (`APPS_INTERNAL_SUBNET` from the overlay, `CODING_GATEWAY_SUBNET` for the coding egress proxy's diagnostics); see deployment/actors/README.md. One expand-only migration (CB-239): coding sessions keep their sandbox's subnet and registry list (`gateway` 4, two nullable columns), so the release before runs on it.

- **Analytics rebuild for rooms, and an index for work cycles (CB-218).** `ETL_VERSION` goes to 9, so the analytics store rebuilds itself from SQLite once, with a new `work_cycles` step and new columns for where each turn ran, its cycle, who acted and who paid. The cycles module moves to schema 2 (an index on `work_cycles` by start, expand-only, so the release before runs on it). Update the app and any separately running analytics process together. `ROOMS_BUDGET_VISIBLE` is optional (off).

- One expand-only migration (CB-239): the model gateway's tables for coding models, their outside-provider repositories, limits, in-flight reservations, usage and dropped field names (`gateway` 1). The gateway listens only with `CODING_EGRESS_SECRET` set, on `GATEWAY_PORT` (default 4340), and accepts connections only from `GATEWAY_SUBNET`; leave them unset until coding sessions ship.

- **Episodes migration (CB-180).** The memories module moves to schema 2: a new `memory_episodes` table, expand-only, so the release before runs on it. No existing data changes and nothing is captured until chats are compacted. `EPISODE_RETENTION_DAYS` (1–3650, default 180) is optional.
- **Private Spaces need no migration (CB-181).** Existing Spaces stay open to the whole workspace. During the deploy a Space can't be made private (a 409, "try again in a few minutes") while the previous release still runs, since it would show it to everyone: each runtime records the `space-privacy` contract. Two new change-counter triggers (`space_members`, `space_settings`) are installed on boot.
- **Space memory migrations (CB-181).** The memories module moves to schema 3 (a new `space_memories` table) and the spaces module gets its first migration (`space_members`, `space_settings`); both expand-only, so the release before runs on them. Nothing is back-filled: every Space starts open to the workspace with no entries, and admins are its editors. `SPACE_MEMORY_RETENTION_DAYS` (1–3650, default 365) is optional.

- One expand-only migration (CB-209): `approval_requests` gains `cycleId`, the work a request was asked in, and an index by conversation and person (`approvals` 5). Requests from before carry on as before, in work of their own. Nothing to configure.

- One expand-only migration (CB-215): a new `room_read_state` table, each room member's read cursor (`rooms` 2). Nothing existing changes, so the release before runs on it.

- One expand-only migration (CB-216): new `room_notify_settings` (each member's notifications per room), `room_mentions` (the people each room message mentions) and `room_notices` (each room notice sent, so none goes twice) tables (`rooms` 3). Nothing existing changes, so the release before runs on it.

## v0.23.0 (2026-10-28)

Admins can now add a shared team calendar from Plugins, and connected agents can keep it up to date once a person approves it. Each plugin can now be opened to members as well as admins, and Learning can be set to look only at conversations that show a repeatable procedure.

- Add a shared Calendar from Plugins: week, month and agenda views, private events, and a private link to follow it in your own calendar app (CB-254)
- Let a connected agent add, change and remove calendar events once you approve its calendar access; sending the same booking again updates it instead of adding a copy (CB-254)
- Choose who can use each plugin: admins only, or members too (CB-254)
- Turn on Learning triage in Operations → Settings so only conversations that show a repeatable procedure go to Learning's analysis, and review each one's score in a new Triage list (CB-200)

### New

- **Socios using apps for you: the shared core (dark, CB-232).** The groundwork for a socio to use a mini app, plugin or coding gateway on behalf of the person it talks to, only with that person's approval: grants a person approves for one socio, one app and one conversation, short-lived credentials kept by hash so either server of a deploy accepts them, one access check that intersects what the person, the socio, the app and the grant allow, revocation that reaches every server within 5 seconds, and an append-only audit with no tokens or content in it. Nothing changes for anyone yet: it sits behind the new feature flags **Socios use apps for you**, **Coding sessions** (CB-239) and **Repository extraction for apps** (CB-267), all in development and off, and no app can be opened this way until the next phases land. The designs record the owner's decisions of 2026-10-28.

- **Socios using apps for you: asking, the approval cards and Socio access (dark, CB-232).** A socio can ask the person it acts for to use one of their mini apps for one task (`request_app_access`) and end it (`end_app_access`). The person decides on a card that puts what Socio knows first (the app by its own address and owner, the access in plain words, how long, and who in a room will see the answers) and the socio's words last, quoted and shown as plain text with hidden characters made visible. Only reading may skip the card, and only by the person's own choice per socio in **Settings → Account → Socio access**; changing data and taking actions always wait for a click, and an app can insist on asking every time. Consequential actions an app asks for show the exact parameters and can be used once; past 2 KB they must be opened before Allow works, and over 64 KB they are refused. Requests expire after 15 minutes. People see and end the access they granted under **Settings → Account → Socio access**, and admins see and end everyone's under **Operations → Socio access**. All of it stays off until an operator turns on `socio-actor-access`.

- **Learning triage: only conversations that show a procedure go to Learning's analysis.** Learning's new triage and checks run on the classification seam as its own use, with the seam's switch, threshold, daily cap, scrubbing, evaluation gate and the workspace's model; it is off by default, and off Learning works exactly as before. With **Learning triage** on (Operations → Settings, over `CLASSIFY_LEARNING`, `CLASSIFY_LEARNING_DAILY` and `CLASSIFY_LEARNING_THRESHOLD`), the model reads each finished conversation's scrubbed title and people's messages (never the replies, at most 1,500 characters) and scores whether it shows a repeatable procedure; once it has passed an evaluation against admins' labels, conversations it is sure are one-off are left out of the analysis. Each proposal that passes Learning's own checks is also checked by the model for a repeat of a rejected or published skill and for private details, counted as two new drop reasons. An analysis also starts when one session intent recurs in five sessions of a container's new conversations. Learning's page gains a **Triage** list per container, where admins see each conversation's score and intent and label it (the golden set for `pnpm run classify:eval -- --use=learning`), the use's calls, tokens and cost for the last 24 hours, and each run's skipped conversations and the tokens and cost its analysis reported; a proposal's evidence shows its triage. The nightly 02:00 pass and Analyze now are unchanged. Learning's runs table gains four columns at start (expand only); nothing to do. (CB-200)

- **A shared calendar, as a plugin, and plugins that run inside Socio.** Admins can install **Calendar** from **Plugins**: week, month and agenda views of the team's events in the calendar's time zone, with holds, cancellations, private events, locations and drag to move, and a private `.ics` link each person can subscribe to from their calendar app. Connected agents get `list_events`, `create_event`, `update_event` and `delete_event`, so an agent can copy bookings from Notion: an event sent again with the same source and page id updates the one already there instead of duplicating it. Each event records who created it and through which agent or socio, and the install's settings decide who may add and change events. The calendar is a new kind of plugin that runs inside Socio, with no service, Docker, app runtime or apps host, so installing it is the whole setup; it is offered on every instance and installed on none (`PLUGIN_CATALOGS=off` hides it). Every plugin now has **Who can use it** (admins only, or members too), which governs its page, launch, data, tools and feeds; each plugin has its own connected-agent scopes (`plugins:calendar:read`, `plugins:calendar:write`), and the older `plugins:read` and `plugins:write` keep covering the social planner only, never a plugin added since; socios in the app can use plugin tools for people who may use the plugin; a service plugin is told the person's time zone, and who they are (signed) when it asks for `person:read`; and the links an embedded plugin may open come from its own manifest instead of a fixed list. A new column records each install's audience (existing installs stay admins only) and the calendar's tables appear when it is installed. Connected agents need to be authorized again to get the calendar's scopes. Help: [Plugins](/docs/admin/plugins), [Calendar](/docs/calendar). (CB-254)

- **Rooms: membership, invitations and roles (dark).** Behind the Rooms switch (Settings → General → Rooms, off by default), a room now has members: its maker is its admin, admins invite people for 7 days, an invite gives nothing until accepted with a notice that joining shows the room's whole history, admins rename, archive, remove people and change roles, and the last admin hands the role on before leaving, even in an archived room; deactivating a room's last admin makes its earliest remaining member admin, and a room with nobody active left is archived and kept. Company socios join a room when a workspace admin authorizes them, and leave when one withdraws them. One access rule now answers every chat route (`roomAllows`): a private chat works exactly as before, a room's active members read it and send per role, and a removed person, a pending invite and a workspace admin get nothing; a removal ends the person's access, running turn and queued work at once. With Rooms off, rooms are read-only. Every change and refusal is in the room's audit, never its messages. The API is `/api/rooms`; the app's room screens come later. (CB-208)

### Changed

- Pages, their revisions, review receipts, page conversations, search index rows and use counts are now written through the Spaces pages repositories on the data layer, each change as one unit of work that waits its turn behind another in progress, including the page conversation's lease and a page use counted while a socio reads it. Nothing changes on screen; no migration. (CB-110)

- Data space datasets, files, grants and their audit are written through repositories on the data layer: saving, deleting and moving a file, revoking a grant and a folder's grants each wait their turn behind another change in progress instead of landing inside it; no visible change, no migration (CB-110)

- **Mini apps moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Mini apps, their sessions on the apps' own addresses, grants, versions and build and start attempts are now written through the apps' own repositories on the same data layer as the board. Registering, packaging, promoting, updating and deleting an app each still run as one transaction that waits its turn behind another change in progress, and signing in to an app, signing out, sharing an app and ending a person's app sessions now wait their turn too instead of landing inside another change. No migration and no visible change.

- Feature flags, workspace chat skills, service accounts and their keys, Souls, Learning (evidence, published skills, analysis runs and proposals), the data space's S3 move and re-seal, a backup copy's runtimes and the migration runner's own records are now written through repositories on the data layer, in the same transactions as before. Saving or clearing a flag rule, choice or plan, removing a workspace skill, recording a file's move to S3 or its new seal, and a service key's last-use stamp now wait their turn behind another change in progress instead of landing inside it. Nothing changes on screen; no migration. (CB-110)

### Upgrading

- One expand-only migration (CB-232): `approvals` 6 adds four nullable columns and an index to approval requests, for actions an app uses once and for long parameters a person opened. It runs under a start-first deploy; the previous release ignores the columns.

- Two expand-only migrations (CB-232): new tables for socio grants, their credentials, contexts and audit (`actors` 1), and `runtime_contracts` (`runtime` 3), where each server records the access contract it enforces; nothing is issued while an older server is still running. Both run under a start-first deploy. Nothing to configure: the new flags `socio-actor-access`, `coding-sessions` and `repo-extraction` stay off unless an operator turns them on.

## v0.22.0 (2026-10-28)

Socios can now help with whatever is on your screen, each one has a Soul you can read, edit and restore, and Operations shows what turns cost. Updates no longer interrupt anyone: the new version starts beside the old one and takes over once it is ready.

- Ask a socio about the page, issue or dataset you are looking at, from a panel on every screen (Ctrl+J or ⌘J) (CB-26)
- Give each socio a Soul: its role and standing instructions, with every earlier version kept and one click to restore (CB-179)
- See what turns cost by model, socio, person and backend in Operations → Cost, with prices kept up to date every day (CB-195)
- Updates no longer interrupt chats: the new version starts beside the old one and takes over once it answers (CB-108)
- Use Telegram direct messages as a full client: a command menu, switching socios, and approvals with Approve and Deny buttons (CB-145)

### New

- **Pick your dictation language.** Settings → Account → Profile has a **Dictation language** select: Auto (the default) or one of the 98 languages the transcription server knows, by name. With a language chosen, Socio sends it as a hint with your own dictation, as the `language` field of a recording (`POST /v1/audio/transcriptions`) and as `input_audio_transcription.language` in live text's and self-hosted calls' realtime session; with Auto nothing is sent and the server detects the language. It is each person's own preference, read on the server for whoever is dictating, and an unknown code is refused. Javanese is left out until socio-voice's code fix ships, and Nynorsk is transcribed as Norwegian. Nothing to do. (CB-221)

- **Each message's work is bounded, and says why when it stops.** Every message, and everything it starts, now runs as one stored piece of work with limits the server enforces, in web chats and Telegram topics alike: the turn time limit, 2,000,000 model tokens (admins can set up to 20,000,000), 20 messages a minute per chat (queued ones included), and a spend cap only where the model reports its cost (none by default). Each model request reserves its tokens first, so a request that would pass the limit is never sent; the socio stops with a message saying which limit it reached, and Runs shows "Hit its message's limit" with the cycle in the trace. A message past the minute's limit is turned away with its text kept in the box. Where a model reports no cost the spend is shown as unknown rather than guessed, and tokens and time still bound it. What a message used is stored as it goes, so a restart neither loses it nor runs the same message again from nothing. Admins set the limits under Settings → General → **Work per message**, over `ROOM_CYCLE_TOKENS`, `ROOM_MESSAGES_PER_MINUTE`, `ROOM_CYCLE_SPEND_USD` and `ROOM_HANDOFFS_PER_CYCLE` (counted now, used once socios hand work on); rooms will only be able to lower them. Queued messages now go as their author's own turn. The new tables are added at start; nothing to do. (CB-211)

- **Voice calls on your own voice server.** Calls can now run self-hosted: speech becomes text on the transcription server's realtime socket (socio-voice's `/v1/realtime`, as live dictation uses), each thing said is a normal turn of the socio as the chat's owner (their sender stamp, the socio's model, tools, permissions, approvals and limits, stored in the chat like a typed message), and the reply is read aloud by an OpenAI-compatible speech server (`POST /v1/audio/speech`, PCM) or, without one, by the device's own voice. An admin picks **Cloud** or **Self-hosted** and the speech server in **Settings → General → Calls**, over `VOICE_BACKEND` (`cloud` when unset) and `VOICE_SPEECH_URL`, `VOICE_SPEECH_MODEL`, `VOICE_SPEECH_VOICE` and `VOICE_SPEECH_API_KEY`; keys follow dictation's rules and never reach the browser. When the voice server is down the call ends before it starts with the reason, and where cloud calls are set up the chat offers **Use cloud voice** for that call. The call view adds **Interrupt**, shows approvals waiting in the chat, and the chat behind it fills in each turn as it ends. Cloud calls work as before. Help: [Voice calls](/docs/voice-calls). (CB-265)

- **Apps on a socio's home page.** A socio's home page has an **Apps** section listing the mini apps it built that you can already open (your own, and those shared with one of your Spaces), each marked Published, In development or Unpublished, with a link that opens it in a new tab or, when unpublished, goes to the Apps page. The list comes from a new light endpoint, `GET /api/apps/dots/:dotId`, which reads the app registry only: it never probes a computer or the app runtime and never starts an app. Visibility is exactly the Apps page's, and another person's personal socio stays a 404, so its apps never show on a home page. (CB-260)

- **Plugins.** An admin can install a plugin on this instance from the new **Plugins** page; each one runs as its own Docker Swarm service on the workspace app runtime, embedded in the workspace or opened in a tab from its own apps-host origin, with a one-time launch code and a session that only active admins get. The first plugin is the social planner (`social-strategy`): week, month and agenda calendar, a composer with an X preview, a reply queue and voice notes, and admin-only MCP tools for planned posts and replies. It never posts to X; **Open in X** only builds an `x.com/intent/post` link that you publish yourself. Nothing is installed by default, and an instance without a plugin catalog offers nothing to install. The Docker socket is reachable only through a body-checking socket proxy that refuses privileged, host-network, bind-mount, capability, device, unpinned-image and other host escapes, strips environment and secrets from what it returns, and requires CPU, memory and process limits. (PR #1)

- **Use Telegram direct messages as a full client (CB-145).** Typing `/` in a direct message with the bot opens a command menu with the bot's own commands and every chat skill (`/grill_me`), kept current as skills change. `/new` starts a new conversation, `/socio` switches the socio with buttons or by name, `/stop` stops the answer in progress, `/memories` lists the memories your socios proposed and `/jobs` shows how your long-running jobs are doing. An approval notice now shows the request's details with **Approve** and **Deny** buttons. A memory a socio proposes during a direct message comes with **Keep** and **Turn down**. 👍 and 👎 on an answer count as its reactions, and a job started there keeps a progress message up to date. Group chats and forum topics are unchanged until rooms define them (CB-214).

- **Choose a socio's character.** Pick Sebas, Manny, Mingo or Rulo when you create a socio, and change it later in the socio's settings. The picker is a labeled radio group that works with the keyboard and fits narrow screens. Several socios can share a character. The choice shows everywhere the socio appears: the sidebar, its home, chats, calls, the command palette and push notification icons. Whoever can edit a socio's settings can change its character (the owner of a personal socio, admins for any socio), and each choice is recorded in the socio's audit. (CB-256)

- **Rooms switch and storage (dark).** Settings → General → Rooms has an admin's switch for shared rooms, off by default over a new `ROOMS_ENABLED` (on only for `true`, `1`, `yes` or `on`). Rooms are not usable yet: with the switch on nothing changes in chats. The card shows admins how many chats there are and that each is private (`GET /api/rooms/readiness`, admins only). Underneath, a conversation now has a kind, private or room, and a new rooms module stores room members, socios, limits, Telegram topic bindings and an audit of room changes; a room is owned by `room:<id>`, which no account can have, so every check that looks for a chat's owner finds nobody in a room until it learns the room rules. (CB-207)

- **See your connection to Socio.** A connection icon in the header shows whether this browser is online, reconnecting or offline, and its bars reflect the round trip to the server. While all is well it stays quiet; otherwise it says Reconnecting or Offline. Hover or tap for details: the latest round trip and the last few, the last check and the last disconnect. Whether the server answers comes from the poll the app already runs; the round trip is an empty `GET /ping` (204, no session, nothing in it) about every 30 seconds while the tab is in view and never while it is hidden or offline. The return of a lost connection is announced to screen readers, the pulse honors reduced motion, and it works on phones and in the installed app. (CB-263)

- **Drag files and folders into folders.** In Media, drag a file or folder onto a folder card or row, the folder tree, or a breadcrumb to move it there, including back to **All files**. Folders that can take the drag are outlined and the one under the pointer is highlighted, with no motion when reduced motion is on. Each file and folder also has a **Move to…** action for the keyboard, screen readers and touch. A move says where it went and offers **Undo**. The server still allows only your own files and folders, refuses a folder into itself or a subfolder and a name already taken where it lands, and now also refuses a folder moved straight into Socios, where it would pass for a socio's own folder and could not be moved again. (CB-255)

- **See and apply updates from the Platform page.** Operators see the releases newer than the running build on Settings → Platform → Updates, each with its notes and Upgrading steps, from a release feed they set there or with `UPDATES_FEED`: GitHub releases (`github:<owner>/<repo>`) or GHCR tags (`ghcr:<owner>/<image>`). A private feed's read token is entered on the card and stored sealed for that feed only, or comes from a swarm secret; it is never shown. Checks run every `UPDATES_CHECK_HOURS` (24, editable on the card; 0 only on demand), send a generic User-Agent and nothing about the workspace or its people, and notify operators once per new release. With the new opt-in updater sidecar, **Update to** guides the operator through every Upgrading step up to the target, then the app takes a backup with the configured backup settings (the off-site copy too, when it is configured; a failure changes nothing) and the updater changes only the image tag keys of the stack through the Portainer stack API, keeping every other env key, waits for `/healthz` to report the new version and rolls back on failure. Automatic updates are off by default; turned on, they apply only releases without Upgrading steps. Only operators can see or trigger any of it. (CB-259)

- **Feature flags and early access.** Features can now ship dark and be tried early, all inside Socio, with no outside flag service and no telemetry. Each flag is declared in code with a safe default, a stage (in development, early access, released), an owning area and a removal date, and the server decides it per request for each person: the default, `FEATURE_FLAGS` on the server, an operator's rule for the server (which can be locked), an admin's rule for the workspace (on, off, or for some roles and people), and a person's own choice where admins let people opt in. Admins manage them in **Settings → Features**, with every change in the workspace audit; people try features under **Settings → Early access**, and What's new marks early-access highlights with a badge. A socio only gets the tools of flags that are on for the person it works for. The first flag, `web-reads`, is on as before and lets an operator or admin turn `http_get` and `http_json` off for some or all people. (CB-169)

- **Plans for features.** Operators can define plans that include or leave out features and put the workspace on one, in **Settings → Features**. A feature a plan leaves out is off and admins can't turn it on; one it includes is on unless admins narrow it. Plan changes are audited. (CB-182)

- Ask a socio about the page, issue, dataset or schedule on your screen from a panel on every screen (⌘J or Ctrl+J, or the header), in your own conversation for that item with the socio you last asked; the socio sees the item only as you can see it, checked again on every message (CB-26)

- Save a link's full source to a page with `/unfurl`: X posts through FixTweet, videos and photos through oEmbed, and articles from their page, with the whole text, attribution and links rather than a summary, under a static link card whose images are copies in your Files, so nothing on the page loads from the linked site (CB-230)

- **A Soul for each socio.** Each socio has a Soul: its role and standing instructions, and optionally its voice, values and working style. Open it with **Soul** on the socio's home page or menu. Anyone who can use a socio can read its Soul and every earlier version, with who made each one and when, what changed from the version before, and a preview of what the socio reads.
