The owner edits a personal socio's Soul and admins edit a workspace socio's. A socio never changes a Soul. **Restore** puts an earlier version back as a new version, so the history is always kept. The Soul is in the socio's system prompt and changes only when someone edits it. It shapes tone and role and never overrides rules, permissions or approvals. (CB-179)

- **Sign-in and code access in the app.** Operators can narrow `AUTH_PASSWORD`, `AUTH_PASSKEY`, Telegram sign-in links, `AUTH_OPEN_SIGNUP`, the OAuth providers, `AUTH_ALLOWED_EMAILS`, `AUTH_ALLOWED_DOMAINS`, `AUTH_ADMIN_EMAILS` and `DISPATCH_CODE_USERS` under **Settings → Access**, without a redeploy. The environment stays the ceiling: the page can only turn a method off or take entries off a list, never add any. Every change needs the operator's password or a passkey again, bound to that one change and usable once. It ends the sessions that no longer qualify, stops the code runs of people taken off the code list, goes into an append-only history and is sent to every admin and operator. Changes that would lock the operator, or every operator, out are refused, and `pnpm run auth access-policy-reset` is the break glass on the server. Admins, members, service-account keys, socios and connected agents can't reach it. (CB-124)

- **What grounded chats, and time to report.** Operations → Knowledge maps which Spaces' pages socios read to answer, by Space and day (a page read, found in a search or open as the chat's page; each turn and page once), lists the pages that grounded the most chats, and counts dataset queries and recalls of the chat, which stay off the map. A chat's transcript in Operations shows what its turns read, written to the access log. Socios record references only (ids, kind and Space), never page, dataset or chat content. An item filed with **File issue** under a failed run is linked to the run, as is a socio's filing to its turn, and Reliability shows how long failures take to be reported. (CB-194)

- See what turns cost in Operations → Cost, by model, socio, person and backend, priced by effective-dated model prices from a daily price sync of public provider and community price lists, with admins' own prices winning from their date onward; turns without a price show as unpriced, and the sync's switch is in Operations → Settings (CB-195)

- Cost self-hosted backends and subscriptions with cost pools (fixed, written off or metered), shared hourly among their turns by weighted tokens, with idle capacity and the cheapest cloud price for the same turns beside them (CB-196)

- **Failure labels in Reliability.** Admins can label a failure that the rules leave **Unclassified** from its row under Recent failures. The panel shows the error text the model reads, with contact details and secrets scrubbed and the text cut to 300 characters. Opening the panel is written to the access log. A label counts within a minute, and every admin label also joins a golden set. **Operations → Settings → Failure labels** turns on suggestions from the workspace's default shared model. It is off by default, has a threshold (90%) and a daily cap (200 calls), and an admin's values override the `CLASSIFY_FAILURES*` environment defaults. A suggestion counts only when the model is at least as sure as the threshold and its model version has passed an evaluation against the golden set (20 labels or more). **Evaluate**, or `pnpm run classify:eval -- --use=failures --provider=workspace`, runs that check and reports per-label precision, recall and a confusion matrix. Every call is logged with its tokens, and with its cost when the provider reports one. Labels never override the rules' other categories. This is the shared classification and extraction seam (`server/classify`) that intent, themes and Learning will use; only the `workspace` provider ships. (CB-198)
- **Operations → Themes: what people come to socios for.** Each of people's sessions can get an **intent** (ask, write, analyze, automate, research, admin or other, a list admins can edit) and one to three **topics** with a hint of whether it seemed resolved, read by the workspace's model from the chat's title and the session's first three messages, scrubbed and cut to 300 characters. The new Themes page shows sessions by intent and by theme (topics grouped by their words, by week) with how they ended, and Value gains **Completion by intent**. A theme is named only when at least three different people had sessions on it; smaller ones are only counted. Both are off by default with their own switch, threshold and daily cap in Operations → Settings (over `CLASSIFY_INTENT*` and `CLASSIFY_THEMES*`), and the model's labels count only after it passes an evaluation against admins' own labels, set in Themes' Review card (on the access log). `pnpm run classify:eval` takes `--use=intent` and `--use=themes`. (CB-199)

### Changed

- **A design for shared rooms (CB-205).** A design sets out how several people will share one conversation with a socio: a room is the same conversation with members, each turn runs as the person who sent the message, work is bounded per cycle, private memories and tool results stay private, and existing Telegram team topics stay as they are until a room admin converts one. `src/shared/rooms.ts` holds the shared rules the coming work builds on. Nothing changes in the app yet.

- **Telegram team topics keep each person's context their own.** In a chat with other people in it, every person's message now reaches the socio after a label Socio writes from who sent it, so a label someone types is only their text, and two people with the same name stay two people. One person's private tool results and approval details reach only their own later turns: anyone else's turns, `recall` and the chat's notes get only the tool's name and whether it worked. The chat's notes are written for everyone, naming who said what, and notes kept while the chat was one person's own are never used there: they start over once. The socio uses a person's time zone there only if they shared their profile with team chats; otherwise it says the time is the workspace's (Operations' time zone). Private chats are unchanged. (CB-210)

- Deploys no longer interrupt anyone: the new release starts beside the old one and takes over once it answers its health check, while the old one finishes its running turns. One of them at a time runs the scheduler, Telegram, backups and the other background work, and hands it over in under a second; a chat that reconnects to the other one keeps following its turn, and Stop works from either. Database changes now only add things unless marked otherwise, so the previous release keeps running on the new schema, and from now on going back a release needs no restore (CB-108)

- Pick a chat's model, a socio's default model and the page chat's socio from the app's own menu instead of the browser's list, grouped by where each model runs and usable with the keyboard and touch (CB-257)

- **Add xAI Grok as a shared model.** Setup and the in-app admin help now explain how an operator adds Grok on Settings → Platform as an OpenAI-compatible backend: base URL `https://api.x.ai/v1`, an xAI model id such as `grok-4`, and the xAI API key, which is stored sealed and never shown again. It is a shared backend billed to that key's account; people cannot connect a personal Grok key. No code changed. (CB-252)

### Fixed

- **Dictation and source links on a chat's first message.** The new-chat composer now has voice dictation and the **Add source page link** action, like a running chat. The link button explains itself with a tooltip in both places. (CB-266)

- **Personal socios and `shared/`.** Copying a file to `shared/…` from a personal socio no longer lands silently in a `shared` subfolder of its own folder. It now fails with a clear message pointing at a plain path its computer already reaches, because `shared/` is only a company socio's shared computer folder. (CB-268)

- **Computer disk usage on Health.** v0.16.0 said Operations Health shows each computer's volume space; in production it never did, because Docker took over 45 seconds to size the volumes and the supervisor gave up after 4. Disk usage now comes from a separate read-only Docker proxy that can only read volume and container sizes (`GET /system/df?type=volume` and `GET /containers/json?size=true`) and answers only for this deployment's computers; nothing privileged reads the volumes. It is measured every 15 minutes in the background, never during a sample, and Socio computers shows each running computer's workspace, browser profile and private volumes together, its container layer, and when they were last measured. When the proxy is not set up or Docker fails, the Disk column says so instead of a dash. The supervisor no longer calls `df` at all. (CB-197)

- **Read the Datasets list on a phone.** On a narrow screen a card's button or form now sits under its title and description at full width, so the description no longer squeezes to one word per line. This applies to every card with a header action: Datasets, Files and Socio access on the Data page, a dataset's Tables, Apps, and the Platform page. The Datasets table wraps long names and leaves Retention and Updated to each dataset's page on phones. Wide screens look as before. (CB-262)

- Dropdowns in settings (External MCP connections, dictation, context, chat skills) no longer cut off the chosen value (CB-258)

- A page with a bold link (`**[text](url)**`) or other nested formatting opens in the visual editor again instead of falling back to Markdown source (CB-230)

- **Not found in the help center.** A help article that doesn't exist, or that your role can't open, now shows an **Article not found** page inside the help center, with its navigation, a link back to the help center and a button that opens search, instead of "Something went wrong. Reloading usually fixes it." The page is the same either way, so it still doesn't say whether the article exists, and the answer is still a 404. Elsewhere in the app, a page that answers 404 shows Not found rather than the reload message. (CB-264)

### Upgrading

- Deploy this release **stop-first** (`APP_UPDATE_ORDER=stop-first`): the running release predates the leader lease. Before it, point every proxy host that forwards to `node-1:4310` at `company-brain_app:4310` on the proxy network: the app no longer publishes port 4310 (`PUBLISHED_PORT` goes from the stack env). After it, set `APP_UPDATE_ORDER=start-first` (the default); later releases deploy start-first. Migrations `runtime` 1 and 2 and `apps` 6 add tables only. Steps, checks and rollback: deployment/portainer/README.md (CB-108)

- **Add the computers' disk usage proxy.** Build the supervisor image (`--only supervisor`; its tag moves to `b6932d3-dot-auth-isolated-v2`), then update `company-brain-computers` with its new `disk-usage` service, then the app, whose stack file adds `COMPUTER_DISK_USAGE_URL`. No secret, network, migration or computer image changes; deployment/portainer/README.md has the steps and checks. (CB-197)

- **Create the updater token secret before deploying this stack file.** `company-brain.yml` now mounts `company_brain_updater_token` into the app: create it with the value `none` to keep the updater off (the Updates card then only notifies), or with a random value (`openssl rand -hex 32`) to use the updater. Resend every stack env key as usual, plus `UPDATES_FEED` (empty or a feed), `UPDATES_CHECK_HOURS=24`, `UPDATES_AUTO_APPLY=false`, `UPDATER_URL` (empty) and `UPDATER_TOKEN_SECRET=company_brain_updater_token`. No database migration: the settings live in the existing settings and secret settings, so the previous image still opens the database. Only the app image changes. (CB-259)

- **Optional: install the updater sidecar.** Build `company-brain-updater:<tag>` (`node scripts/build-images.mjs --only updater`; a plain build skips it), create `company_brain_updater_portainer_token` with a Portainer API key that may update the `company-brain` stack, deploy `deployment/portainer/company-brain-updater.yml` as its own stack `company-brain-updater` with `UPDATER_IMAGE_TAG` and `UPDATER_PORTAINER_URL`, then set `UPDATER_URL=http://company-brain-updater:4350` on `company-brain`. The steps are in deployment/portainer/README.md. It updates only the stack it is configured for, never itself. (CB-259)

- **New `flags` tables.** The `flags` module adds its first migration (rules, people's early-access choices, plans and the workspace's plan); it runs at start and changes no existing data. The optional `FEATURE_FLAGS` variable (e.g. `web-reads=off`) sets this server's defaults; the stack passes it through, empty by default. Nothing changes until someone sets a rule. (CB-169, CB-182)

- A new `ask` migration (version 1) adds the `context_threads` and `ask_preferences` tables; it runs on start and changes no existing table (CB-26)

- **Rebuild the app image.** The first boot applies module `media` migration 5, which adds the `media_embeds` table that records link cards' image copies. An older image refuses a database migrated by this one: to go back, restore a backup taken before the upgrade. No setting changes; `unfurl_link` uses the existing web read settings (`WEB_*`) and the socio's web research permission. (CB-230)

- **Keep a verified pre-upgrade database backup.** SQLite workspace advances from 15 to 16 and a new rooms module starts at 1. Workspace 16 adds three columns to conversations with constant defaults and a partial index, so it rewrites no table and is instant: every existing chat becomes `private`, with no creator or conversion date filled in, and no room rows are made. Private chats keep their readers, history and links. Older images refuse the newer schema, so rollback requires restoring the pre-upgrade database with the old image and deliberately recovering later writes. Were an older build forced onto the migrated database, it would show any room to nobody (its owner is no person) and lose nothing. Never drop the new columns to go back. (CB-207)

- Workspace migration 17 adds `socio_souls` and makes version 1 of every socio's Soul from its role instructions, word for word. The version is dated and signed by the audit line that last set them. Prompts stay the same, byte for byte, so prompt caches hold. `dots.instructions` is kept as a copy of the Soul's instructions for rollback; instructions edited on an older build after a rollback don't reach the Soul, so edit the Soul again after upgrading. Admins can no longer change a personal socio's instructions; its owner can. (CB-179)

- **Workspace migration 18** adds an optional `character` column to socios. It is instant and backward compatible: existing socios keep the character they had (it still comes from their id) until someone picks one, and new workspaces store Sebas for their first socio. Rebuild the app image; no other image changes. (CB-256)

- **Auth migration 4 (access policy).** It adds the `access_policy` and `access_policy_audit` tables and records how each new session began (`auth_session.signInMethod`). Sessions from before the upgrade have no method: they stay valid while their account has a way in. Nothing changes until an operator saves a policy. (CB-124)

- **Telegram schema 2 (CB-145).** A new `telegram_replies` table records which socio answer each bot message in a direct message shows, for reactions; the migration runs at start. Older images refuse the newer schema, so a rollback needs the pre-upgrade database. Chat skills can no longer be named `new`, `socio`, `stop`, `memories` or `jobs`. An existing workspace skill with one of those names keeps working in the app, but in a Telegram direct message the bot's command takes the name.

- **Migrations and an analytics rebuild.** This update migrates runs 4→5 (`run_refs`), issues 13→14 (`issue_runs`) and data 9→10 (the data audit's chat) and bumps the analytics ETL to version 5, so the analytics store rebuilds from SQLite after the deploy. Update the app and any separately running analytics service together, and keep a verified pre-upgrade backup: an older image refuses the newer module versions. Turns from before the upgrade have no grounding. (CB-194)

- Operations migrates SQLite 3→4 (model prices and cost pools) and the analytics store rebuilds once (ETL version 6). The price sync is on by default in the production image (`OPS_PRICE_SYNC=off` or the Settings switch turns it off for offline installs; it sends a generic user agent and no identifiers). Keep a verified pre-upgrade backup: an older image refuses the migrated database (CB-195, CB-196)

- **New module `classify` migration 1, and an analytics rebuild.** The first boot adds the `classify_labels`, `classify_calls`, `classify_evals` and `classify_cursors` tables. `ETL_VERSION` goes to 7, so the analytics store rebuilds itself from SQLite once, and a new `labels` ETL step reads the labels. Update the app and any separately running analytics process together. Nothing calls a model until an admin turns on Failure labels. As with any module migration, rolling back to an older image means restoring a verified pre-upgrade backup. (CB-198)

- **Plugins are opt-in and need operator action.** Nothing changes unless you deploy `deployment/portainer/plugins.overlay.yml`, the only file that mounts the Docker socket (on `docker-socket-proxy` alone). To offer the social planner on one instance: build and push `company-brain-plugin-social-strategy` (`scripts/build-images.mjs`, target `plugin-social-strategy`) and pin its `name@sha256:` digest in both `SOCKET_PROXY_IMAGE_PINS` and `APPS_RUNTIME_IMAGE_PINS`; create the Swarm secret `company_brain_plugin_service_token` and add it to the app service's secrets; set `"no-new-privileges": true` in `/etc/docker/daemon.json` on every node; deploy the stack with the overlay, `PLUGIN_CATALOGS=bundled:social-strategy` and `PLUGIN_CATALOG_HOSTS` set to that instance's public host (Socio refuses to boot with a catalog on a host outside `PLUGIN_CATALOG_HOSTS` or inside `PLUGIN_CATALOG_DENY_HOSTS`, both operator settings); then an admin installs it from **Plugins**. Mini apps must be configured (`APPS_HOST`, `APPS_RUNTIME_URL`, `APPS_RUNTIME_TOKEN`). The `plugins` schema migrates on boot (empty tables); the planner's tables are created only when it is installed. Rebuild the app and apps images; CI's image now installs Go for a socket-proxy test, so rebuild it too. Plugin services have not yet been run against a live Swarm. (PR #1)

- **Connected agents ask again for plugin tools.** Plugin tools need the new `plugins:read` (list) and `plugins:write` (add and change) scopes on top of an admin's role and the install's permissions. Existing connections get none of them until the person approves the new scopes; a call outside the granted scopes is refused. (PR #1)

- **Analytics rebuild for intent and themes.** `ETL_VERSION` goes to 8, so the analytics store rebuilds itself from SQLite once, with a new `session_labels` step. No database migration. Update the app and any separately running analytics process together. Nothing calls a model until an admin turns on Session intent or Session themes. (CB-199)

## v0.21.0 (2026-10-26)

Read a role-aware in-app help center and follow first-use product tours that link each step to the relevant article. Socios and connected agents can also read the same docs through a new, separately consented MCP tool.

- Browse role-filtered help articles and search them in the app (CB-94)
- Read the same docs through a separately consented MCP tool (CB-94)
- Take first-use product tours that link each step to its help article (CB-253)
- Keep per-person tour progress and resume where you left off (CB-253)

### New

- **In-app help center.** A help center renders server-side Markdown as sanitized HTML at `/docs`, with full-text search at `/docs/search`, raw Markdown, and `llms.txt` summaries for agents. Twenty-seven articles cover getting started, chats, socios, privacy, Spaces, pages, goals, schedules, approvals, the improvements board, data and media, Telegram and connected agents for everyone, plus separate deployment, secrets, upgrades, backups and storage guidance for operators and settings, service accounts and learning guidance for admins. Each article is shown only to the audience it is for: public, admin and operator views are filtered on the server, and a missing or unauthorized article returns a 404 rather than revealing that it exists. Article content stays server-only; it is never bundled into client assets, and the client build rejects docs source and content imports. Pages are served private with no-store and a fresh viewer before lookup. (CB-94)
- **Docs for connected agents.** Socios and connected agents can list, read and search the same role-filtered docs through new `docs:read` MCP tools (`list_docs`, `read_docs`, `search_docs`). Docs access is a new, explicit OAuth scope and consent: existing issues and pages grants give no help access, and a person reviews the docs scope before a connection can read any article. The tools resolve the current person per stateless request and use the same shared docs catalog as the web help center, with canonical path validation and role filtering. No database migration is required for the scope. (CB-94)
- **Contextual onboarding guides.** First-use product tours (Driver.js) walk new people through getting started, goals, schedules, improvements and, for admins and operators, the admin and operator areas. Guides are filtered by role, module and configuration, so members see member guides and admins and operators see progressively more. Substantive steps offer a Read More link to the relevant help article (CB-94), and reading it opens in a new tab without losing your place in the tour. Tours respect keyboard control, focus order and reduced motion, work on mobile, and handle a missing target or route safely. They never send, save, approve or create anything. Progress is stored per person and per guide version: you can skip, dismiss, pause and resume, and a guide you completed or dismissed at one version can return when its content changes. (CB-253)

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the app image.** This update adds the in-app docs content and onboarding preferences. No database migration is required: onboarding progress is stored in existing per-person preferences with a strict, versioned schema and a guide-level nested server merge, and the docs scope is held in existing connection settings. Dispatch, computer, supervisor, browser and apps-runtime images need no rebuild for these changes. (CB-94, CB-253)
- **Consent again for docs access.** A connected agent that already has issues or pages access does not gain help access automatically. Its person reviews and grants the new `docs:read` scope before it can list, read or search docs; until then its docs calls are refused. (CB-94)

## v0.20.0 (2026-10-26)

New socios start private to their creator, with an explicit choice to share them. Personal MCP connections let you review external tools and approve each requested call.

- Create private socios by default and choose workspace sharing explicitly (CB-251)
- Connect personal external MCP servers from Account settings (CB-250)
- Choose tool access separately for each socio (CB-250)
- Review every external call and retain its recorded outcome (CB-250)

### New

- **Personal external MCP connections.** Account settings accepts a name and a public HTTPS server URL on port 443, using Streamable HTTP with OAuth or no authentication for public tools. OAuth supports client metadata documents, dynamic registration and issuer-bound pre-registered client details, with an explicit scope review. Connections and credentials belong to the current person, including when a socio is shared. Private network endpoints, custom API-key headers and legacy SSE-only or local stdio servers are outside this increment. Third-party providers have not completed live OAuth acceptance; local protocol tests are not proof that a particular provider connects. (CB-250)
- **Review and grant tools explicitly.** Inspect the provider's complete tool schemas and descriptions, then select supported tools separately for each socio. A catalog refresh clears existing grants for another review. Inputs and structured outputs use bounded JSON Schema 2020-12 validation; unsupported keywords, other dialects, recursive or external references and excessive data are refused. Provider descriptions and permission hints remain untrusted data. Granting a general-purpose tool does not create provider-specific permissions for each operation it might expose. (CB-250)
- **A human decision for every call.** In your web chats, a socio can describe a granted tool and request one call with its complete arguments, limited to 4,096 UTF-8 bytes. Auto and bypass policies cannot approve external MCP calls. Before sending, Socio checks current account, conversation, socio access, credentials, grants and tool catalog again. Remote prompts, resources, sampling, elicitation and embedded applications are not enabled; these tools are unavailable to Telegram, scheduled, background and dispatch turns. (CB-250)
- **Disconnect and keep outcomes clear.** Disconnecting or removing a connection, or deactivating its owner through People or the auth CLI, invalidates its local credentials, consent attempts and grants. Reactivation does not restore access. A durable journal claims each business call once and keeps bounded, paged text or JSON results, including after connection removal. Interrupted or uncertain calls retain an unknown outcome and are never automatically replayed; verify them with the provider before taking further action. Socio cannot undo external changes. Disconnecting here does not revoke the provider's own authorization grant. (CB-250)

### Fixed

- **Make new socios personal by default.** Operators and admins now explicitly enable **Share with the workspace** to create a workspace socio; member-created socios remain personal. Creation and editing dialogs, sidebar tooltips and socio home labels explain visibility. Existing socios keep their ownership, and admins retain access to personal socio settings. Workspace socios still keep each person's conversations separate, while their shared computer files are available to everyone who uses them. (CB-251)

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the app image and verify a fresh backup.** The first app boot applies module `mcp-connections` migration 1, adding connection settings, one-use OAuth attempts, socio grants and the call journal. It does not import connections or contact providers. No dispatch, computer, supervisor, browser or apps-runtime change is required. The previous app's strict startup guard rejects the new module: reverting to an unmodified older image requires a verified pre-upgrade database backup and its matching user-data snapshot. Preserve and reconcile later writes before restoring that backup. Do not remove migration records to bypass the guard. (CB-250)
- **Retain the existing encryption keys and canonical origin.** MCP credentials and pending PKCE state use the separate `mcp-credentials` sealing purpose and join the existing key status and resealing process. Keep `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, any previous rotation key and `SESSION_SECRET` available as appropriate. OAuth uses the configured canonical HTTPS `APP_ORIGIN`; there is no new provider secret required at startup. Server requests pin public DNS addresses and verify TLS. Browser consent URLs are checked before handoff, but Socio cannot pin a browser's DNS or control later provider navigation. (CB-250)

## v0.19.1 (2026-10-26)

Reduce issue-board rendering during successful refreshes while keeping changed cards current. The reported vertical scrolling jank in the iPhone PWA remains open; these measured improvements do not establish an actual-device fix.

- Reuse unchanged issue cards after successful refreshes (CB-249)
- Preserve fresh issue data while a move is pending or fails (CB-249)

### Changed

- **Avoid rendering unchanged cards on refresh.** Successful issue-list responses retain unchanged issue and assignee data. Changed votes, status, labels and names still update, and incoming lists still control ordering, additions and removals. Only changed cards need to render after an issue update. This reduces measured rendering work; physical iPhone scrolling acceptance remains outstanding. (CB-249)
- **Keep moves consistent with fresh server data.** Polls and detail responses preserve a pending move. If it fails, the board uses the latest known server state, retaining concurrent vote and text changes and keeping removed issues out of the list. (CB-249)

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the app image.** This update introduces no database migration or configuration change. (CB-249)

## v0.19.0 (2026-10-26)

Follow parent and child pages directly, and write chat messages above a compact row of actions.

- Navigate page ancestors and discover a page’s children (CB-237)
- Keep chat text above grouped message actions (CB-238)

### New

- **Navigate existing page hierarchies.** Page breadcrumbs link to ancestors, with a full-path menu to keep the desktop header compact. Phones keep wrapping parent links above the page title, and parent pages list their direct children. All pages retains its existing browsing and search views and shows the complete known parent path on page cards. Navigation uses the current Space’s authorized page list; missing, ambiguous or cyclic parent trails are omitted rather than invented or repaired. (CB-237)

### Changed

- **Group chat actions beneath the draft.** Existing socio and page-chat composers give text its full width, then group source-link, attachment and dictation controls at the left and send, stop or queue controls at the right. Their toolbar buttons provide 44-pixel targets without extra gaps, and keyboard order follows text, tools, then send. Message submission, IME composition, dictation, attachments and queueing retain their existing behavior. (CB-238)

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the app image.** These navigation and layout changes introduce no database migration, new page permissions or configuration. Dispatch, computer, supervisor, browser and apps-runtime images need no rebuild for these changes. (CB-237, CB-238)

## v0.18.0 (2026-10-26)

Import reviewed chat skills from public GitHub repositories, show supplied data as inline charts, and subscribe to one future issue-status match. Socios and connected agents can also enumerate the board, read recorded status history and file issues with dependencies.

- Review and pin imported chat skills before installing them (CB-144, CB-248)
- Read inline charts with an accessible table of their supplied data (CB-242)
- Subscribe once when all chosen issues reach a status (CB-243)
- Enumerate issues and inspect their recorded status timeline (CB-241)
- Include dependencies when filing an issue (CB-246)

### New

- **Import reviewed repository instructions.** Admins can preview a public GitHub repository's `SKILL.md` in Settings or through their socio, inspect its complete instructions and source, then install or explicitly update the reviewed revision. Imports pin the resolved commit, verify Git object identities, and retain the source file hash and available root license text. A preview belongs to its reviewing admin, expires after ten minutes and refuses to overwrite a skill changed since preview. Local edits retain their origin; rollback restores the selected revision's content and provenance. Only instructions are imported: referenced files, scripts, dependencies and declared tool permissions are not installed. Private repositories, arbitrary hosts and automatic updates are outside this increment. (CB-144, CB-248)
- **Inline charts in assistant replies.** Socios can format supplied data as a single-series line or bar chart with up to 100 points. Charts retain a normal Markdown summary and table, accessible labels and keyboard readouts; unknown values remain gaps. At most three charts render richly per message, and invalid or incomplete chart fences remain readable code. Formatting does not fetch or verify observations, infer missing history or supply a burndown data source. Chart data cannot execute HTML or JavaScript or fetch external resources. (CB-242)
- **One-shot issue subscriptions.** People can subscribe in Account → Notifications or ask a socio or connected agent to watch 1–20 explicit issue keys. One notice is recorded when all members match the chosen status after a future transition, including a recorded match recovered after restart. Existing matches produce no historical notice. Subscriptions belong to the current person, can be listed and canceled, and use their chosen notification channels and privacy settings. An in-app notice remains available under the existing visibility and retention rules. External delivery is bounded and best effort: saturation, failure or an interrupted attempt does not trigger an automatic retry, and a recorded notice is not proof of device delivery. Saved queries and recurring alerts are outside this increment. (CB-243)
- **Board enumeration and recorded status history.** Socios can list issues with paging, including closed issues, and socios and connected agents can read dated creation, import and status events in recorded sequence. Responses expose paging and completeness indicators. The timeline covers retained records only; it does not invent pre-import transitions, and live issue pages are not a frozen historical snapshot. (CB-241)
- **Dependencies when filing.** Socios and connected agents can supply structured `blockedBy` and `blocks` relationships when filing an issue, within the acting person's existing permissions. Filing and its relationships commit together, with attribution on the resulting history. Dispatch builders receive bounded dependency context as untrusted hints; this does not introduce dependency-based scheduling or grant extra tools. (CB-246)

### Changed

- **Keep imported instructions complete.** Imported descriptions support up to 2,000 characters and instructions up to 64,000; manual skill creation retains its existing limits. A skill that exceeds its share of the selected model's prompt budget is refused with an explanation, including when switching to a fallback model, instead of silently truncating its instructions. (CB-144, CB-248)
- **Keep the iPhone scrolling issue open.** This release does not resolve the reported vertical scrolling jank in the iPhone PWA. (CB-249)

### Upgrading

- **Update dispatch before the app.** Deploy the matching dispatch worker image before the app so it accepts the new dependency fields in builder requests. Keep the existing singleton app and stop-first update policy. Computer, supervisor, browser and apps-runtime images need no rebuild for these changes. (CB-246)
- **Keep a verified pre-upgrade database backup.** SQLite issues advances from 11 to 13 and chat skills from 2 to 3. Existing issue history and skill revisions are preserved. Older images refuse the newer schema versions, so rollback requires restoring the pre-upgrade database with the old image and deliberately recovering later writes; an image-only downgrade will not start. (CB-144, CB-248, CB-243, CB-246)

## v0.17.1 (2026-10-26)

Reduce unnecessary issue-board rendering during app refreshes and avoid page-scroll corrections outside text entry. The reported vertical scrolling jank in the iPhone PWA remains open; the v0.17.0 scrolling description has been corrected.

- Keep unrelated app refreshes from rendering the whole issue board (CB-249)
- Limit page-scroll correction to keyboard adjustments (CB-249)
- Correct the previous release’s mobile scrolling claim (CB-249)

### Changed

- **Keep the board out of unrelated refresh work.** The board reuses its rendered cards when unrelated app state changes. Issue changes, moving, voting and selection still update it. This reduces measured rendering work, but does not establish that the iPhone’s reported vertical jank is fixed. (CB-249)
- **Keep keyboard correction scoped to text entry.** Viewport handling corrects document scroll while a touch keyboard is in use and once when its height is cleared after blur. Ordinary browsing no longer triggers that document-scroll correction. (CB-249)
- **Correct v0.17.0’s description.** That release removed forced horizontal snapping and adjusted layout constraints. It did not resolve the reported vertical scrolling jank in the iPhone PWA. (CB-249)

## v0.17.0 (2026-10-26)

Manage Learning scopes directly and catch up daily analysis after downtime. Review complete schedule instructions and clearer issue cards. Mobile boards no longer force horizontal snap positions; vertical scrolling in the iPhone PWA still has reported jank and remains open.

- Manage Learning scopes and see what analysis needs before it can run (CB-245)
- Review complete instructions before approving a schedule (CB-244)
- Read full issue titles and summaries in chat (CB-247)
- Remove forced horizontal snapping on mobile boards (CB-249)

### New

- **Learning scope controls.** Admins can assign a socio to a new or existing scope and choose whether it uses that scope’s published skills directly from Learning. Changes apply to new conversations; existing evidence keeps its original scope. (CB-245)

### Fixed

- **Recover daily Learning analysis.** The daily check catches up after 02:00 server time when the server or shared model becomes available, keeps a persisted per-scope daily run check, and limits scheduled analysis concurrency. Learning explains missing shared-model configuration and finished-conversation evidence instead of offering ineffective manual runs. (CB-245)
- **Review complete schedule instructions.** Approval cards retain the entire supported 4,000-character schedule prompt and its line breaks before a person approves it. (CB-244)
- **Understand issues from chat.** Standalone issue cards wrap the full title and include a short plain-text description alongside the issue key and existing status, priority, kind and area indicators. (CB-247)
- **Remove forced mobile horizontal snapping.** The issue board no longer forces horizontal snap positions, and column layout constraints were adjusted for short screens. This did not resolve the reported vertical scrolling jank in the iPhone PWA; that problem remains open. (CB-249)

### Upgrading

- **App-only update.** Update the app image; existing supervisor, computer and worker images remain compatible. No schema migrations are added. Daily Learning catch-up uses the existing shared-model configuration and only scopes with new finished-conversation evidence. (CB-245)

## v0.16.0 (2026-10-26)

See resource usage and app OOM counts for each socio’s computer. Telegram acknowledges messages waiting behind a running answer and keeps messages from separate chats distinct.

- Inspect computer CPU, memory, disk and app OOM history (CB-197)
- Receive a Telegram receipt when a message waits for the current answer (CB-223)
- Keep Telegram messages from different chats separate across restarts (CB-240)

### New

- **Computer resource history.** Operations Health shows CPU, memory usage and limit, the computer’s volume space, and retained app OOM attempts for each socio in the selected period. Running computers are sampled alongside server resources; stopped computers stop producing samples. Missing readings remain explicit gaps. (CB-197)

### Fixed

- **Acknowledge waiting Telegram messages.** A linked person’s message received during a running turn gets a quiet reply saying it is queued. The receipt does not enter the model’s conversation, and a failed receipt does not block the queued message. (CB-223)
- **Separate Telegram chat identities.** Message deduplication includes the verified bot and conversation, so chats that reuse a Telegram message number no longer suppress one another. Private, server-signed ingress records prevent rejected retries from entering the next turn after a restart. (CB-240)
- **Revoke access after an observed computer stop.** Authoritative stop observations from resource sampling revoke that computer’s development app and job leases. Supervisor failures and stale observations from before a fresh start do not revoke current leases; promoted apps and other computers retain access. (CB-197)

### Upgrading

- **Update the app and supervisor images together.** Deploy the new supervisor before the app. Existing computer images and containers need no rebuild or recreation; dispatch, browser and apps-runtime images remain unchanged. (CB-197)
- **Allow the first Telegram transition to settle.** For each bot’s first upgraded start, the adapter keeps legacy deduplication for 300,001 milliseconds while recording durable signed ingress. It then uses conversation-scoped identities. The private proof key and transition timestamp persist across ordinary restarts; invalid established proof state fails closed. (CB-240)
- **Keep a verified pre-upgrade database backup.** SQLite operations advances from 2 to 3 and apps from 4 to 5. Existing server resource rows retain their history in the server scope. Older images refuse the new schema versions, so rollback requires restoring the pre-upgrade database with the old image and deliberately recovering later writes. An image-only downgrade will not start. (CB-197, CB-240)

## v0.15.0 (2026-10-25)

See server resource history and missing measurements in Operations Health. Admins can choose how long to keep operational history, with every policy change recorded.

- Inspect server memory, CPU, storage and collection gaps (CB-193)
- Manage retention with audited overrides and server defaults (CB-193)
- Keep operational history longer by default (CB-193)

### New

- **Server resource history.** Operations Health collects process memory and CPU, system load and memory, data-volume space, database pages and WAL size every five minutes, with hourly aggregates. Daily readings add userdata and dataset/file/media sizes. Collection gaps distinguish missing or stale measurements. Incomplete bounded directory scans report unavailable totals instead of partial sizes. (CB-193)
- **Audited retention settings.** Admins and operators can set sampling and retention in Operations Settings, explicitly keep history with 0, or return to environment defaults. Override changes are audited, repeated identical saves are no-ops, and an audit failure rolls the setting back. (CB-193)

### Changed

- **Keep history by default.** Daily page-use aggregates, notification deliveries and resource measurements now default to 400 days. Daily search aggregates, notification records, in-app notices and derived analytics facts/hourly rollups default to no pruning. Independent delivery and notice retention survives parent-notification pruning; raw agent runs are never pruned. Analytics rollups depend on retained derived facts. Longer retention can increase stored operational history. (CB-193)

### Upgrading

- **Update the app and any separate analytics process together.** Child analytics follows its app image automatically. Computer, supervisor, dispatch, browser and apps-runtime images need no rebuild for this change. Explicit saved or environment overrides remain in force. (CB-193)
- **Keep a verified pre-upgrade database backup.** SQLite module versions advance from pages 8 to 9, notifications 1 to 2 and operations 1 to 2. Older images refuse these newer module versions. Rollback therefore requires restoring the pre-upgrade database with the old image and deliberately recovering any later writes; an image-only downgrade will not start. (CB-193)

## v0.14.1 (2026-10-25)

Approval controls fit narrow phone screens even when socios have long names.

- Use approval filters and policies with long socio names on phones (CB-220)

### Fixed

- **Keep Approvals within the available width.** The socio filter shrinks within its card, and policy controls stack on narrow screens while staying side by side on desktop. (CB-220)

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the app image.** Infrastructure and computer images, settings and database schema remain unchanged. (CB-220)

## v0.14.0 (2026-10-25)

Ask your socio to create and update workspace chat skills. Operations health also fits phone screens when long job titles appear, and Settings loads cleanly across time zones.

- Manage workspace chat skills from an admin conversation (CB-231)
- Keep long Operations health rows within phone screens (CB-220)
- Load Settings consistently before showing local timestamps (CB-220)

### New

- **Manage workspace skills in chat.** Socios can list available skills, and admins and operators can read, create and edit workspace skills through their conversations. Changes keep the same attributed revision history as Settings. Built-in skills remain protected, and permissions are checked again when each tool runs, including account deactivation during a turn. (CB-231)

### Fixed

- **Read populated Operations health on phones.** Long run titles and the shared schedule, bar and coverage rows now shrink within the available width on narrow screens. (CB-220)

- **Load Settings across time zones.** Backup and restore status timestamps use the same UTC text on the server and the first browser render, then switch to the reader’s local time without hydration errors. (CB-220)

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the app image.** Computer, supervisor, dispatch and apps runtime images remain unchanged. No database migration or new setting is required. (CB-231)

## v0.13.2 (2026-10-25)

Stopping a socio’s computer now immediately ends the data access granted to its development apps and jobs. Promoted apps keep running independently. Workspace settings and Data tabs also fit narrow phone screens.

- End development app and job data access when their computer stops (CB-226)
- Run TypeScript jobs and Bun package scripts in private computer homes (CB-226)
- Use workspace branding controls and every Data tab on narrow phones (CB-220)

### Fixed

- **Private computer compatibility:** TypeScript jobs execute their approved entry directly, and Bun package scripts work in private homes. Each home, its tokens and the principal registry retain their private permissions. (CB-226)
- **Phone layouts:** branding actions wrap, all six Data tabs remain reachable, and workspace section grids stay inside 320 px and 390 px viewports. (CB-220)
- **Stop-time data leases:** stopping a computer, or observing it stopped, revokes command and job tokens plus development apps’ tokens and connections. Restarting an app issues a fresh lease. Promoted workspace apps and other socios retain their own access. (CB-226)

### Upgrading

- Rebuild the computer image and point the supervisor at `company-brain-computer:b6932d3-isolated-v2`. Replace existing computers while retaining their browser, shared and private volumes; the credential alias stays unchanged. No database migration is needed. (CB-226)

## v0.13.1 (2026-10-25)

Chat notices now follow the model selected for that conversation or socio. A working selected model stays usable when the automatic workspace default is unavailable.

- Use a selected model without a false no-model warning (CB-236)

### Fixed

- **Effective chat model:** the availability notice follows the conversation override, socio default and automatic fallback in that order, including live picker changes. New chats honor a working socio default; unavailable explicit choices show the relevant warning after options load. (CB-236)

## v0.13.0 (2026-10-25)

Commands and files on a shared socio computer are now private to the person using it. Apps and jobs keep separate credentials, while shared files remain available explicitly.

- Keep other people’s commands out of your private files and command tokens (CB-227)
- Share files deliberately through the shared folder (CB-227)
- Run apps and jobs with their own private credentials (CB-227)

### Breaking

- **Computer commands run without root:** each person, app and job gets a separate Linux identity and private home. Existing workspace files remain under `shared/`; system package installation from commands is no longer available. Development app source belongs in `shared/`, with its start command relative to that folder. (CB-227)

### Fixed

- **Concurrent command isolation:** another person’s command cannot read private run files or process environments. App credentials are private to the app, and authorized app logs are read through `app_logs`. (CB-224, CB-226, CB-227)

### Upgrading

- Rebuild and deploy the isolated computer and supervisor images before the application. Existing computers are replaced while retaining browser profiles and the shared workspace; a third volume retains private homes and the UID registry. Back up and restore that private volume as a unit. Old computer images are refused for shell and file work. No application database migration is needed. (CB-227)

## v0.12.1 (2026-10-25)

The workspace sidebar now shows the running server version beneath your workspace name.

- See the running server release tag beside your workspace branding (CB-235)

### Changed

- **Server version beneath the workspace name (CB-235).** The sidebar shows the running server release tag, such as v0.12.1, where the hosting subtitle used to be. It comes from server release metadata and follows deployments while preserving the client's name and logo.

## v0.12.0 (2026-10-25)

See dictation as you speak and manage the files and tables your team shares with its socios. Workspace pages use a consistent layout, and messages keep the right sender across queued, voice and Telegram turns.

- Browse and clean up company socios’ shared data, with attribution and retention controls (CB-229)
- Preview live dictation while speaking (CB-222)
- Use consistent page layouts across the workspace (CB-220)
- Preserve the sender of deferred and queued messages (CB-228)

### New

- **Manage what company socios keep together (CB-229).** Data → Shared with socios lists shared files and tables, their sizes, and who added or changed them when the audit records it. People who have used the socio, plus admins, can download files, browse tables and delete files, tables or individual rows. Deletions affect everyone using that socio and preserve a recovery copy; personal data stays private. Company computer commands can delete files in their own shared area; personal, job and promoted-app tokens cannot. Operations → Knowledge shows admins each shared area's storage. Settings → Limits can expire shared files and datasets after a chosen period without changes; the default is 0, keeping them until someone deletes them.
- **Dictation shows the words as you speak (CB-222).** With "On our server" dictation and a transcription server that streams, such as socio-voice, the words appear above the text box while you talk, as a preview. When you press Done, the whole recording is transcribed, as it is today, and that text goes into your message to edit and send as before (about half a second later; the preview stays meanwhile, marked "Finishing…"). If that fails, the streamed text is used instead. The audio streams through Socio, which passes it to the server's realtime socket at its URL plus `/v1/realtime` with the server's key, so the key never reaches the browser: only over wss (or to this machine), only to that URL, never across a redirect. A session lasts at most two minutes, one per person at a time, and the audio is not stored here (on Cancel, what was said so far has already gone to the transcription server). If the stream can't start or drops, there is no preview, and Done sends the recording all the same, once; OpenAI's URL and servers without the socket record, then send, as today. Admins choose Automatic, On or Off under Settings → General → Transcription → Live text (`VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_STREAMING`). It works in Chrome; it is designed for Safari on iPhone, pending a check on a device. No migration.

### Changed

- **Workspace pages use the Issues page's layout (CB-220).** Data, Media, Memories, Approvals, Apps, Schedules, Activity, Learning, Runs, Socios, Connected agents, a Space's pages, Settings, Operations and the release notes now start like Issues: the module's tile, the title with a line under it and the page's buttons at the right, across the page's whole width, with the content below starting at the same left edge instead of in a centered column. Settings and Operations keep their section nav as a rail at the left, which stays in view as the section scrolls; on a phone its entries wrap in rows above the section instead of scrolling sideways. Lists, tables and dashboards use the width; settings cards stop at a width where forms still read well, and the release notes, a schedule, an approval request and a chat opened from Operations keep a comfortable line length. Home, chats, a page you're writing, the connect screen and Not found keep their layout. Nothing else changes: the same addresses, data and controls.

### Fixed

- **Every message keeps the person who sent it (CB-228).** Queued and steering messages, voice compute requests and Telegram topic messages now carry their original sender into stored history and Operations attribution. A topic message deferred while Socio was paused is not credited to the next speaker. Sending a new message also closes an unanswered page review as skipped, keeping each tool result next to its call; a late answer cannot break subsequent turns.
- **The Access log names what a view was narrowed to.** In Operations → Access log, an entry opened with a socio, person or Space filter showed its raw id ("socio d0b7469d-3f44-…"). It now shows the socio's, person's or Space's name, as the entry's subject does, and the start of the id once that socio, person or Space is gone; so does an entry about one that is gone.

### Upgrading

- **Shared-area audit migration (CB-229).** The data module moves to schema 9. Back up the database and user data before updating. A rollback to an older image requires restoring the matching pre-migration database. No existing data expires by default; `DATA_SHARED_RETENTION_DAYS` or the saved admin limit enables shared-area retention.
- **Rebuild the app, dispatch and apps-runtime images from this release.** Live dictation adds the WebSocket dependency to the lockfile; the dispatch worker checks dependencies against its image.

## v0.11.0 (2026-10-25)

Messages keep the sender who actually sent them, and queued attachments survive a reload. Operators can give a client workspace its own name and logo, while shared-computer apps and jobs no longer inherit a person's private data access.

- Queue messages and files while a socio answers, including after reloading (CB-223)
- Web messages carry the signed-in sender and cannot rewrite history (CB-225)
- Shared computers use restricted data tokens for commands, apps and jobs (CB-224, CB-226)
- Use a client's logo and workspace name in navigation and sign-in (CB-233)
- Connected-agent consent stays readable when a client logo is unavailable (CB-234)

### New

- **Give a client workspace its own name and logo (CB-233).** Operators can save a display name and upload a PNG, JPEG or WebP logo in Settings → Workspace → General. Navigation and sign-in use that identity, and the sidebar no longer shows a hosting sub-label. Deployment defaults can be supplied with `BRAND_NAME` and `BRAND_LOGO`; saved operator choices override them and can be reset. Members and admins cannot change branding.
- **You can queue messages while a socio is answering again, with files too, and after a reload (CB-223).** With an attachment in the message box, the queue button stayed disabled while a turn ran, and the reason was only in a tooltip that a phone never shows, so it looked as if queueing had stopped working. A message with attachments now queues like any other, on a computer and on a phone, and shows as queued with its files. Words queued on their own still reach the socio after its current step. A message with attachments, and anything queued after it, waits for the reply to finish and then goes as the next message, in order, so the socio reads the files just as it reads files you send between replies. Only your own files can be queued, either uploaded in this chat or not yet sent anywhere. While it waits you can edit a queued message down to just its files, or take a file off it. Files waiting in the queue are no longer deleted by the daily cleanup of unsent attachments, for example while Socio is paused; a file whose queued message you cancel is cleaned up as usual. A chat opened or reloaded while a turn runs now shows the turn running, with Stop and Queue, where before it showed only a disabled Send until the reply ended. Whenever the message box can't send or queue what you wrote, the reason now shows as text beside it, such as files still uploading, the chat still connecting, or Socio paused.

### Fixed

- **Development apps and jobs on a company socio's computer no longer inherit their owner's private data access (CB-226).** Their tokens now reach the socio's shared scratch area and can read granted datasets shared with a Space. The data API checks this restriction on every request, including a token created before the restriction. Personal socios and isolated promoted-app grants keep their existing access. This is a scope restriction: operating-system process and credential isolation remains separate work in CB-227.
- **An unavailable connected-agent logo has a neutral fallback (CB-234).** Consent still shows the client's name, trust warning, requested scopes and approval controls when a remote image is blocked or cannot load. This does not enable external image hosts in the content policy.
- **A web chat message is always from the person who sent it (CB-225).** A crafted request could say a message was from someone else, or add an answer, a result or a system message as if the socio had written them. That wrong sender then reached the socio's speaker labels, the admin transcript and the Operations figures. Now the server marks each message with the person signed in, whatever the request says, and keeps only what is new from them: their messages, and the answer to a page review that is waiting. What the conversation already holds always comes from what Socio stored, so a request can't rewrite it either. A message that claims to be a voice call's receipt is refused. Instructions or state that a request sends beside its messages no longer reach the socio either, and the unused suggestions route is closed. Telegram messages are marked the same way, from the linked person.
- **A command on a socio's computer no longer leaves its data token where others can read it, and on a company socio it can't reach a person's data (CB-224).** Each command on a socio's computer used to write the data token of the person it ran for to `.socio/data.env` in the computer's workspace and leave it there, valid for up to an hour after that person's last command. A company socio's computer is shared by everyone who uses that socio, so another person's turn could have read it. Each command now gets a token of its own, revoked when the command returns, in a file that the command deletes before anything else runs. Commands running at the same time on one computer still aren't isolated from each other, so on a company socio that token now reaches only the socio's shared dataset and folder, which everyone who uses the socio can see, and can read datasets shared with a Space; a person's own scratch dataset, folder and private data answer as if they didn't exist. To hand a file to a company socio's computer, the socio copies it to `shared/`. For a person's own data, use the data tools in the chat or a personal socio. The computer file tools refuse token files under `.socio/` and leave them out of listings, and data tokens are redacted from what a computer sends back. On start, the app deletes old token files from running computers; tokens written before this release ended when the app restarted. Mini app tokens (CB-226) and job tokens on a shared computer still act for their owner, and running each person's commands as their own user is CB-227.

### Upgrading

- **Company-computer data access is narrower (CB-226).** Development apps or jobs that used a person's private datasets must use a personal socio or an isolated promoted app with explicit grants. The shared computer can use its socio-owned scratch dataset and read Space-shared datasets.
- **One migration, chat 7, rebuilds the queued-messages table (instant).** An older image refuses the migrated database, so roll back by restoring the pre-deploy backup.

## v0.10.0 (2026-10-22)

Admins get Operations, a dashboard of how Socio is used: activity, value, knowledge, reliability, health and an access log of who viewed whose data. Under the hood, schedules, media, model settings and integrations move to the new storage layer, and the images run Node 24.22.1 with pnpm.

- Operations: a dashboard for admins (CB-184)
- Activity, Value, Knowledge, Capabilities, Reliability and Health (CB-189 to CB-192)
- An access log of who opened whose data (CB-188)
- A key for a self-hosted transcription server (CB-204)
- Long page titles wrap and HTML quotes show as quotes (CB-201, CB-202)

### New

- **Operations, a dashboard for admins (CB-184).** Admins and operators find Operations at the bottom of the sidebar, at /operations: Overview with its tiles (turns, failed turns, active people, messages, chats and tokens, each against the period before), a chart and the turns in the range, plus places for Activity, Value, Knowledge, Reliability, Capabilities, Health, Cost and the Access log, which later releases fill in. A range bar picks 24 hours to all time or custom days, how charts cut them, and shows the filters a click set (a socio, a person, a Space, a source); all of it is in the address, so a link opens the same view for another admin, and Back goes to the view before. Lists load more as you scroll, with a Load more button too, and no page numbers. Days start in one reporting time zone for the whole workspace, UTC unless an admin picks another in Operations → Settings (`OPS_TIME_ZONE` sets the server's). Members don't see it, and the server refuses its API to them. Until the analytics store is running, Overview says so instead of showing numbers.
- **Each turn now records what the Operations dashboard will need (CB-185).** A turn's trace keeps when its request reached the server (so its queue time is known, including the time a Telegram message waits behind its chat's running turn, but not the time an earlier message sat refused while its sender was unlinked or Socio was paused), who or what started it (the person who sent it, a schedule, a dispatch run or Socio itself) and who wrote each message it added, person or socio, so chats with several people and socios can be counted per person. Each model round says what it showed of the model's reasoning: a provider's summary (ChatGPT plans), the model's raw thinking (self-hosted models), reasoning that was counted but not shown, or none, so reasoning is only ever compared within one kind; a turn's kind leaves out its rounds without any reasoning, such as a quick tool step. The page, data, app, schedule and goal tools now give their errors a code, such as `http_404`, that the trace keeps, and a permission error counts as refused. Each scheduled task's run keeps when it came due, so late runs can be seen. Nothing changes on screen, and the socios' system prompt is unchanged. One migration, schedule-tasks 2, adds the due time to task runs; runs from before have none.
- **An analytics store for the Operations dashboard (CB-186).** Operations will read its numbers from a DuckDB file of data copied from the database, `analytics/ops.duckdb` next to it, kept by a process of its own so dashboard queries never slow a chat. By default the app starts that process itself (`OPS_ANALYTICS_MODE=child`); in the stack it can run as its own one-replica `socio-analytics` service, which the app reaches over the internal network with a shared secret (`service`, see docs/OPERATIONS-ANALYTICS.md); `off` runs none. DuckDB never loads extensions, reaches the network or opens other files, and reads the database read-only through Node, never through its own SQLite. The file holds nothing that isn't in the database, so when it is missing, damaged or from another version it is rebuilt from the database. Meanwhile Operations says it is rebuilding and how far it has got: a file from another version (after an upgrade) is never asked, and only an admin's rebuild of a current file keeps answering from it. Its memory (512 MB) and threads (2) come from `OPS_ANALYTICS_MEMORY_MB` and `OPS_ANALYTICS_THREADS`, under what an admin saves. The app image grows by about 94 MB; nothing changes on screen yet, and there is no migration.
- **The analytics store now copies the turns in (CB-187).** The analytics process reads each turn, the messages it added with who wrote them, and its tool calls from the database into its own file, every minute and soon after each turn ends, in short read-only batches that never hold up a chat or the database's writes. It carries on where it stopped after downtime, reading again any turn still running until it ends, and reading something twice changes nothing. Chats with several people and several socios are kept as they are: turns that recorded who started them and wrote each message are credited exactly, older ones to the chat's owner (a team for a shared Telegram topic). Where turns come from is an open list that modules add to (Telegram does), and a source nobody listed still shows under its own name. Hourly and daily totals are kept in the reporting time zone (UTC unless an admin picks another) and cut again from the turns when it changes; hourly totals are kept 30 days (`OPS_HOURLY_DAYS`) and everything else as long as the database keeps the turns (`OPS_FACT_DAYS`, 0). Operations → Overview now shows real numbers from it: its tiles, chart and list of turns, with "Data as of"; with `OPS_ANALYTICS_MODE=off` it still says analytics isn't running. While the store rebuilds after an upgrade, Operations shows how far it has got instead of numbers. At 50,000 turns a rebuild takes about 20 seconds and answers come in under 100 ms. There is no migration.
- **Admins can read any chat from Operations, and every look is on the record (CB-188).** Each turn in Overview's list has Open chat, which shows that chat read-only for an admin, whoever's it is, newest turns first with earlier ones on request; in a Telegram team topic each message names the person who wrote it. Each time an admin opens a chat, the trace of another person's turn (in Runs, or a failure's in Reliability), a list of a person's, a socio's or the workspace's turns, or another section's rows about people (Reliability's recent failures; Health's tokens by person, schedules and long runs; Activity's and Value's people, socios, chats and sessions, and either page narrowed to one person; Knowledge's datasets), the server writes who looked, at what, when, from which page and with which filters to the new Operations → Access log, which every admin sees in full, their own entries too, loading more as it scrolls. A list is written once per admin and filters every 15 minutes, so scrolling adds nothing; opening a chat or a trace is written every time. Nothing can change or remove an entry, the person whose chat was read isn't told, and a view that can't be written isn't shown. Members and service keys are refused. One migration, operations 1, adds the log.
- **Operations → Reliability shows what fails and why (CB-189).** Every failed turn and tool call is now sorted into one of nine categories by rules over the codes it recorded: Permissions and approvals, Quotas and limits, Not available or not set up, Model provider, Other external services, Invalid input, Runtime error, Unclassified (generic codes the rules can't tell apart, shown on their own) and Stopped, which isn't a failure. The page shows the failure rate against the period before, interrupted turns (the server stopped mid-turn) counted apart, retries and fallbacks to the shared model, the failure rate over the range for every failure or one category, each category's share, where failures happen (tools with their p50 and p95 call times, error codes, socios, models, sources), recurring friction (the same failure for 3 or more people, or 5 or more times for one person, in the last 7 days), and the recent failures, loading more as you scroll; opening one shows its turn's trace, names, times and codes only. A category or a tool narrows that list through the address, so the view can be shared. The board's reporting and resolution speed (items filed and closed, median and 90th-percentile time to close, open at the end of the range) is there too. The analytics store rebuilds once to add the categories; a later change to the rules re-sorts the stored codes without a rebuild.
- **Operations → Health (CB-192).** Health shows how long people wait and whether background work keeps up, for the range and filters above: a model's time to first output and tool call times (by model and by tool), how long turns and jobs wait in their queues, how long a job's computer takes to wake, and how late scheduled runs start, as p50 and p95 with a p95 chart over the range. Tokens are broken down by model, socio, person and reasoning kind; each kind is counted on its own, reasoning tokens only where the model reported them, and turns that didn't record a kind show as Unknown. Schedules that are failing, paused or overdue, late or failed runs and long runs (with their dead items) come in lists that load more as you scroll, beside counts of goals that are active, stalled, done or canceled. Collection coverage says how much of the range records each signal, such as runs from before due times were kept. Backups, the continuous copy, the restore drill, this month's dispatch spend against its cap and the analytics process are read live, so they show even while analytics is down or rebuilding, and each copy into the analytics store says when it last ran, marked stale when it falls behind. The analytics store is rebuilt once to add job runs, scheduled runs, schedules, goals, long runs and model rounds; until it swaps in, Health says Rebuilding instead of showing its numbers. There is no migration.
- **Operations → Activity and Value (CB-190).** Activity shows how many people work with socios and how often: active and returning people, stickiness (daily people over the people of the last 7 days), sessions, chats and messages per session, each against the period before, with a chart; the channels people and automation use, kept apart by the source registry; how many chats have several people or several socios; tool calls by capability; and pages made and edited in each Space by people and by socios (a page a socio's tool made, or its draft a person approved, is the socio's; a chat a person saved as a page is theirs). Value shows how people's sessions ended (completed, answered or abandoned, by the design's definitions: completed needs a thumbs up, an approval carried out, a page the socio wrote or a memory kept within a day), chats resumed, the approvals funnel per capability and how many answers got a reaction. Both list people, socios, chats and sessions, last active first and loading more as you scroll; a name narrows the page to that person or socio, and a chat or session links to its transcript. A session ends after 30 minutes without a message, an admin setting in Operations → Settings over `OPS_SESSION_GAP_MINUTES`; sessions are worked out from the messages each time, so a new gap or time zone applies to every range at once. The analytics store now also copies approvals, reactions, page writes and chats' memories (the store rebuilds once).
- **Operations shows Knowledge and Capabilities (CB-191).** Knowledge has each Space's pages (how many, how long, created and edited in the range, and which have gone stale: nobody edited or used them for 90 days), with the pages stalest first; page search counts and times; who did what with datasets, and what was denied; the datasets largest first; what is stored (datasets, their row history, files, chat attachments, app versions and the database, with chat history), now and over the range; and the data space limits in force beside how full they are. Capabilities lists every socio tool by the feature that gives it and the capability that declares it, with its calls in the range, and flags the gaps: tools socios can't discover because no capability declares them, and declared tools this deployment doesn't give. It also lists what socios asked for and couldn't do. Each dataset's sizes are now kept per day (data migration 8) so they can be shown over time, and two new indexes let the analytics process read pages and dataset access without slowing the app (pages migration 8).
- **A key for your own transcription server (CB-204).** An admin can now set a key for the transcription server in Settings → General → Transcription, so a self-hosted speech-to-text server such as `https://stt.example.test` no longer has to accept audio from anyone. The key is sealed under the data key and never reaches the browser: the card shows only whether one is set, with Replace key and Clear key, and password managers aren't offered the field. It is sent only to the URL it was set for, as a bearer token, never to OpenAI unless that is the URL, and never across a redirect, which is refused and reported as such. Saving a new URL clears it, so set it again for the new server; a key from a tab that still shows an older URL is refused. If `VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_URL` changes later, the key stays with the old URL and isn't sent. Any key, this one or `VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_API_KEY` (still the server's default, also as a `_FILE` secret), now goes only over https or to this machine: with a key for a plain-http URL to another machine, server dictation there is off, the server warns at start, and the card and the error say what to change, where before the key went in the clear. One migration, core 7, adds the table the key is kept in.

### Changed

- **The repository uses pnpm (CB-183).** Dependencies install with pnpm 10 from pnpm-lock.yaml, which replaces package-lock.json with the same versions. package.json names the pnpm to use (`packageManager`, which corepack reads) and the Node and pnpm it needs (`engines`: Node 24.22.1 or later within 24, pnpm 10.15 or later within 10; `.nvmrc` and `.node-version` name Node 24.22.1), and an install on any other stops at once. The images, CI and the dispatch worker's gate install and run with pnpm, and `pnpm run release` bumps only package.json. Commands are now `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm run dev`, `pnpm test` and so on (docs/SETUP.md). Nothing changes in the app.

### Fixed

- **A long page title now wraps instead of being cut off on a phone (CB-202).** A title like "Hugging Face Hub OpenUSD support — post and DFM analysis" showed as "Hugging Face Hub Ope" at the right edge of the page editor on a narrow screen. The title now wraps onto more lines and the field grows with it, with no sideways scroll; the header's breadcrumb still shortens it to one line. It still behaves as a one-line title: Enter moves to the page, and pasted line breaks become spaces. On a wide screen nothing changes.
- **A quote written as HTML now shows as a quote on a page (CB-201).** A page with a `<blockquote>` (and `<br>`) from a socio showed the tags as text and opened only in Markdown source. It now opens in the visual editor with a proper quote block, keeping links, emphasis, line breaks and nested quotes, and the draft review card shows it as a quote too. Nothing is rewritten until you edit the page; then the quote is saved as `>` lines. Other HTML, such as scripts, iframes, styles or tags with attributes, is still never shown or run, and such a page still opens in source mode. Socios are now told to write quotes as Markdown, and a `<blockquote>` they write is saved as Markdown.

### Upgrading

- **The images move to Node 24.22.1, the latest LTS (CB-183).** Every image built from this repository (app, browser, dispatch, apps runtime) and CI's image now use `node:24.22.1-bookworm-slim`, pinned by digest, instead of 24.21.0; rebuild them all from the release's tag. In a checkout, use Node 24.22.1 (`nvm use` reads `.nvmrc`): package.json's engines now ask for it, and an older Node fails the install.
- **Rebuild the app and dispatch images in the same release (CB-183).** Build the app, dispatch and apps images from the release's tag (`--only app,dispatch,apps`) and deploy them together. The images now install with pnpm, and the dispatch worker checks pnpm-lock.yaml, which an older dispatch image doesn't have: an older worker refuses every run of the new code. In a checkout, run `corepack enable pnpm`, remove node_modules, then `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`.

## v0.9.0 (2026-10-22)

Under the hood: schedules and jobs, media, model settings and integrations move to the new storage layer. Deleting a conversation is now all-or-nothing, and conversation titles never save after shutdown. Nothing else should look different.

- Schedules, jobs and goals on the new storage layer (CB-110)
- Media, model settings and integrations on it too (CB-110)
- Deleting a conversation is all-or-nothing (CB-110)

### Changed

- **Schedules, goals and jobs moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Scheduled tasks and their runs and history, the conversation each schedule runs in, goals and their comments, jobs with their versions and runs, and jobs that run until done with their items and set-aside items are now read and written through the Scheduling context's own repositories on the same data layer as the board. Each change is one short write transaction that waits its turn behind another in progress: a job's start, a run's status, a goal with its schedule, and a long run's progress. Deleting a conversation now hides it and stamps its scheduled tasks in one transaction, so a failure leaves all of them as they were instead of hiding the tasks alone. Nothing changes on screen. One baseline migration, schedule-tasks 1, is adopted over the tasks tables as they are, with no data change.
- **Attachments and media folders moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Chat attachments (including Telegram files and socios' screenshots) and the Media page's empty folders are now read and written through Media's own repositories on the same data layer as the board, and Media asks your data space for files instead of reading its tables. Binding a message's attachments, adding a screenshot, removing an unsent attachment and the hourly clean-up now wait their turn behind another change in progress instead of landing inside it, and a folder rename still moves its files, empty folders and folder grants together or not at all. Storage, image limits, who can see what and what you see are unchanged. No migration.
- **Model settings moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Shared model backends, the default backend, the subscriptions people may connect and their models, each person's own connections and the response times behind the wait estimates are now read and written through the same data layer as the board, with the reads every turn makes prepared once.
