Adding or removing a backend, saving the model settings and the first start's backend from the environment are each one transaction, and a refreshed sign-in is saved only while the person is still connected. Keys stay sealed and never leave the server; the model picker, the fallback to the shared model and retries are unchanged. No migration.
- **Telegram links, web reads, computers and custom domains moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Telegram account links and their one-time codes, Telegram conversations and group members, each socio's web identity and web-read audit, computer permissions and audit, and the custom domains' hostnames and operations are now read and written through the same data layer as the board. A Telegram conversation's new chat is created and bound in one transaction, so two messages at once can't both create it; a code is still redeemed only once and expires after ten minutes. Setting a socio's web identity saves it with its audit line in one transaction, and computer and web audit lines still wait behind another change without holding up the action. Their migrations moved, unchanged, into one place; there is no new migration and nothing changes on screen.

### Fixed

- **A conversation's automatic title no longer outlives a shutdown (CB-110).** Stopping the server now cancels a title still being asked for and waits for it, so the last conversation's title request never reaches the model, or saves a title, after the data is closed. Titles asked for while the server runs are unchanged.

## v0.8.0 (2026-10-22)

Under the hood: the board, memories, approvals, dispatch and notifications move to a new storage layer that keeps writes from colliding when a socio does several things at once. Nothing should look different.

- New storage layer for the board (CB-110)
- Memories, approvals, dispatch and notifications on it too (CB-110)
- Parallel socio actions can't undo each other's writes (CB-110)

### Changed

- **The Improvements board has a new storage layer under it (CB-110).** The board's issues, comments, files, history, votes, triage, socio requests and the record of what socios and connected agents did are now read and written through one data layer (Drizzle ORM on the same SQLite database), with every change made in one short write transaction and anything that reacts to it (triage, dispatch) run only after it is saved. A socio request's "done" or "won't fix" notice to the people who asked is now written in its own transaction just after the status change is saved, rather than in the same one: if saving the notice failed, the status change would stay and the notice would be missing, where before neither would have been saved. The app's other writes on the same database (settings, schedules, goals, approvals, pages, profiles, memories, your data space, notifications, Telegram links, dispatch runs, job runs, skill sessions, queued chat messages, mini app deletions, learning analysis, and the computer and web audit records) now wait their turn behind a board change in progress instead of running in the middle of it, so a socio filing an issue while it creates a page, saves a memory or sets up a schedule in the same step no longer fails with "cannot start a transaction within a transaction", and a board change that fails can no longer take another tool's change with it. Nothing changes on screen. One migration, issues 11, gives each issue history row its own order number, filled in for existing rows, so history and connected agents' change feeds keep their order without relying on SQLite's internal row ids.
- **Memories, profiles and time zone notes are on the new storage layer too (CB-110).** A person's memories, their profile and the notes of a detected time zone that moved are now read and written through the same data layer as the board, each change in one short write transaction: a socio proposing a memory checks for the same words and saves it in one step, so two at once can no longer both save it. They are their own part of the database now, with a baseline migration, memories 1, that is adopted over the tables as they are, with no data change. Nothing changes on screen.
- **Approvals moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Approval requests, decisions, auto reviews, policies, guidance, the approval audit and the notes a socio carries on with are now read and written through the same data layer as the Improvements board, each change in one short write transaction, and the admins' socio activity reads the approval audit through the approvals service. Deciding, undoing, changing a policy or your guidance, a deleted socio's cleanup and how a socio carried on after a decision now wait their turn behind another change in progress, as requests already did, instead of failing or landing inside it. Nothing changes on screen, and there is no migration.
- **Dispatch moves onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Dispatch runs, their logs, executor positions, the "Agent active" switch and the GitHub publishing settings are now read and written through dispatch's own repositories on the same data layer as the board, and each run's issue number and title still come from the board. A run's start, usage and end are each saved as one transaction, and a GitHub connect step can only be used once even when two callbacks race. Nothing changes on screen, and there is no migration.
- **Notifications moved onto the new storage layer (CB-110).** Notifications, where each one went, in-app notices, notification images, push subscriptions and the push key are now read and written through the same data layer as the board. A socio's notification, its image and its in-app notice are saved in one transaction together with the check of today's limits, even when it has to wait behind another change, and the hourly clean-up of old notifications now runs as one transaction. Delivery to devices and Telegram, the daily limits, private mode and what you see are unchanged. No migration.

### Fixed

- **Release notes test no longer breaks on every release (CB-165, CB-178).** It keeps the history up to v0.6.1 as a fixed tail, checks the newest release against package.json, and cuts its trial release from a copy with its own Unreleased section. `npm run release` now runs it before committing and undoes the release if it fails.

## v0.7.0 (2026-10-22)

Socio now has version numbers, release notes and a What's new feed, says socio everywhere, and keeps what you queue when you press Stop. Asking a socio to remember something about you saves it right away.

- Release notes and What's new (CB-166, CB-168)
- Socio wording everywhere (CB-163)
- A socio's card on Home opens its home page (CB-167)
- Stop keeps your queued messages (CB-170)
- Remember something about yourself without an approval card (CB-172)

### New

- **Socio has version numbers (CB-165).** Every update is now a release with a version, a date, a short summary and its highlights, starting with the first release as v0.1.0; each update since has its own, up to v0.6.1. Settings shows the version you are on, with its commit, and `/healthz` says the version too. `npm run release` cuts the next release: it works out the version from Unreleased (anything new makes a minor release, only changes and fixes a patch; a breaking change makes a major one, but while Socio is before 1.0 a minor one, and 1.0 itself comes only when asked for with `--major --yes`), moves the notes under it with its summary and highlights, bumps package.json, commits and tags it, and never pushes. See "Cut a release" in deployment/portainer/README.md.
- **Release notes in the app (CB-168).** **Release notes** in the account menu, and the version under Settings, open a page with every release, newest first: its version, date, summary and highlights, and its full notes, with each issue key linking to its issue. `/releases/v0.5.0` opens at that release. It works on a phone and in dark mode.
- **"Remember that" about yourself saves at once (CB-172).** Ask your socio to remember something about yourself ("remember I prefer metric units") and it is saved right away, with no approval to give; the reply says so with a link to **Memories**, where it shows as yours ("You asked Mingo to remember it in Planning") and you edit or forget it as any other. What a socio infers, or anything about other people, still waits for your approval. A socio can't skip approval on its own: your own message must ask, in a chat of yours, in a few words of your own, about you and no one else (no one named or close to you, such as "Bob is…" or "my wife…"), in English or Spanish. Text you quote, fence, or ask to have translated or summarized doesn't count, nor do the notes Socio writes into a chat when you decide an approval or a job ends. Adds the `asked` option to `propose_memory`. No migration.
- **How much a reply recalls is a setting (CB-173).** **Settings → General → Socio replies → Recalled context per reply** sets about how much of your memories goes with each message (250 to 8,000 tokens), and the pinned facts of a long chat that match it grow and shrink in step. It starts at `TURN_RECALL_TOKENS`, or 1,000 tokens, the amount used until now. Only the context sent with each message changes: the socios' system prompt stays the same, so the model's prompt cache keeps working.
- **What's new in Socio, on Home and in the header (CB-166).** Home lists the releases as cards, newest first, each with its version, day, a short summary and up to three highlights linked to their issues; older ones load as you scroll, and a link goes to all releases. The header has a **What's new** button (the gift), on phones too, with the same list; a dot on it means a release came out since you last opened it, and opening it clears the dot. Your last seen release is kept on the server, so the dot follows you to other devices; people who join start at the running version, so older releases aren't new to them. For support, the app also keeps when each person was first shown each release, and admins see it under **People → Releases seen**. On a phone the theme switch is in the account menu only, so a chat's title keeps its room. If the release notes can't be read, Home leaves the section out and the button shows no dot.
- **Mini app logs are kept for 14 days or the last 50 attempts, and only owners and admins read them (CB-175).** Each build and start of a promoted mini app keeps its log for 14 days or while it is one of the app's last 50 attempts, whichever keeps more; older ones are removed in the background, about hourly, and the attempt running now never is. Admins change both numbers under Settings, Mini app logs (`APPS_LOG_DAYS` and `APPS_LOG_ATTEMPTS` are the starting values). Only the app's owner and workspace admins can read the logs, in the Logs dialog, the API and `app_logs`; anyone else gets a refusal (403). An app keeps at most 500 attempts whatever the settings say, and the count can't be set below 10, the runtime's own memory. No migration.

### Changed

- **Your detected time zone follows you when you travel (CB-176).** When your time zone was detected from your browser and your browser now reports another, Socio switches to the new one and says so once, quietly, in your next chat and on the Memories page: "Your time zone is now Europe/Madrid (detected from your browser)." A time zone you set yourself is never changed, and two browsers that name the same zone differently (Asia/Calcutta and Asia/Kolkata) don't count as a move. Times already written in a chat keep their words; new ones use the new zone. Core migration 6 adds the note's table.
- **Stop keeps your queued messages, and a chat queues up to 5 (CB-170).** Pressing **Stop** while messages wait for your socio no longer risks losing track of them: they stay in the chat with **Edit** and **Cancel** until the stopped reply has ended, then go as the next turn, in order and once, and the chat now shows that turn as it runs even when it was still showing the stopped one. Messages left waiting while Socio was paused go as soon as an admin resumes it, not only at the next restart. At most 5 messages can wait per chat (20 before); an admin can set 1 to 20 under Settings → General → Socio replies → **Queued messages per chat**, over `STEERING_MAX_QUEUED`. With the queue full, a quiet note says so and your text stays in the message box. No migration.
- **Signing out warns about recent unsent drafts (CB-171).** If you sign out (or switch user) with unsent drafts less than a day old, Socio first says how many and in which chats (the first few, then "and N more"), and asks **Sign out anyway** or **Cancel**. Older drafts are still cleared quietly. **Sign out everywhere** in Account settings does not ask: it has already ended every session when it finishes, so there is nothing left to cancel.
- **Releases are easier for agents to cut (CB-178).** `npm run -s release -- --dry-run --json` (and a real run with `--json`; `-s` keeps npm's banner out of the output) prints one JSON object, also for a wrong option, with the version, why that bump, the sections and what is missing, and errors say what to fix. `npm run release:check` validates Unreleased (summary, highlights, issue keys, no "Dot" wording, sections); `--notes-only` skips the summary for CI. `npm run release -- --draft-summary` lists the highlights to rewrite. docs/RELEASING.md covers the rules (never 1.0 without the owner, one release per deploy, when images need rebuilding, never push without the owner's go), linked from the README and CONTRIBUTING.md.
- **Steadier database writes (CB-110).** The app now opens its database with `synchronous=NORMAL` (safe with WAL: a power cut can lose the last moments of writes but never damages the file) and, only when `LITESTREAM_REPLICA_DIR` is set, leaves checkpoints to Litestream so the continuous copy never races the app. Writes also take the database lock when they start and wait up to five seconds for it, so two overlapping processes during a deploy no longer fail with "database is locked". Nothing to change; without Litestream the WAL checkpoints as before.
- **The Socio rename reaches the rest of the app (CB-163).** Screens, toasts, errors, notifications, tool descriptions, the socios' own prompts and the setup docs now say "socio" and "socios" where they still said "Dot" and "Dots". Existing chats, settings and API fields are unchanged; the socios' system prompt text changes once, so the model's prompt cache restarts once.
- **A new workspace's first socio is called Sebas (CB-177).** The socio created in a brand-new workspace is named Sebas, one of the mascot cast, instead of Socio, and it wears the Sebas look. Existing workspaces and their socios are unchanged.

### Fixed

- **"Remember that" about someone unnamed now waits for approval (CB-172).** A memory that says something about another person in a relative clause ("I manage an intern who is pregnant", "I live with bob who is diabetic", "alguien que tiene epilepsia"), or names a generic person such as an intern, someone or the new hire, no longer saves at once, whether or not the name is capitalized; it goes to you for approval like any memory about other people.
- **A failed first /grill-me turn ends the skill (CB-174).** If the first turn of a `/grill-me` session fails (a model error or the time limit), the skill session ends with a short line saying so, and your next message starts fresh instead of carrying on a half-started interview. Stopping a turn yourself does not end it. Also, the `⟦Name wrote:⟧` speaker labels, which are for the model only, are now taken off any answer where a model writes one, so they never show in a chat bubble, after a reload, in a copy or in Telegram. No migration.
- **A socio on Home opens its home page (CB-167).** On Home, selecting a socio's card, name or picture now opens that socio's home page (its chats, goals and schedules) instead of starting a new chat. Each card has a separate **New chat** button beside it, large enough to tap on a phone, for the old one-step way. No migration.

### Upgrading

- **Rebuild the dispatch image once, with this release (CB-165).** The dispatch worker checks that a checkout's package-lock.json matches the one in its image, and every release now bumps the project's version in that file. The worker now leaves the project's own version out of the check, so later releases don't need the dispatch image rebuilt; but this release's lockfile says 0.6.1 instead of 0.1.0, and an older worker compares the whole file, so until the dispatch image is rebuilt (`node scripts/build-images.mjs --only dispatch`) and `DISPATCH_IMAGE_TAG` updated, every dispatch run fails with "Dependencies changed since the dispatch image was built". No migration.

## v0.6.1 (2026-10-22)

Socios can now see the screenshots they take and tell you the time in your own time zone. Sending a message feels steadier, and chats fit a phone better.

- Socios see their screenshots (CB-157)
- Times in your own time zone (CB-159)
- A message you send stays in place until it is taken (CB-154)
- The composer stays above an iPhone's keyboard (CB-155)
- The dataset page fits a phone (CB-162)

### Fixed

- **Long runs resumed after a restart no longer fail before the app is listening (CB-161).** Jobs that run until done now resume only once the server is listening, plus a short settle. Each run of a job also waits up to 45 seconds for the app to answer before the job starts, and a job's own calls are left as they are. A batch that fails only because the app wasn't up yet, soon after it started and with nothing done, does not count as a retry in a row and charges none of its items a try. Your socios just keep going.
- **The dataset page fits a phone (CB-162).** On a phone the dataset's Settings card and table chips ran past the right edge and the page slid sideways, because the page's one-column grid grew to its widest content. The cards now stay as wide as the screen, Retention and Shared with stack on narrow screens, long table names wrap in their chips, and wide rows scroll inside their own table. The `mobile-fit` smoke scenario now also checks a dataset, a run's trace, the backend editor and the Account, Inference, Notifications and People pages at 390 px.
- **A new chat's message box stays at the bottom, with the suggestions above it (CB-160).** On a socio's new-chat screen the suggestions (Help me think this through, Research a public page, Make a plan I can follow) now sit just above the message box instead of under it, and the box is anchored at the bottom as in a chat, so it no longer floats mid-screen on a phone or moves when what is above it changes, and it is as tall as what it holds, with no blank space under its buttons. The socio's welcome and the suggestions scroll on their own when the screen is short, and while you type above a phone's keyboard they give way without moving the box.
- **A message you send stays in place, dimmed until it is taken (CB-154).** Sending no longer made your message vanish while its request was out, with only the "Sending to…" status line in its place, and then come back, so the chat jumped twice. The chat added your message to a list it only re-read once the turn started; it now shows it at once in its place, dimmed until the server takes it, then at full strength, and its action row is there from the start, so nothing moves. A message the server refuses, or that you stop before it starts, used to disappear (or, while Socio was updating, went back into the box); it now stays where it was, dimmed, with **Not sent**, **Retry** (the same message, sent once) and **Edit** (back into the box), and its text stays in the chat's draft (up to five such messages), so a reload puts it back in the box after what you typed. One the server turns out to have is shown as sent, and Retry never sends it twice. A message too large to send still goes back into the box. A queued message (CB-151) has the same dimmed look.
- **The whole composer stays above an iPhone's keyboard (CB-155).** In the home-screen app and in Safari, a long message pushed the composer's buttons and Send behind the keyboard's ⌃ ⌄ ✓ bar: the transcript kept 100 px and both headers stayed, so once the text box grew the composer ran past the bottom of what was visible and was cut off. Now the transcript gives way first, down to nothing; when the keyboard leaves less than 400 px the workspace header hides while you type a message, and below 300 px (a phone in landscape) the chat's header and most padding too, so the text box, its buttons and Send always fit, and the text box scrolls once it is full. The visible height is also measured again as you type and a second after focus, in case the bar comes after the keyboard. Other fields and desktops don't change.
- **Socios tell you the time in your own time zone (CB-159).** Socios gave people times in UTC ("your 08:45 message") while the app showed local times, because your time zone never reached them. Each turn now knows your zone: your profile's, else your browser's, which the web app sends with each chat request and saves to your profile when it has none (marked **detected from your browser** on the **Memories** page, where you can change it), so Telegram, scheduled and job turns use it too; UTC only when neither is known, and the socio says so. The current time and new date-and-time notes along a chat give your local time with the zone and its UTC offset, schedule and job times (stored in UTC) are translated, and in a group chat each person's turn tells them the time in their own zone while what the group's later turns see stays in UTC, unless they shared their profile with team chats. The system prompt stays the same, byte for byte, for every zone and day, and earlier notes in a chat keep their wording.
- **Socios see their screenshots (CB-157).** `computer_screenshot` gave the model its PNG as 1.7 MB of base64 text: the model never saw an image, fitting cut it to an excerpt that meant nothing, and a socio checking a page or playing a game in the browser was answering blind. A screenshot is now a JPEG (the computer's 1280×800 view, at quality 80, about a tenth of the size) stored as an attachment of the chat, in the person's files under `attachments/`, and its result names it instead of holding its bytes, so history stays small. A model that can see images gets it as an image, right after the result; only the newest images go (the model's image limit, 4 by default, the person's photos counted, each within `MEDIA_IMAGE_MODEL_MAX_MB`), on the shared model too after a switch to it, and older ones become a short note. Past the image limit, each newer image turns the oldest one shown into a note, which changes the request from that image onward, so the prompt cache holds only up to it; the note then stays the same. A model without vision gets a note with the image's id and size and is pointed to `computer_snapshot`; `attachments_view` shows an older image again. Screenshots older versions stored as base64 (such as a Reversi game's) become a short note in the prompt; stored history isn't changed. In a turn's steps and on **Runs**, a screenshot shows as a thumbnail that opens at full size, to people who can read the chat. Deploy runs one migration (media 4, which rebuilds the attachments table: instant). Rebuild the computer image (`company-brain-computer:b6932d3-browser-limits-git`, same tag) for JPEGs, then Stop and Start each computer to get it; until then screenshots still work, as PNGs.

## v0.6.0 (2026-10-22)

You can keep writing to a socio while it works, and what you type but haven't sent is never lost. Long chats with screenshots work again.

- Steer a socio while it works (CB-151)
- Unsent messages wait for you (CB-153)
- Report a problem from chat with /report (CB-152)
- Chats with screenshots no longer stop at "Request is too large" (CB-156)
- Page chats show your words as you typed them (CB-158)

### New

- **Unsent messages wait for you (CB-153).** Text you typed but didn't send stays in its chat's box when you switch to another chat, while a reply is still running, after a reload or a reconnect, and is there again when you come back; each chat keeps its own, and so does each Dot's new-chat box. It is never sent for you. It goes once the message is sent, or when you delete the text; a message that never reached the server (an update or a lost connection) comes back to the box even if you left the chat meanwhile, and after a reload one that may not have arrived is checked and given back if it didn't. Drafts are text only (attachments stay in the box you added them to while it is open), are kept in this browser only, not on your other devices, per person, and are cleared when you sign out or someone else signs in on the browser. No migration.
- **Steer a Dot while it works (CB-151).** While a Dot's turn runs in the web chat you can keep writing: the composer keeps **Stop** and adds a send button that queues your message, which shows faded at the end of the chat with **Edit** and **Cancel** until the Dot takes it. The Dot reads it at its next safe point, once its current tool calls have finished and before its next model round, as your message, and can change course; a tool call is never interrupted. If the turn ends first, your queued messages start the next turn. The queue is kept on the server, so a reload keeps it, and each message reaches the Dot once, through a busy model's retries, a fallback to the shared model or a compaction. The system prompt and each round's prompt before it stay the same, for the prompt cache. Telegram is unchanged. See "Messages while the Dot works" in docs/SETUP.md. Deploy runs one migration (chat 5: a new table).
- **Report a problem from chat with `/report` (CB-152).** Start a message with `/report` (and what went wrong, if you like) and your socio asks a few short questions: what happened, what you expected, where, and any screenshot to attach. It checks the Improvements board for the same problem, then shows you the issue it would file and files it only when you say yes, or adds your evidence or vote to the item that already covers it. You get the link and the skill ends. A socio that isn't allowed to file issues says so instead. There is no `/bug` alias yet.

### Fixed

- **Page chats show your words as you typed them (CB-158).** In a chat about a page, each message you sent began with a "From" line naming the page, though the banner above already says "Working on" that page. Messages are now sent and stored exactly as typed, the page shows only in the banner, and your socio still gets the page with each turn, beside your message. Older messages hide that line when shown; what is stored is unchanged. Skills such as `/grill-me` and `/report`, and messages you queue while your socio works, are sent as typed too.
- **Chats with screenshots no longer stop at "Request is too large." (CB-156).** After a Dot took a screenshot in its browser, every later message in that chat failed with that banner and Reconnect did nothing: the browser sent the whole conversation with each message, a 1.7 MB screenshot included, and the app refused any request over 1 MB before the chat's compaction could help. A chat now sends only what the server doesn't have yet, usually just your new message, and the server takes the rest of the conversation from what it stored, whole, so the Dot sees the same history and recall still finds everything. A tab opened before this update is taken too: once you're signed in, a chat's requests may now be up to 32 MB, though each new message is still at most about 1 MB. If a message you write is ever too long to send, the chat says so calmly and puts its text back in the box instead of showing an error.

## v0.5.1 (2026-10-21)

Steadier mini apps: a new version starts only once its install has finished, and the first install after an update waits for the network proxy. Only the apps runtime and dispatch changed; the app itself is the same as v0.5.0.

- A mini app starts only once its install has finished (CB-150)
- The first install after an update waits for the proxy (CB-149)
- The proxy closes connections a client left open (CB-150)

### Fixed

- **A promoted app starts only once its install has finished; the egress proxy closes tunnels a client left (CB-150).** An owner who pressed Start while the app runtime was still waiting for the egress proxy or installing a new version's dependencies could launch that version without them. A version can now start only once its install has succeeded: a Start meanwhile waits for the install to finish (one install, not two), and a failed install leaves nothing to start. The egress proxies (the apps' and dispatch's) also left the connection to an allowed site open when a client went away while the apps' proxy was still looking up the site's address; they now close it whenever either side goes. What the proxies allow, refuse and log is unchanged. The apps runtime and dispatch worker images must be rebuilt.
- **A promoted app's first install after a deploy waits for the egress proxy (CB-149).** When the stack was redeployed, the app runtime could install an app's dependencies a few seconds before the new egress proxy was reachable by its name (the stack only routes to it once it is healthy), so `bun install` failed at once with `FailedToOpenSocket` and the app waited a minute or more for the next try. The proxy settings and credentials the install gets were never wrong. The runtime now waits up to a minute (`APPS_RUNTIME_PROXY_WAIT_SECONDS`) for the proxy to take connections before it installs, saying so in the build's log, and says plainly when it never does. The egress proxies (the apps' and dispatch's) also no longer stop when a client resets its connection as it is refused, as Bun does when it retries a download.

## v0.5.0 (2026-10-21)

When a mini app fails to build or start, you and its socio can now see why. Pages move between Spaces, and chats work with more self-hosted models.

- A log for every build and start of a mini app (CB-146)
- Move pages between Spaces (CB-142)
- Chats work on models that need the roles to take turns (CB-147)
- The rest of the app on the new components (CB-98)

### New

- **Why a promoted app's version failed, in its own log (CB-146).** When a version of a promoted mini app fails to build or start, its Dot can now read why with `app_logs`: every build and every start of every version keeps its own last lines and how it ended (exit code, signal, out of memory, never answered, build failed), with secrets taken out, and they outlast a restart of the app runtime. Before, one log mixed every version, and after a failed update the restart of the previous version buried the new version's error, so a Dot kept guessing and sending updates. On **Apps**, a promoted app's card says when its last start failed, and **Logs** shows each attempt on its own. Only the app's owner, and the Dot that built it for them, can read its logs. Deploy runs one migration (apps 4); the apps runtime image (`company-brain-apps`) must be rebuilt and shipped with the app.
- **Move pages between Spaces (CB-142).** A page's ⋯ menu has **Move to…**: pick another Space and the page goes there, with its subpages, instead of being copied and deleted by hand. It keeps its id, text, history, conversations and review receipts, and old links to it open it in its new Space. You need to be able to edit pages in both Spaces; a Dot can do the same with the new `move_space_page` tool, only between Spaces it was granted and for a person who could move the page. Moves are recorded in the workspace audit. The old in-Space move is now **Move within Space**. No migration.

### Changed

- **The rest of the app on the new components (CB-98).** Sign-in, the loading and error screens, page headings, "not found", the paused banner, a chat's messages (their Markdown, tables, attachments, call receipts and the turn's trace), the computer pane, the research brief and the computer screen in a chat are now styled with the shared components and Tailwind, and look as they did in light and dark (a pixel comparison of every view at 1440 and 390 px). The legacy stylesheet is down to the plain elements' base rules and the page frame. Three things change on purpose: the computer pane opened over a page (Show computer) has the same header as beside a chat, so its tabs are no longer 10 px; Platform is a Settings page with the section menu, its backend editor too; and focusing a field on a computer with a mouse no longer restyles the whole page, which a phone's keyboard still needs, so a click into a page's text gets its caret about 20 ms sooner.

### Fixed

- **Chats work on models that need the roles to take turns (CB-147).** Strict chat templates (llama.cpp with Gemma- or Mistral-style ones) refuse two user or two assistant messages in a row, and a chat could send them: a compacted chat's notes went as a message of their own before your first kept message, a failed or stopped turn left your message with no answer, several people wrote in a team chat before the socio answered, a turn stored only its reasoning, and the server's own messages (skills' answers, scheduled turns' and jobs' reports, busy-model notices) sat next to the socio's. Now one last step before every request merges them: back-to-back messages from people become one, with the notes, the turn's note and the time at its start, once each, and, when they are from more than one person in a team chat, each person's words after a label with their name (as it was when they wrote, and numbered when two share one) in brackets no one can type, so no one can pass off their words as someone else's; back-to-back answers become one, up to the next tool call, which stays where it was; and a request that starts or ends with an answer gets a short fixed line from Socio before or after it. It depends only on the stored chat, so the prompt stays the same, byte for byte, from turn to turn and the prompt cache keeps working.

## v0.4.0 (2026-10-21)

Socios remember what you approve, long jobs keep going until they are done, and chat skills such as /compact and /grill-me arrive. Promoted mini apps start again.

- Memories that follow you, with your approval (CB-106)
- Jobs that run until done (CB-122)
- Chat skills: /compact, /grill-me and your own (CB-143)
- Dispatch opens draft pull requests on GitHub (CB-32)
- Promoted mini apps start again (CB-148)

### New

- **Memories that follow you, with your approval (CB-106).** Your socios now know you the same way in every chat: **Memories** has a profile you write (what to call you, your role, time zone, how you like answers, standing instructions) and the memories in use, each saying where it came from and when you last confirmed it. A socio can propose a memory when you mention something lasting (a preference, a decision, a fact pinned in a long chat's notes); it waits under **Waiting for you** and is used only once you press **Remember**, and you can edit it first or say **Not now**. Edit, confirm (**Still true**) or forget any memory, or clear your profile, at any time: socios stop using it at once. Each turn gets your profile and the memories that bear on your message, with your message, never in the system prompt, so the prompt cache keeps working, and always as your data, which can't change a socio's rules, permissions or approvals. Nobody else's turn ever gets yours, and in a chat with other people (a Telegram team topic or group) socios use only what you marked **Use in team chats**, off by default. See docs/MEMORY.md.
- **Jobs that run until done (CB-122).** A long collection or enrichment job no longer stops for good when its computer stops, the server restarts or a deploy lands, and is no longer "succeeded" because one run exited cleanly. A Dot saves such a job with `untilDone`; the server, not the computer, then owns it as a long run in the database and keeps starting batches (each within the job's timeout) until the job says it is complete: its whole list of items declared and none left, or an explicit `complete`. Through a checkpoint API at `$CB_JOB_API` a batch declares and claims items, marks each done once (with an upsert, so a redo after a stop leaves no duplicates), fails bad ones (retried, then set aside with their error while the rest go on), keeps a cursor, and on a 403 or 429 pauses instead of hammering; the long run waits and resumes. A stopped batch's items go back at no cost, a restart resumes open long runs from their checkpoint, and a deploy no longer waits for them. A job that crashes on every batch stops as failed after its retries, each waiting twice as long, instead of setting good items aside one by one, even when its checkpoint changes every batch; a retry waits for the next item due, and a 403 or 429 wait holds across a stop or a restart; a long run whose items were mostly set aside ends as failed, and one with some set aside says it is done "but not all of it". Schedules & jobs shows "412 of 726 done" and a Progress panel (items, batches, last checkpoint, retries, the wait, failed items, outcome, Stop); the Dot's new `job_status` answers "how is it going?"; at the end the Dot reports in the chat that started it, or the owner gets a notification. Tries per item, retries in a row and batches per run are in **Settings → General → Schedules and jobs** (`JOB_ITEM_ATTEMPTS`, `JOB_BATCH_RETRIES`, `JOB_MAX_BATCHES`). Any job now ends as soon as its computer stops instead of at its deadline. See "Jobs that run until done" in docs/SETUP.md. Deploy runs one migration (schedules 8: two new tables, two new columns).
- **Dispatch opens draft pull requests on GitHub (CB-32).** An admin selects **Connect GitHub** in **Settings → General → Pull requests from dispatch**, then **Create** and **Install** on GitHub: the app makes a private GitHub App for the repository (`DISPATCH_GITHUB_REPO`, `cbnsndwch/socio` in the stack) with only contents and pull requests write, and keeps its key sealed; nothing is pasted. From then on, when a coding agent's run makes commits, the app pushes its `dispatch/…` branch and opens a draft pull request into the base branch (`main`; a setting too), titled with the issue's key and title, linking back to the issue, with the agent's summary and the checks. The agent comment, the issue's run list and the run's panel link it. Each push uses a token minted for that one repository and revoked right after; the coding agent and the dispatch worker never get any GitHub credential, and only the app talks to GitHub. It never force-pushes or pushes to `main`, and pushes only the run's own commits, by the dispatch identity. Without a connection runs stay local as before; if GitHub fails, the run keeps its work and the issue shows why. Runs that change `.github/` or CODEOWNERS are never pushed. The pull request's text and commit messages carry no emails, closing keywords or mentions; where GitHub has no draft pull requests (private repositories on free plans) it opens a ready one titled "[draft]".
- **CI treats every branch as untrusted (CB-32).** CI's token only reads and keeps no credentials, and its one action is pinned by commit. The runner runs nothing from the branch: dependencies install without scripts in a container whose only way out is a proxy to registry.npmjs.org, and install scripts, format, lint, typecheck, tests and the build run in containers with no network, on a copy of the checkout without `.git`. A dispatch pull request that changes a `package.json`, a lockfile or `.npmrc` fails the `gate` check until a person adds the `run-ci` label. Copilot code review is optional, after checking how it runs tools on private repositories; see `docs/SETUP.md`.
- **Chat skills: `/compact`, `/grill-me` and your own (CB-143).** Start a message with a skill's name to have the Dot do that; typing `/` lists them, and your message shows the skill as a chip. `/compact` folds the chat's earlier turns into its notes now and says how many; it keeps only what the notes' model summarized and says when it couldn't. `/grill-me` (adapted from Matt Pocock's MIT-licensed grill-me skill) interviews you about a plan or idea, round by round, and stays on until you're done: `/done`, the End button above the composer, or the Dot ends it. `/help` lists the skills; text after `/help` or `/compact` still goes to the Dot. Admins add workspace skills in Settings → Chat skills, for one message or until done; every edit is kept as a revision with a lock record (source, commit, SHA-256, who and when) and can be rolled back. A skill's instructions go with your message, never in the system prompt; a `/word` that names no skill is sent as text. On Telegram the same skills are commands (`/grill_me`). Deploy runs two migrations (chat-skills 1 and 2: three new tables and an index).

### Fixed

- **Promoted mini apps start again (CB-148).** No promoted app could start: the runtime gave an app its data token file and then changed the file's mode, which it isn't allowed to do once the file belongs to the app's user. It now writes the file with its final mode under a temporary name, gives it to the app's user and renames it into place. Apps now start with `bun <entry>` (`APPS_RUNTIME_COMMAND=bun`): `bun run` can't read the directories above the app's and failed, so an old `bun run` setting is read as `bun`. Deploying an older version for a rollback no longer deletes it at once, so a rollback no longer fails with "Version 1 is not deployed here." The every-minute check no longer starts a broken app (and installs its dependencies) every minute: it waits 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 minutes between tries and, after 6 failures, waits until the app changes or its owner presses Start. Apps now reach `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `::1` without the egress proxy, and an update whose earlier version didn't start again either now says so and that the app isn't running, instead of "is still live". The runtime's ingress now connects only to an app's own socket: an app could replace its socket with a link to another app's, and requests meant for it then reached the other app past that app's access check. Deploy needs a new apps runtime image (`company-brain-apps`) as well as the app's.
- **A refused batch that then exits with an error no longer costs an item a try (CB-122).** When a long run's batch was refused (a 403 or 429, or a pause) and then exited with an error while still holding items, the first item it held was charged a try though the batch never reached it, so after a few such refusals a good item was set aside as failed. A refusal now charges nothing, as the setup guide says; a batch that crashes with no refusal still charges the item it was on.

## v0.3.0 (2026-10-21)

Long chats open faster, keep the facts that matter in view and reuse the model's cache. A busy model no longer loses your turn, and you can react to a socio's replies.

- Long chats open fast (CB-136)
- Long chats keep the facts that matter in view (CB-134)
- Quick reactions on a socio's replies (CB-133)
- A busy model no longer loses a turn's work (CB-140)
- A monthly restore drill for the continuous backup (CB-111)

### New

- **The date and time along the chat, not in the system prompt (CB-141).** A Dot's system prompt no longer carries the date, so it stays the same, byte for byte, from day to day and a model's prompt cache keeps it. Instead a short note with the date and time goes with your message on the chat's first turn, on the first turn of a new day (UTC) and when more than 30 minutes have passed since the last one; it is stored and sent with that message on every later turn, so the prompt up to your newest message stays the same. Web chats, scheduled turns, Telegram and dispatch runs all work this way. When a long chat folds its oldest turns into its notes, only the latest of these notes is kept, the notes keep its date and time once its turn is folded, and your newest message gets a new one if none is left. Your newest message still has the time to the minute. Deploy runs one migration (chat 4: a new table, two new columns).
- **Long chats keep the model's prompt cache (CB-139).** A long chat's notes at the start of the prompt now stay the same, byte for byte, from turn to turn until its next fold, so a model with a prompt cache (ChatGPT, llama.cpp) reads the system prompt, the notes and the older turns from it instead of again: the facts chosen for what you just asked now go in a short labeled note with your message, the notes' size no longer moves with the model's token counts, a chat folds again only once what is left no longer fits (down to about 60%, as before), and a turn's later rounds keep the tool results its first round had in full. The system prompt now carries only the date (UTC): the time to the minute, your saved preferences, the page the chat is about, news of your capability requests and the reactions since the last turn go in the same note, so the system prompt is the same for every turn of a day. Runs shows the share of a turn's input the model read from its cache ("12.4k in · 80% cached"), for the turn and for each round, when the model reports it.
- **Long chats' notes come from the shared model (CB-137).** When a long chat's oldest turns are folded into its notes, the workspace's shared model now writes them, with its one-shot options (as titles use, such as no reasoning), instead of the chat's own model: in testing a fold waited 12–28 seconds instead of about 7 minutes with a reasoning model, and the chat's model spends nothing on it. An admin can switch back in **Settings → General → Long chats → Notes are written by** (`CONTEXT_SUMMARY_MODEL=shared|chat` is the starting value); without a shared backend the chat's model writes them, as before. Each update of the notes is now in the turn's trace in Runs ("Notes by the shared model (qwen38-flash) · 6 turns · 12.1k in · 640 out"), with its time and tokens, and says when the plain summary was kept instead and why; its tokens count in the run's. The notes now pin every fact a later question could need, ids and numbers exactly, and say what a changed decision replaces.
- **Long chats keep the facts that matter in view (CB-134).** Every fact a chat's notes pin is now kept, past the 80 the notes hold, and each reply now gets the ones that match what you just asked first, then the newest, instead of only the newest: in testing, questions about early decisions and ids 60 turns later were answered 18–19 times in 20 instead of 11–14, for a few milliseconds and about 400 tokens a reply. An admin chooses in **Settings → General → Long chats → Each reply sees** (`CONTEXT_SELECTION=relevance|age|pointer`): the facts that match each request (the default), the newest facts (as before), or a short core with the rest found by a new `search_notes` tool (smaller prompts, but many more rounds). Runs shows how many facts went with each round ("64 of 212 facts by relevance").
- **The turn time limit is a setting (CB-123).** An admin can set how long one Dot reply may run in **Settings → General → Dot replies** (30 to 300 seconds; `TURN_TIME_LIMIT_SECONDS` sets the starting value). It applies to new replies at once, with no restart: a reply already running keeps the limit it started with. Deploys wait for running replies to finish, up to this limit plus 20 seconds, so the most is 300 seconds, which keeps the wait under the stack's 330-second stop grace period. The wait follows the saved value, not just the environment's.
- **A monthly restore drill for the continuous copy (CB-111).** The app now proves its Litestream copy restores, so a new Litestream release or a damaged replica can't break restores unnoticed. Every 30 days (`RESTORE_DRILL_INTERVAL_DAYS`, or an admin's interval in **Settings → General → Backup schedule**; **Run drill now** on Service setup runs one at once) it restores the latest point and a random point of the last 14 days from the local replica and, with the off-site copy, the latest point of its newest lineage, into scratch files on the backup volume; checks each with `PRAGMA integrity_check`; compares the latest restore's rows with live, allowing for writes since the copy (only a missing table or a gross mismatch fails); and deletes the scratch files, failed or not. Service setup shows `Restore drill: …` under the continuous copy, with when, how long each restore took and, in red, why it failed; admins are notified of a failure. The app image now carries Litestream 0.5.17 for `litestream restore` only; the litestream service stays the replica's one writer.
- **Quick reactions on a Dot's messages (CB-133).** Every reply from a Dot has four small buttons in its action row, next to Copy: thumbs up, thumbs down, try again and needs work. Press one to give it, press it again to take it back; yours are highlighted, a thumbs up and a thumbs down replace each other, and the row stays in view on a message that has reactions. In a team chat (a Telegram topic) each shows how many people gave it. They are saved per message for whoever can read the conversation, and show on your other tabs and devices within seconds. The Dot hears of them on its next turn only, once, as one short note just before your newest message (what you reacted with, to which message, and who when it was a teammate), not as a reply to answer. Each such turn records how many in its trace under Runs ("Told of reactions") and in its OpenTelemetry export (counts only), and the Learning analysis reads the reactions under each reply as evidence. "Try again" is feedback for now: it does not re-run the reply.
- **Comments on goals, and no duplicate goals (CB-135).** A goal now has a comment thread, shown on the Schedules page, the goal's schedule page and the Dot's home, where you can add context or a changed requirement and the Dot reads it in each run. When you ask a Dot for a goal you already have (active, paused or waiting for your approval), `goal_set` sets nothing and shows the match; the Dot adds to the existing goal with the new `goal_comment`, or sets another with `force` only after you confirm.
- **Long chats open fast (CB-136).** A chat now shows its history from a store in your browser at once and asks the server only for what came after, instead of replaying the whole conversation on every open: a 32-turn, 1.5 MB chat shows about 400 ms sooner, its longest freeze drops from about 300 ms to the app's usual 100 ms, and it downloads a few KB instead of 1.5 MB. A chat opened for the first time gets its newest 20 turns; older ones load as you scroll up, and you stay where you were. Long tool results in older turns come as a preview and load in full when you open the step; approval and page review cards stay whole. The store is kept per person in IndexedDB, bounded (the 30 most recent chats, about 15 MB), and emptied when you sign out or someone else signs in on the browser. The Dot still gets the whole conversation, and a message is still never sent twice when the server restarts. Built on TanStack DB (`@tanstack/db` 0.15.1).

### Fixed

- **Busy-model retries keep to the turn's time (CB-140).** A wait for a busy model now never uses the last fifth of the turn time limit (it was the last tenth), the model client no longer waits on its own for up to a minute outside the turn's wait budget, the shared model after a switch also keeps to its own image limit, and Runs says when Stop or the time limit cut a wait short and nothing was sent again.
- **A busy model no longer loses a turn's work (CB-140).** When ChatGPT (or the shared model) rate-limits a request mid-turn, or can't be reached, the request is sent again within the turn: after the wait the backend asks for (`Retry-After`), else a backoff of up to 1, 2 and 4 seconds, up to 3 times a round and 60 seconds a turn, never into the last fifth of the turn time limit. Retries don't count as rounds, and Runs shows each one and its wait in the turn's trace. If someone's own ChatGPT plan is still busy, the rest of the turn runs on the workspace's shared model, fitted to its context, and the reply says so once; an admin can turn this off in **Settings → General → Busy models**, and it never happens when the person chose their own model for that conversation. If no model can carry on, the turn ends with a status of what it did (the tools it ran) and what is left, instead of "ChatGPT is busy right now" after work was already done; the next message carries on. Runs shows such a turn as **Model unavailable**. A request that already streamed part of its answer is never sent again.
- **The backup taken before migrations is private (CB-138).** The copy of the database the app takes at boot before running migrations was readable by every user on the host (0644), unlike scheduled backups (0600). Every database backup is now written as a private temporary file and renamed into place, so it is never readable by others, not even briefly; copies of users' files and the object snapshot are private too. At boot the app also tightens any older `company-brain-*.sqlite` backup in `BACKUP_DIR` that is looser than 0600 (never following a symlink) and logs how many.

## v0.2.0 (2026-10-21)

Two weeks of work since the first release: socios of your own, goals, dictation, page search, long chats that never run out of room, and agents such as Claude Code that act for you. The whole app moved to one set of components, and it fits a phone.

- Connect your own agents, such as Claude Code (CB-90)
- Long chats never outgrow their model (CB-129)
- Dictate a message (CB-101)
- A home page for each socio (CB-89)
- Settings are pages now (CB-95)

### New

- **Long chats never outgrow their model (CB-129).** A conversation no longer stops with "too long", "start a new conversation" or no answer once its history fills the model's context. Before every model round (not just every turn) the prompt is fitted to about 70% of the model's window, cheapest first: a tool result over about 10k tokens goes in as its start and end with a note naming its ref; older results (screenshots and snapshots first) are set aside behind the same kind of note; and when that is not enough, the oldest turns are folded into the chat's running notes, a summary plus pinned facts (what you are working on, your decisions, and the ids, paths, datasets, apps and links named, word for word), kept per chat and updated as the chat grows, never rewritten from the start. A pinned fact stays word for word even when a later summary leaves it out, and a turn writes at most 3 summaries (any further turns are noted from their own words). The system prompt, recent turns and the last rounds always stay whole; only a single pasted message too big for the model on its own is cut to its start and end, with a ref to read it whole. Nothing is deleted: socios get `recall` to search older turns and read any set-aside result in full, page by page. If the model still refuses a prompt as too long, it is compacted harder and sent again, twice at most, before the turn could fail. While notes are written, the status line says "Tidying notes". Each model has a context window: 258,400 tokens for ChatGPT plans (the Codex catalog's 272k at its 95%), and for a self-hosted backend whatever the operator sets in **Settings → Platform** (new "Context window" field; `MODEL_CONTEXT_WINDOW` seeds the first backend), 32,768 when unset. A ChatGPT 400 that says the prompt is too long now counts as too long rather than as a refused attachment. Each compaction adds a `company-brain.context-compaction` event to the run (sizes and counts only), and shows in the turn's trace in Runs as "Context fitted" before its round (a retry and the notes' requests are marked on their round, not counted as rounds) and in its OpenTelemetry export. Existing chats are compacted on their next turn.
- **Every turn keeps a trace, and can send it to OpenTelemetry (CB-128).** When a socio fails, Runs now says why: open **Trace** under a turn for a timeline of its model rounds (time, tokens, why each stopped) and tool calls (duration, ok, error, refused or timed out), the waits for an approval or for you, and why the turn ended (completed, round cap, time or output limit, error, stopped, interrupted). Admins see every turn; members now get Runs too, with their own turns only. The `Turn: finished` log line adds `reason=`, `rounds=` and the tokens in and out. With `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` set, each turn also goes to an OpenTelemetry collector (Langfuse, Phoenix, Grafana Tempo, an OTel Collector) as GenAI spans, in the background, without content unless an admin turns on **Settings → General → Tracing → Include content in traces**. See "Tracing" in docs/SETUP.md.
- **Limits you can change without a redeploy (CB-108).** An admin can now set the schedule and job limits, the data space limits (largest file, dataset, data per person, datasets per person) and the monthly dispatch spend in **Settings → General**, and they apply at once, with no restart. What is saved takes the place of `SCHEDULE_*`, `SCHEDULES_PER_USER`, `JOB_*`, `DATA_*_MAX_*`, `DATA_MAX_DATASETS` and `DISPATCH_MONTHLY_USD`, which stay the starting values; "Use the server's defaults" goes back to them, and members see the values but can't change them. The setup guide now lists every setting the server reads under [Settings and the environment](SETUP.md#settings-and-the-environment): which are in Settings, which could be and what would make them so, which are secrets and which need a restart.
- **The continuous copy goes off-site too (CB-112).** Losing the host no longer loses up to a day of work: with the off-site copy and `LITESTREAM_REPLICA_DIR` set, the app seals each new file of Litestream's replica under the data key, as it seals the daily backup's database, and sends it to the off-site target under `litestream/<lineage>/` every minute (`BACKUP_LTX_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `0` off). Only whole files go, each once (a restart sends nothing twice), the highest level first, so a send cut short leaves no gap in the transactions, and what Litestream compacts or drops is removed off-site too, but only a file the app sent itself, once a newer file it sent holds the same transactions, and never while a file is still waiting or the replica can't all be read, so the target always holds a set Litestream can restore from. A new host, a restore, `/backups` put back from an older copy, or a second host sending with a copy of it sends a new lineage and never removes or overwrites an older one, so a host started before the restore can't wipe the copy you need. Service setup shows `Continuous copy off-site: …` under the continuous copy: when everything was last off-site, how long the newest copy has waited, and failures, in red, retried later each time. `backup-cli.js fetch-ltx <dir> [lineage]` downloads and opens it as a file replica for `litestream restore`. Admins set that interval and how often backups run in **Settings → General → Backup schedule**, over `BACKUP_LTX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` and `BACKUP_INTERVAL_HOURS`, without a restart.
- **Blocked-by links; a batch runs blockers first (CB-29).** An admin can mark an issue "blocked by CB-n" in its side panel (the other issue shows "Blocks CB-m"); self-links and loops are refused, and board cards and rows show "Blocked by 2" while a blocker is still open. A blocker counts as done once it is Done or Won't fix. "Dispatch selected" now queues the selection together: an issue starts only after its blockers in the selection have succeeded, and if one fails or is canceled its dependants are not started and say why on their issues. Dispatching a single issue with open blockers asks first ("Dispatch anyway"). Connected agents and socios see `blockedBy` and `blocks` when they read an item.
- **Automatic triage of new issues (CB-30).** An admin can turn on **Settings → General → Automatic triage** (off by default; `AUTO_TRIAGE` and `AUTO_TRIAGE_DAILY` set the starting values). A new issue in triage is then sent once to the workspace's model, as data, and gets a comment from "Automatic triage" proposing a kind, area, priority and labels, with a one-line reason. Nothing changes by itself: an admin applies the proposal from the issue's side panel in one click, or ignores it. An answer that is not exactly a proposal from the existing lists is dropped and recorded, and a failed issue is not asked again.
- **A new chat is ready to type in (CB-116).** Opening a new chat with a socio, from New chat, the sidebar, the socio's page or ⌘K, puts the cursor in the message box. Phones and tablets are left alone, so the keyboard doesn't cover the chat; so are chats with history, the page's chat panel, and anything opened over a dialog or while you are typing in another field.
- **Dictation on your own transcription server (CB-101).** "On our server" dictation no longer has to go to OpenAI: it sends the recording to any OpenAI-compatible speech-to-text server (`POST {url}/v1/audio/transcriptions`), such as one on your own network, so the audio stays in your network. Set `VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_URL` and `VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_MODEL` (OpenAI's `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` by default), and `VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_API_KEY` only if the server wants a key (it goes only to `VOICE_TRANSCRIBE_URL`'s origin, never to another URL an admin sets); `VOICE_API_KEY` is sent to OpenAI's URL only. An admin can change the URL and model in Settings → General → Transcription, which shows which key is sent and never the key. Server dictation is now offered without `VOICE_API_KEY` when the server isn't OpenAI's. No language is sent, so the server detects it. A server that takes longer than 60 seconds gets "Voice provider took longer than 60 seconds…", and the server log names the host. Voice calls are unchanged.
- **Dictate a message (CB-101).** The chat composer has a mic next to the attach button. Tap it, speak, and tap Done: the text goes into your message at the cursor, for you to edit and send; it is never sent on its own. Cancel drops it. You choose how speech becomes text, once, in Settings → Account → Profile → Dictation: **on this device** (your browser does it and nothing goes to our server; on iPhone, Safari may send the audio to Apple) or **on our server** (the workspace's voice provider transcribes a recording of up to 2 minutes). Only the ways that work in your browser and on your server are offered; with one, the mic uses it without asking, and with none there is no mic. An admin can allow only one in Settings → General. The "Text is ready. Voice needs separate server configuration." note is gone from the composer; "Text and voice, one conversation." now shows below it, on wider screens only, when voice is set up.
- **Socios attach the screenshots you sent to what they file.** When you share screenshots or files in a chat and ask a socio to file an issue or add a comment on the Improvements board, it now attaches them: `file_improvement` and `comment_on_improvement` take the ids of files from that conversation and copy each onto the item or the comment, as your upload, with the board's checks on type, size and the 10-per-item cap. A file from another conversation, someone else's, a deleted one or a type the board refuses is left out on its own, and the socio is told which were attached and why the others were not, so it no longer says "attached screenshots" when there are none. Images are now labeled for the socio with their id, as other files already were. `read_improvement` (and a connected agent's `read_issue`) lists an item's attachments and each comment's by name, type and size.
- **A home page for each socio (CB-89).** Clicking a socio in the sidebar now always opens its page, instead of sometimes its latest chat and sometimes a new one. The page is a dashboard of what the socio does for you: Now (your active chats with it, its turns in progress, approvals waiting for you), Earlier (archived chats, recent turns, what it filed on the board for you) and Next (your goals for it, with status and progress, and its schedules with their next run). Below, all your chats with it, in Active and Archived tabs (`?tab=archived`). New chat is on the page, in the socio's ⋯ menu, on Home's socio cards and in ⌘K, at `/dots/<id>/new`; old `/dots/<id>` links open the page. Over a chat, the socio's name in the breadcrumbs is a menu of its latest chats, with All chats and New chat, so switching to another chat with the same socio is one pick. Typing a socio's name in ⌘K lists its page and its chats. An archived socio's page is read-only, with Unarchive for its owner or an admin. Everything shown is yours only; `GET /api/dots/:id/runs` and `GET /api/issues/filed?dot=` are new, and another person's own socio is not found.
- **A turn's work, in one line (CB-72).** A socio's reasoning and tool calls no longer show as scattered blocks, or not at all: each turn has one line, "Worked for 34s · 5 steps", above its reply, with "· 1 failed" when a step failed. It is open while the turn runs, so each step shows as it happens, and folds when the turn ends. Opened, it lists the steps in the order they happened, one line each with an icon, a plain sentence ("Searched the board for “export”", "Ran a script"), how long it took and whether it failed. A step opens to what it took and gave: commands, SQL, scripts and their output as code blocks with copy buttons, search matches as a list, an error first and in red, long output cut until "Show all"; never raw JSON. A turn that only reasoned still reads "Thought for 12s". The runner now stamps every event it stores with its time, so the times survive a reload ("Thought for 12s", not "Thought"), and each message's time shows in its action row (the full date on hover). Runs stored before keep their start and end times (runs migration 3): their user messages and replies have a time, their steps none. Approval and page review cards show as before; the computer's live screen shows in the trace of the turn that last used the browser.
- **Connected agents see and add attachments.** `read_issue` now lists the files on an item and on its comments, and sends its newest images with the answer as image content (at most 5, and 5 MB in all; the rest are listed with the reason), so an agent triaging an item sees its screenshots. `read_issue_attachment` reads one file by id: an image, a text file's text, or a PDF's details. `file_issue` and `comment_on_issue` take attachments (name, media type, base64 data), checked as web uploads are: the same types, 5 MB each, 10 on an item. If one file is refused, nothing is written. Each file is the person's, with the agent recorded beside it. See docs/CONNECTED-AGENTS.md.
- **Connected agents: your own agents, acting for you (CB-90).** An agent you run elsewhere, such as Claude Code, can now act in the workspace for you: `claude mcp add --transport http socio https://<host>/mcp`, then approve it in the browser. The consent screen shows the agent's name, logo and domain (or "Unverified local app" for one on your computer, which anyone there could claim to be), what it may do in plain words, and your name, and lets you name the connection ("Claude Code on my laptop"). The workspace is an OAuth 2.1 authorization server for `/mcp`: authorization code with PKCE (S256 only), refresh tokens that rotate, tokens bound to `/mcp` (RFC 8707) and stored hashed, clients identified by their Client ID Metadata Document (no Dynamic Client Registration), and discovery at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` and `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/api/auth`. The metadata fetch goes only to public https addresses, follows no redirects, and reads at most 8 KB within 5 seconds; redirect URIs must match the document. Account → Connected agents lists your agents, with their scopes and last use, and Disconnect ends one at once. Admin → Connected agents lists everyone's; admins can disconnect any, and limit agents to a list of domains (off by default). Deactivating someone disconnects their agents. Better Auth is now 1.7.10. See docs/CONNECTED-AGENTS.md.
- **Socios file to the Improvements board (CB-31).** An admin can turn on "File to the Improvements board" in a socio's settings; it is off for every socio until then. That socio gets `search_improvements` and `file_improvement`: when someone asks it to file or track something (in a chat, on Telegram, or in a scheduled task they set up), it checks the board for close open items first and shows them instead of filing, then files a bug, feature or improvement in triage, keeping what was observed apart from what it suspects, and answers with the item's link. Each item records both the socio and the person it acted for, plus what started the turn; the database refuses a socio filing without either. The item shows "Filed by ‹socio› for ‹person› (asked in chat)". The socio can't set priority, owner or status. Filings share the capability-request limits (`DOT_REQUESTS_PER_DOT_DAILY`, `DOT_REQUESTS_PER_USER_DAILY`), counted separately. The `capabilities` tool lists the board, with how many items were filed today. A filing from a goal's or a schedule's turn records that goal or task too, and one from a person's own socio records its owner, who must be the person it acted for; the database refuses either rule broken.
- **Issue cards in chat (CB-87).** A link in a socio's reply to one of this app's issues ("Open issue CB-85", relative or on this app's address) shows as a small card: the key, title, status, priority, kind and area. A click opens the issue as the link did. The card is read with your own session, one request for all the issues in view, and kept for a minute; when you can't read the issue, it was deleted, or the request fails, it stays the plain link. A paragraph or list of nothing but issue links is a stack of cards. Copy still gives the message's Markdown. Bare keys ("CB-85") stay text.
- **Votes, and more board tools for socios (CB-83, CB-84).** Everyone can upvote an item on the Improvements board, once each, from its card, its row or its detail, and withdraw the vote; the count shows on all three, and "Most votes" sorts the board by it. A socio allowed to file now also gets `read_improvement`, `vote_improvement` and `comment_on_improvement`. `search_improvements` covers open, closed or all items, matches the title and the description, whole words, word starts and text inside words ("expo" and "port" find "export"), and answers best first with a short excerpt, status and vote count. Before filing, the close items shown also include open ones whose description has the new title's words, or whose title is in the new problem. Read gives an item's fields, votes, description and newest comments, without emails or people's names. When the person asks, the socio votes as them (it is their one vote, with the socio recorded) or adds their evidence (impact, how to reproduce it, who is affected) as a comment shown as "‹socio› for ‹person›", which that person can delete. Each vote and comment records the socio, the person and what started the turn, shows in the socio's admin audit, and has its own count against the filing limits. The board's search box now ignores accents too.
- **MCP tools for connected agents (CB-90).** `/mcp` is the brain's MCP endpoint (Streamable HTTP) for agents such as Claude Code, Cursor and VS Code that a person connects to act for them. With `issues:read` an agent searches, lists and reads board items. With `issues:write` it files items (similar open items are shown first, as for socios), comments, votes, withdraws votes and changes fields. With `pages:read` it lists, searches and reads pages. It always acts as its person, within both the grant's scopes and the person's role, so a member's agent can't change an item's status. What it writes is the person's: they are the reporter, the comment's author, the voter and the actor in the history. The agent is recorded beside each action, and the issue shows "‹label› for ‹person›", such as "Claude Code on my laptop for Ada". Without a valid token `/mcp` answers 401 and names its protected resource metadata. A tool outside the grant gets 403 `insufficient_scope`. Each grant is rate limited. See docs/CONNECTED-AGENTS.md.
- **Socios of your own.** Anyone can make a socio with "Create socio": it is private to them (only they see it and chat with it), it works only for them, and they edit it from the sidebar. Admins still make company socios, shared by the team, and can edit anyone's socio. Settings that reach beyond one person stay with admins: filing to the Improvements board, the web identity and Learning. Ten each. Another person's own socio is "not found" everywhere: chats, page chats, its computer, approval policies and data grants. The Telegram channel and agent runs use company socios only.
- **Archive a socio, never delete it.** A socio's owner (or an admin, for any socio) archives it from its settings. An archived socio leaves the sidebar, Home, the palette and every picker, starts no chats and answers no turns; its chats stay readable ("This socio is archived"), and its filings, goals and audit stay. Its schedules and goals pause ("Paused: its socio was archived."). Unarchive brings it back (from the chat's notice, or the socio's settings); its schedules stay paused until their owners resume them. An admin's archive is an admin's to undo, the last company socio can't be archived, an archived socio's computer, jobs, apps and voice calls don't run, and its Telegram conversations go on with the channel's socio. Archived socios don't count toward a person's ten.
- **Socios, for admins.** A Socios page (Admin) lists every socio, the company's and each person's own, archived ones too, with its owner, chats, people and last activity. Each socio's page shows its settings, its chats (owners and dates only: titles summarize the chat), and its audit, newest first: setting changes (who, and which settings, never the instructions' text), turns, board filings, approvals, and computer, web and data actions, job runs and goals, each with the person it acted for. Archive and unarchive are there too.
- **A socio's menu.** A socio's ⋯ in the sidebar (or a right-click) opens a menu: New chat, Rename (in place), its everyday permissions (public-page research, saved memories and, for admins, filing to the Improvements board) as checkboxes that save at once, Activity (admins: the socio's audit), and Archive…, which asks first and offers Undo. All settings… opens the full settings, now as wide as the app's larger dialogs.
- **Goals.** Ask a socio to keep working toward something ("keep the onboarding doc current", "find three venues by Friday") and it proposes a goal with `goal_set`; you approve it as you approve schedules (`goal.set`, or bypass). It then works on it on its cadence, in a conversation of its own, as you, records a progress note each time, and marks it done when it is met (`goal_list`, `goal_update`). Schedules & jobs lists your goals with their status and latest progress; pause or cancel one by its schedule. A goal counts toward your schedule limit.
- **Page search.** ⌘K finds pages by their text, not only their title: text hits come a moment after the instant title matches, with a snippet and the matched words marked. Text inside words matches too ("voic" finds "invoice"). A misspelt word is corrected ("Showing results for invoice", with "Search instead for …"). Each result says how fresh the page is: when it was edited, or that it was used recently, never by whom. The Space library's search box uses the same search. Socios get `search_space_pages`; `pages:read` keys get `GET /api/spaces/search`. Operators see search timings by day on the Platform page.
- **Chat menu.** Every chat you own has a ⋯ menu in the sidebar (also on right-click) and in the chat's header, which now shows the chat's title. Rename edits the title in place; a renamed chat keeps your title. Delete asks first and names the scheduled tasks that go with it; it waits while the socio is working. Deleting the open chat starts a new chat with the same socio (CB-66).
- **Chat transcript (CB-66).** Copy under every message: the Markdown goes on the clipboard, with a check mark. A socio's replies are full-width text; your messages stay bubbles on the right; the chat is one centered column. In team chats, other people's messages show on the left with their name.
- **One status line.** A running turn shows one readable line at the end of the chat, such as "Waiting for ChatGPT · 4 s" or "Ada is thinking · 6 s". The usual wait and the prompt size are in its tooltip. The header no longer says "Thinking…". Reasoning stays folded while it streams and then reads "Thought for 12s". Text that was 9 px or smaller is now 12 px.
- **Approval bar.** While a socio waits for your approval, the composer shows what it asks, with Approve, Deny and Show request. You can keep typing.
- **Scrolling.** The chat follows new text only when you are at the end. Scroll up to read in peace; a ↓ button takes you back.
- **Tabs keep up with each other (CB-74).** A change you make in one tab shows in your other tabs at once, not at their next poll: a renamed or deleted chat, a sent message, a decision, a page, an issue, a memory, a task or a schedule. A tab left on a chat you deleted elsewhere shows that it is gone, without errors. A tab you come back to after two seconds or more refreshes what it shows.
- **Phone header.** On phones the chat title has room again: Pause is an icon (its name is in the tooltip), and Report issue moved to the account menu. On wider screens the header is unchanged. Report issue is in the account menu at every width.
- **Older team messages.** In team chats, messages from before this release have no stored sender. They now show on the left as "Team member" instead of on the right as if they were yours.
- **Preview attachments before sending (CB-85).** Click an attached image in the composer to see it at full size; the previous and next buttons, or the arrow keys, step through the other attachments, and Escape closes. A text file shows its text, read-only (the first 256 KB of a long one), and any other file shows its type and size, with Download. Images in sent messages open in the same viewer.
- **Issue areas and a wider issue (CB-76, CB-82).** Issues have two new areas, Web chat and Homepage. Report issue on a chat page picks Web chat for you, and a socio's filing that mentions the web chat or the home page gets that area. Existing issues keep their areas. An open issue uses more of a large screen: up to 1440 px wide on full HD and larger, with a wider fields column. On tablets and phones the fields sit below the description, in one scroll, with nothing wider than the screen.
- **A page for each schedule (CB-88).** A schedule's title under Schedules & jobs, and "Details and past runs" on a task under Scheduled & activity, open a page that says what it is for, what it does and what it has done. Its purpose (the goal, or the prompt) is Markdown at the body's sizes, folded when long. What it does: the socio, the cadence in words ("Every weekday at 09:00", crons too), the next three runs in your time, its time zone, its chat, the approval policies its runs act under, and its status (active, paused and why, completed or canceled), with Run now, Pause, Resume and Cancel. What it has done: its runs, newest first, ten at a time with Load more, each with its status, start, how long it took, the start of its answer or its error, and links to its turn in the chat and to what it filed, commented on or voted for on the board. A goal's schedule also shows the goal and its latest progress note. Only your own. The task card under Scheduled & activity shows the prompt the same way, under a normal-sized title.
