Issues have a Schedules area; Report issue on the schedules pages and on Scheduled & activity picks it for you.
- **Archive chats.** The chat menu has Archive, between Rename and Delete. An archived chat leaves your sidebar, the ⌘K palette and Home, with "Chat archived" and Undo; nothing is deleted. Its link still opens it, with "This chat is archived" and Unarchive. Account → Archived chats lists them, with Unarchive and Delete. Scheduled tasks in an archived chat keep running.

### Changed

- **A roomier composer on phones (CB-97).** On a phone, and in a narrow chat panel, the message box now has a full-width row of its own, with the link, attach and mic buttons and Send in a toolbar under it, so the placeholder and what you type use the whole width. Wider screens keep one row. The new-chat composer starts at one line, with attach at the start of its toolbar and the model and Send at the end.
- **Learning on the new components (CB-95).** The Learning page (Skills for members) is built on the app's shared components and looks as it did, in light and dark. A few things that were off are fixed: on a phone the containers sit above the selected one instead of squeezing it off the screen; "Analyze now" no longer wraps onto two lines; a proposal's name is as easy to read and click as a published skill's, and says whether its review is open; the container counts are plain text instead of a shaded bar; the delivered revision is a compact picker next to "Deliver"; and the primary buttons use the app's mint, as the rest of the app does. Editing a proposal's SKILL.md on a phone no longer zooms the page in (CB-96).
- **Runs, Activity and Memories on the new components (CB-95).** The Runs page's list of turns is now the app's shared table and looks as it did. On a phone it scrolls sideways in its own frame instead of pushing the whole page wider than the screen. The status filter is the same menu as on the Dispatch tab, and the socio's name next to a conversation is 12 px, no longer 10 px. Scheduled & activity looks as it did too. On a phone its search box uses 16 px text, so an iPhone no longer zooms in when you tap it, and the task card's buttons and footer are 12 px, no longer 10 px. Memories looks as it did, except for Add memory: it is now the app's mint button, like New chat on Home, instead of the old blue.
- **Settings are pages now (CB-95, CB-18).** The Settings dialog, the Account dialog and the Telegram dialog are pages under `/settings`, with a section nav: Workspace (General, People, Service accounts, Platform) and Account (Profile, Inference, Archived chats, Connected agents, Telegram). Their address shows in the browser, Back works, and a reload stays on the page. Everything that opened them opens the right page: Settings & setup, the account menu's Account item, the Telegram item, "Open Account → Inference" in a chat, and the Settings button in a Space; `/settings` opens General. A socio's settings stay a dialog, as wide as before, on the new components; its Archive button now sits on its row, and its approval policy and data grant links are underlined. On a phone the fields are 16 px, so iOS no longer zooms in, and Platform no longer scrolls sideways.
- **Chat on the new components (CB-95).** The chat view, its composer, the new-chat page, the voice call and the page's chat panel now use the shared components and color tokens, and look as before in light and dark. A few things that were off are fixed: the model picker and buttons no longer cover the socio's name when the chat is narrow (beside the computer pane, in a page's chat panel); the page's chat panel shows its whole composer; the minimized call is a small card again and stays on screen on phones, as does the open call; the round send button on a new chat's phone layout is round; the send button no longer turns blue on hover; and a focused composer shows a quiet teal border instead of a blue ring over its note. On phones the message box, the source link and the model pickers use 16 px text, so iPhones no longer zoom in when you tap them (CB-96).
- **Spaces on the new components (CB-95).** A Space's library (its pages as cards or rows, search, the sort menu and the page outline) and its "Page not found" and "This page was deleted" notices use the shared components and color tokens, and look as before in light and dark. In the list view on a phone, the Delete button no longer sits on top of a page's first line, and a focused page card shows the app's teal ring instead of a blue one.
- **Pages on the new components (CB-95).** A page's header, its formatting toolbar, the ⋯ menu, the / block menu, Move page, the save notices, the page chat box, dataset views and the page review card in a chat use the shared components, and a page reads and writes exactly as before. In dark mode a page's text, quotes, code, tables and links take their colors from the app's theme. A few things that were off are fixed: a dataset view's fields use 16 px text on phones, so iPhones no longer zoom in when you tap them; the review card's Approve and Open page buttons are the app's mint, and its focus ring is no longer blue; the / menu highlights the block under the pointer.
- **The old stylesheet is retired (CB-95).** The styles the views used before they moved to the shared components are gone, and nothing looks different. What is left is labeled with the screens that still use it: sign-in, the chat's messages, the computer pane and the research brief. The app's stylesheet is 60 kB smaller (12 kB compressed). One fix on the way: typing / in a page no longer puts the slash in a tall box above the block menu; it stays a plain character in the line.
- **Socios can file more each day.** A socio may now make 50 capability requests and board filings a day, and 50 may be made for one person, up from 5 and 10; comments and votes each count separately against the same limits. An admin can change both in **Settings → General → Socio filing limits**; `DOT_REQUESTS_PER_DOT_DAILY` and `DOT_REQUESTS_PER_USER_DAILY` set the starting values.

### Fixed

- **Computers have Git (CB-130).** A socio's computer had no `git`, so `git clone` of a public repo failed with "git: command not found". The computer image now includes the Git CLI and its CA certificates, and its build fails if `git --version` does not run. The image is now `company-brain-computer:b6932d3-browser-limits-git`: rebuild it, set `COMPUTER_IMAGE` to that tag in the computers stack, then Stop and Start each computer to get the new image.
- **A goal waiting for your approval shows its card in the chat (CB-131).** When a socio set a goal that needed your approval, `goal_set` returned the request wrapped in another result, so the chat did not recognize it and showed no Approve and Deny card, in the live turn or after a reload; asking again then created a second request, and both were approved as duplicate goals. The card now shows for these requests, including chats saved before this fix, and a socio that asks again for a goal already waiting for approval gets that same request back, not a new one.
- **Long tasks end with a status, not silence (CB-127).** A socio's turn was cut off after its fifth model round (its tenth with learned skills), right after a tool result, so step-by-step browser or computer work ended with "The current turn returned no response" and no reply at all. A turn may now use 30 rounds (`DOT_MAX_MODEL_ROUNDS`, or **Settings → General → Socio replies** for an admin; five more when the socio has learned skills), and the turn time limit (`TURN_TIME_LIMIT_SECONDS`) still bounds it. When the rounds run out, the socio gets one last round without tools and says what it did, where it stopped and what is left, so you can ask it to carry on. Traces get one `socio.loop-end` event per turn saying how it ended (completed, round cap, time limit, output limit, error, stopped or waiting for you), with the rounds run and the cap.
- **A tool call cut off by the model's length limit no longer fails the turn (CB-126).** When a socio wrote a large file in one call, the model's output limit could cut the call off, and the turn ended with "Socio could not complete this request". Now the cut-off call never runs (not even a part of it), the socio is told to write in parts (`computer_files_write` with `append`, or several smaller calls) and carries on. If it happens again in the same turn, or a reply is cut off before any of it was written, the turn ends with a message that says so. The file-writing tools and the socio's instructions now say to write large files in parts. This works for ChatGPT plans (`response.incomplete`) and for OpenAI-compatible models (`finish_reason: "length"`).
- **A socio's browser no longer gets stuck for good (CB-125).** When a computer's Chromium died without the computer noticing, closing it never finished, and every snapshot and screenshot after that waited 70 s and failed until someone restarted the container; shell and file tools kept working, and the computer still looked healthy. Now the computer gives a close 10 s and then kills the browser, checks that a browser answers before using it, and starts a new one when it doesn't (a page that is only busy running its own script keeps its browser, tabs and sign-ins), and its health says when the browser is stuck. Snapshots and screenshots give up after 20 s. When the browser is stuck, the socio is told to have the computer restarted (Stop computer, then Start computer, in its Computer panel), and its browser calls fail at once for a minute instead of each waiting out the limit. Each computer also gets an init process, so the processes a dead browser leaves behind are cleaned up. This needs the new computer and supervisor images, and each computer stopped and started once to pick them up.
- **Updates no longer break open chats (CB-108).** While the server finishes running turns before a restart, the app keeps working: pages, reads, live updates and writes are served as usual, and only new turns, dispatch runs, voice calls and learning analyzes are refused, with 503, the code `draining` and `Retry-After`. A message sent then shows "Socio is updating. Your message will send as soon as it’s back." instead of an error, and is sent once the next server is up (`/healthz` answers and isn't draining), asked with backoff from 1 to 10 seconds. A turn cut off as the server went away is never sent twice: the chat reconnects first, and if the server had the message it shows what it stored. Stop ends the wait and puts the message back in the box when the server never took it, and stops the reply once the chat is back and streaming it. After 10 minutes without hearing from the server it gives up the same way; a message the server may have stays shown, never offered to send again, until a reconnect shows whether it arrived; if it didn't, it leaves the chat and goes back in the box. Approving a page review as the server starts draining no longer counts the message as unsent (it was the run carrying on after the review that was refused): the message stays, and once the chat is back the review is open again to approve. A chat with a stopped reply in it no longer loses every later turn when it reconnects: the stop was stored with an end the browser rejected, which ended the replay there. A chat opened while the server is away connects on its own when it is back, and the shell shows "Socio is updating. Reconnecting…" rather than "Failed to fetch" or a 502, polling with the same backoff. When the browser says it is offline, the shell and the chat say "You’re offline. Reconnecting when you’re back online." instead, and ask again as soon as it is back online; a message sent then goes once it is. A page whose code fails to load in the gap reloads only once the server answers, so the installed app never lands on the proxy's error page. The service worker still keeps no cache.
- **Enter sends on a Windows desktop (CB-115).** In the new-chat message box, Enter, Ctrl+Enter and Shift+Enter all added a line break: that box had no send key at all. And on a computer whose touch screen made the browser report a coarse pointer, the chat's own box treated it as a phone, where Enter adds a line. Now Enter and Ctrl+Enter (Cmd+Enter on a Mac) send and Shift+Enter adds a line, in both boxes; a Windows, Mac, Linux or ChromeOS computer is never treated as a phone, and an IME choosing a candidate never sends. On phones Enter still adds a line, and Ctrl+Enter sends.
- **Media keeps its layout with long folder names (CB-117).** A long folder name, such as a UUID, no longer runs past the folder list into the page: it ends in an ellipsis, with the full name on hover. The breadcrumbs stay on one line, folding earlier folders into a ⋯ menu when the path is deep, the folder tiles fill the width, and the page no longer scrolls sideways on a phone.
- **An issue link on its own line is a card again, whatever a socio puts after it (CB-118).** Since CB-104 a link is a card only when it stands alone, and a socio that wrote "[Open CB-117](…) ↗" got the inline link with a status badge instead, on every screen: the arrow counted as words. Anything after or between issue links with no letters or digits in it (arrows, brackets, emoji, punctuation) is now dropped, and the link shows as its card. A link followed by words stays inline.
- **Closing an issue you opened from a chat takes you back to the chat (CB-119).** An issue link in a socio's reply opens the issue over the Issues board, and closing it left you on the board. It now goes back to the chat you clicked it in, as closing an issue opened from the board goes back to the board. An issue link opened in a new tab or typed in still closes to the board.
- **The socio chat header follows the workspace layout (CB-120).** On a wide screen the socio's header sat centered in the message column, far from the page edges. It is now one row across the workspace, like Issues: the socio and "Here with you" at the start, the model picker and the file, history and call buttons at the end, with the same edges and top space. Messages and the composer keep their readable column, and the phone header and the page panel's chat are unchanged.
- **Long lines no longer widen the chat on phones (CB-107).** A long URL or line of code in an approval card or a message pushed the whole chat wider than the screen and cut it off at both sides. Long words and URLs now wrap, and code blocks scroll sideways in their own box.
- **An issue link in the middle of a sentence no longer leaves its full stop on a line of its own (CB-104).** A socio's reply showed every link to an issue as a card, which breaks the line, so "added to CB-95." put the "." under the card, and a sentence after a link could start with ". The…". A link is now a card only when it stands alone: its own paragraph or list item, with nothing after it but punctuation, which is dropped. In a sentence it stays a link, with the issue's status beside it and its title on hover, and the text flows on.
- **The chat composer and header fit an iPhone (CB-97, CB-100, CB-102, CB-103).** The message box grows with what you type, up to 40% of the visible height on a phone and half of it on wider screens, then scrolls inside itself with the line you are typing in view; it fades at the top while earlier lines are hidden. Before, it showed two lines and scrolled the rest out of sight. The send and stop buttons stay round, and the new-chat composer no longer squeezes them out of its border. The model picker is quiet small text with a chevron, as wide as its name and cut short when long; it opens the same list. On a phone the conversation header is one row: the socio, its name and status, then the model and the buttons, so messages start about 65 px higher. The page chat panel keeps its actions above the socio. The app is now as tall as what is visible, above the keyboard too, and the page itself no longer scrolls, so you can no longer scroll past the composer into blank space.
- **The iPhone app no longer zooms in and scrolls sideways (CB-96).** iOS zooms in on a text box, menu or editor whose text is under 16 px when you tap it, and the installed app stays zoomed, so the page then scrolls sideways. The chat composer and its model menu, page search and sorting, the page chat, Activity's search, Approvals, the socio settings, rename boxes and the search palette were all smaller than that. On touch screens every field's text is now 16 px (desktops are unchanged), and pinch-zoom still works. The Platform page and a socio's admin page also no longer run wider than a phone screen.
- **The dispatch panel notices a run that ends before its first check (CB-93).** A run that was already over when the issue's panel first read its log stayed "active" for about 90 seconds, and the agent's comment showed only after a reload. The panel now treats it as finished at once: it shows the result, toasts it and reloads the issue.
- **Parallel tool calls without an index run as separate calls (CB-92).** Some OpenAI-compatible servers stream several tool calls in one reply without numbering each one. Their names and arguments ran together into one call with invalid JSON, and the turn ended with an error. Each new call ID now starts a new call, so every call runs with its own arguments.
- **Page titles sit closer to the header (CB-91).** Module pages and the Space library no longer leave a band of empty space between the breadcrumbs and the page title, or below the title: content starts about 60 px higher on a desktop and 40 px on a phone. Every page now starts at the same distance under the header (24 px on wider screens, 16 px on phones), Home and Issues included, and on module pages the content follows the title at one distance too (24 px, 20 px on phones). Titles, colors and buttons are unchanged.
- **Lighter board and Space updates (CB-71).** When something changed, the Issues board downloaded every issue with its description, and an open Space every page with its text. They now get each item's title, fields and a short excerpt; an issue's description and a page's text load when you open them. On a 3,000-issue workspace the board's update went from 683 kB to 178 kB (gzipped) and a 167-page Space's from 121 kB to 15 kB. The board's search still finds words anywhere in a description: matches in titles and excerpts show at once, the rest a moment later from the server. Service-account keys get the same lists; read one issue or page for its full text.
- **Home fits the window.** Home no longer runs off the right edge or scrolls sideways. A long chat title, such as a link, pushed the At a glance and Recent conversations column past the page; it is now cut short with an ellipsis. The page stays centered at up to 72rem, and its apps and At a glance sit side by side when there is room and stack when there isn't, with the sidebar open or collapsed (CB-77).
- **Page drafts for review no longer stay on "Preparing your draft…" (CB-81).** A draft the card could not read, such as one without a Space, with an extra field or longer than 20,000 characters, waited there for good, with Approve grayed out. A draft without a Space now goes where `create_space_page` would put it: the current page's Space, else the socio's default destination. Drafts can be as long as any page. A draft that still can't be saved shows what it has and why, with Send back, which tells the socio what to fix. With two drafts in one turn, the second says it opens once the first is decided.
- **Dispatch smoke with the fake model (CB-70).** The fake model (`tests/fixtures/serve-fake-model.ts`) answered a socio dispatch at once, so the run ended before the issue's panel could watch it and the `dispatch` smoke scenario timed out. A dispatch of the smoke's issue now gets its triage note over about 8 s, with token usage, and the smoke passes against it.
- **Seeded Media has its files (CB-69).** The bench's seeded workspace (`tests/fixtures/seed-workspace.ts`) wrote media rows without contents, so the Media page on it logged a 404 for every thumbnail. It now stores a tiny real file of each type (PNG, JPEG, PDF, Markdown, CSV) through the data space, as an upload does. Seeding takes about 15 s longer.
- **No more 429s for the passkey list (CB-68).** Every page load asked Better Auth for your passkeys, to decide whether to suggest adding one, and a few reloads within a minute hit its rate limit, with errors in the console. The sidebar now asks once, right after you sign in without a passkey (by password, a link or a provider), and keeps the answer for that tab. The account dialog still lists your passkeys when you open it.
- **Team topics.** Opening a Telegram team topic no longer shows "Conversation not found." The chat asked for the topic's computer capture, which only a chat's owner gets, and nobody owns a team topic. It now asks only in chats you own (CB-73).
- **Deleting an issue stops its agent runs.** A dispatch run that is queued or running for an issue you delete is canceled. Its status says "The issue was deleted.". Before, a running one went on to the end, and its result wasn't posted.
- **Unreadable tool calls (CB-12).** When the model sent a tool call whose arguments weren't valid JSON, the tool ran with no arguments, the model was told a field was missing and sent the same call again, until the turn ran out of rounds or hit its time limit. Common slips (text after the arguments, a missing closing brace, arguments sent as a quoted string) are now repaired. Otherwise the tool doesn't run and the model is told its arguments weren't valid JSON, so it can try again once; a second unreadable call stops the turn at once with a short message. The server log names the tool, the arguments' length and a few characters from each end, never the rest.
- **Telegram files on another Bot API (CB-8).** With `TELEGRAM_API_ROOT` set (a self-hosted Bot API server, or the tests' fake), files sent to the bot are now downloaded from that server too. The Telegram package always fetched them from api.telegram.org, so the tests' fake Bot API couldn't cover attachments. Only the bot's own file downloads are redirected, and only while the channel runs.
- **Deleting a chat with scheduled tasks.** If the server fails partway through the delete, the chat's tasks can't keep running: the tasks are hidden first, then the chat. Deleting the chat again finishes the delete.

### Upgrading

- **issues migration 6** adds the table that records each socio filing (the socio, the person, the trigger). **workspace migration 9** adds the per-socio "File to the Improvements board" setting, off for every existing socio. Both are instant.
- **Indexes for the Socios page:** approvals migration 4, data migration 7, runs migration 2, schedules migration 6 and workspace migration 12 index the audit logs, job runs, goals, turns and chats by socio. Each is a single index build: seconds on a large database.
- **workspace migration 11** adds archiving to socios and each socio's own audit (`dot_audit`): who made, changed, archived and unarchived it. It is instant; changes made before it aren't in the audit.
- **issues migration 7** rebuilds that table with the filing's cause and the socio's owner. **workspace migration 10** adds each socio's owner; every existing socio stays the company's. **schedules migration 5** adds goals. All are instant.
- **workspace migration 6** adds a "renamed" flag to conversations. It is instant.
- **pages migration 5** builds the search index from your pages at the first start: a few seconds per 2,000 pages. The database and backups grow by about 3.6 times the pages' Markdown. An older build refuses the migrated database; to go back, restore the backup taken before the migration.
- **Audit and run history are kept.** Computer activity, web reads and domain operations keep every entry instead of the newest 1,000 per socio (500 operations). Job runs are all kept too: the newest 50 of each job keep their output, and older ones show "Output not kept for older runs." with their times, status and exit code. A job's runs list has "Load more" to reach them. The database grows by about 300 bytes per web request or computer action, about 100 MB a year for a socio making 1,000 a day.
- **computers migration 2, domains migration 2, schedules migrations 3 and 4** add indexes for the audit reads, the output stamp on job runs, and job run counts kept by triggers. They are instant.
- **Replacing a file keeps the old one.** When a file is saved over (by you, a socio, a job or the API), its previous contents are kept, hidden, as an earlier version. The file keeps its place, its id and its attachments in chats. Saving the same contents again keeps nothing. Earlier versions don't count toward anyone's quota; operators see them in "Deleted, kept" under Everyone's usage. Seeing and restoring versions is for the admin view, later. A file can't be replaced while its current contents can't be read (in S3 while S3 isn't configured, or sealed under a data key the server doesn't have), since that would lose them; save it under another name instead.
- **Folder renames don't stop halfway.** Renaming or moving a folder in Media moves all its files, its empty folders and the socios' access together, or, if anything fails, nothing at all. Before, a failure could leave some files and the socios' access on the old path.
- **Everyone's usage loads faster** for operators: one pass over the files instead of one per person (about 1 s to 15 ms on a 200-person workspace). No migration for these three.
- **Page edits keep earlier versions.** When a page's title or text changes, the version it replaces is kept, one per 10 minutes of editing by each person or socio. Nobody sees them yet: not in the page, search or socio tools; operators see how many are kept on the Platform page. They are for the admin view to come. Deleting a page keeps them.
- **pages migration 6** adds the table for those versions. It is instant. The database and backups then grow by about 5 kB per kept version of a 4 kB page: about 30 kB per hour of one person's editing.
- **Remove became Deactivate.** Under People, admins now deactivate a person instead of removing their account. A deactivated person can't sign in by any way, and their sessions end at once. The service account keys they made are revoked, their mini app sessions end, their schedules pause (kept), their subscriptions are disconnected, and their Telegram link stops acting for them. Their chats, files, pages, issues and comments are kept and keep their name. **Reactivate** lets them sign in again. Both are audited. `DELETE /api/accounts/:id` deactivates too.
- **auth migration 2** adds Better Auth's deactivation columns to accounts and sessions. It is instant; nobody is signed out by it.
- **Deactivation reaches further.** A deactivated person is no longer offered as an issue assignee, and can't be given a new issue. Issues already assigned to them keep their name, and the assignee picker marks them "(Deactivated)". Their job runs in progress stop, saying "Stopped: its owner was deactivated.". A queued agent run they asked for fails with "The person who asked for this run was deactivated."; a running one finishes. The auth command gets `deactivate --email …` and `reactivate --email …`, with the same guards, follow-ups and audit as People. Its `list` already marks deactivated accounts. No migration.
- **The same chat in two tabs (CB-74).** A message you send in one tab, and the socio's reply, show in your other tab on that chat without a reload.
- **An issue deleted in another tab.** The open issue says "This issue was deleted." instead of an error.
- **The Platform page, a dataset and an approval request keep up.** A change made in another tab, or while you were away, shows without a reload. A dataset deleted elsewhere says it isn't found.
- **data migration 5 and workspace migration 7** add indexes for a file's kept versions and for your chat list (about 0.6 ms less per sidebar refresh for 500 chats). They are instant.
- **workspace migration 8** adds the archive date and who archived to conversations. It is instant, and rows that are never archived don't grow.
- **pages migration 7** compresses the kept earlier versions of pages (about 3 times smaller on disk; they read back unchanged). It rewrites each kept version once, about 0.5 ms each.
- **Dataset upserts keep the rows they replace.** When an upsert changes a row, the old values are kept in the same dataset file, with who changed it and when; inserts and unchanged rows keep nothing. Nobody sees them yet (not describe, queries, views, exports or the Data page), and they don't count toward the quota. **data migration 6** is instant; each older dataset gets its history tables on its next write. Kept rows take about 1.35 times their size: about 54 MB a year for a socio upserting 1,000 rows of 110 bytes a day.

## v0.1.0 (2026-10-07)

The first team release of Socio, forked from OpenDots and running entirely on your own infrastructure: no CopilotKit Intelligence, no vendor accounts, no telemetry.

- Everyone signs in; Spaces, pages and socios are shared
- Talk to a socio on Telegram
- Socios learn skills from finished conversations
- Backups, a health check and Portainer stacks

### New

- **Accounts.** Everyone signs in. Spaces, pages and socios are shared; admins manage Spaces, socios, permissions and settings. Conversations, page conversations, scheduled tasks, memories and calls are private. This release uses mock auth (pick a configured user); Clerk replaces it later.
- **Telegram.** Talk to a socio from a Telegram group with topics, or by direct message. Link your account from the app with a one-time link. Topics are read-only team conversations in the app; direct messages are your own.
- **Automatic Learning.** Finished conversations in a socio's learning container become evidence. Your model proposes skills (threshold, daily, or on demand). Admins review them with their evidence and a diff, then publish, revoke or pin. Socios load published skills through CopilotKit's skill-delivery tools.
- **Search providers.** Web search goes through a provider interface: Parallel (default; keyless use is free and rate-limited) or your own SearXNG. Rate limits are reported to the socio instead of failing the turn.
- **Conversations without Intelligence.** The runtime runs in SSE mode with a durable SQLite runner: history, stop, crash recovery and automatic titles.
- **Operations.** `npm run backup` (consistent copies with retention), `/healthz`, an exit test (`npm run exit-test`), and Portainer stacks for a swarm deployment.

### Changed

- Slack and CopilotKit Intelligence are removed.
- The owner token is now a shared gate in front of sign-in.
- Data from the single-owner template moves to the first admin on first start.

### Known limits

- Mock auth: anyone past the owner token can choose any account. Keep deployments on trusted networks.
- One app replica (SQLite single writer).
- Computers have open internet access; an egress proxy comes later.
- Telegram channel posts and channel direct messages are not supported.
- Voice is planned for a later release (Chatterbox TTS and speaches STT).

### Upgrading

Back up, deploy the new image, and run the exit test. See the production checklist in [SETUP.md](SETUP.md#production-checklist).
